\displaystyle\to-g[P_{\mu},V^{\mu C}_{\text{B}}t^{C}]^{aB}-gV^{\mu C}_{\text{B}}\left(t^{C}P_{\mu}\right)^{aB},

(33)

and by replacing the heavy scalar masses by the masses of the ghosts. In the next section we will extend the known UOLEA result to the BSUOLEA and include the terms necessary for this extraction to work. We will therefore not consider the ghost contributions any further.

2
Including mixed statistics and open covariant derivatives

In this section we extend the UOLEA to a form that can be used to match a renormalisable, Lorentz invariant UV theory, containing scalar fields, vector fields and spin-
1/2
fermions, to an EFT including effective operators up to mass dimension six. The vector fields are assumed to be associated with a gauge symmetry, either spontaneously broken or unbroken. We assume that the gauge group is semi-simple and include both heavy and light massive vector fields, keeping in mind the issues associated with the latter that were raised in the previous section. We start by briefly discussing the appearance of open covariant derivatives and the issues connected to mixed statistics. Then, the computation is presented. The presentation of the computation is based on Ref.
[
57
]
where fermions were included into the UOLEA using the bispinor parameterisation as a part of this thesis. In the following
X_{AB}
denotes the matrix

\displaystyle X_{AB}=-\secondfuncDer{S_{\text{UV,int.}}}{A}{B},

(34)

where
A
and
B
are any two field multiplets and
S_{\text{UV,int.}}
is the interaction part of the UV action. This matrix is always evaluated at the expanded classical field value of heavy fields, c.f. Eq. (
53
).

1
Open covariant derivatives

To simplify the computation as much as possible it is useful to determine how open covariant derivatives can enter. To this end, we consider the possible terms appearing in the Lagrangian containing at least one covariant derivative. These are restricted by the assumptions of gauge invariance, Lorentz invariance and renormalisability. Indeed, denoting a generic fermion by
\psi
, a generic scalar field by
\phi
, a generic vector field by
V_{\mu}
and the covariant derivative by
\mathcal{D}_{\mu}
we have the following allowed terms in the Lagrangian

\displaystyle\bar{\psi}\not{\mathcal{D}}\psi,

(35)

\displaystyle\cov_{\mu}\phi\cov^{\mu}\phi,

(36)

\displaystyle V^{\mu}\phi\cov_{\mu}\phi,

(37)

\displaystyle V_{\mu}V_{\nu}\cov^{\mu}V^{\nu},

(38)

\displaystyle[\cov_{\mu},\cov_{\nu}]\cov^{[\mu}V^{\nu]},

(39)

\displaystyle V^{\mu}[\cov_{\mu},\cov_{\nu}]V^{\nu},

(40)

\displaystyle\cov_{[\mu}V_{\nu]}\cov^{[\mu}V^{\nu]},

(41)

where we do not list the gauge-kinetic term for the field strength tensor associated with
\cov_{\mu}
, which was treated in Section
1
. We also use the notation
T^{[\mu\nu]}=T^{\mu\nu}-T^{\nu\mu}
for any Lorentz tensor
T^{\mu\nu}
carrying two indices.

Eq. (
35
) is simply the gauge-kinetic term of the fermions. A covariant derivative appears in the fluctuation operator only when considering two fermionic fluctuations. The resulting term will appear as the leading term in
\Delta_{\psi}
(c.f. Eq. (
52
)). Hence,
X_{\bar{\psi}\psi}
does not contain any open covariant derivatives. It also follows that open covariant derivatives only appear in the bosonic sector. Considering Eq. (
36
) there are two ways in which open covariant derivatives could appear in the fluctuation operator. The first alternative is to consider two scalar fluctuations. This gives rise to the gauge-kinetic operator of the scalar field, which does not contribute to
X_{\phi\phi}
. The second possibility is to consider one scalar fluctuation and one gauge boson fluctuation. This gives rise to a genuine open covariant derivative contribution to
X_{\phi A}
and
X_{A\phi}
with one open covariant derivative. Similarly, one open covariant derivative appears in
X_{V\phi}
and
X_{\phi V}
by considering one scalar fluctuation and one vector field fluctuation in Eq. (
37
). From this term we also obtain an open covariant derivative contribution to
X_{\phi\phi}
. Correspondingly, Eq. (
38
) gives rise to an open covariant derivative contribution to
X_{VV}
. Eq. (
39
) is not present as long as
V_{\mu}
obtains its mass from the spontaneous breaking of a semi-simple gauge group and
\cov_{\mu}
is the covariant derivative of the residual, unbroken gauge group. An explicit proof of this fact can be found in Ref.
[
52
]
. Eq. (
40
) can be non-vanishing depending on the gauge indices and yields an open covariant derivative contribution to
X_{VA}
and
X_{AV}
with one open covariant derivative. Note that it does not yield an open covariant derivative contribution to
X_{VV}
since here the covariant derivatives appear in a commutator. Finally, Eq. (
41
) gives rise to the gauge-kinetic term of the vector field fluctuations presented in Eq. (
15
) when considering two vector field fluctuations. Combining this with the
R_{\xi}
gauge-fixing term one obtains Eq. (
21
), which by choosing
\xi=1
can be reduced to

\displaystyle\Delta_{V}^{\mu\nu}=P^{2}g^{\mu\nu}+2[P^{\mu},P^{\nu}]-M^{2}_{V}g^{\mu\nu}+X_{VV}^{\mu\nu},

(42)

so that no open covariant derivatives appear in this part. One can then only produce one open covariant derivative contributing to
X_{AV}
and
X_{VA}
by considering one gauge boson fluctuation and one vector field fluctuation. In the computation presented in this thesis we will make this simplifying gauge choice. The same gauge choice will be made in Eq. (
14
) such that it becomes

\displaystyle\Delta_{A}^{\mu\nu}=P^{2}g^{\mu\nu}+2[P^{\mu},P^{\nu}],

(43)

again avoiding terms with open covariant derivatives. Making these gauge choices we conclude that open covariant derivatives only appear in the bosonic sector and that there can be at most one open covariant derivative contributing to the individual second derivatives
X_{AB}
, which appear in the fluctuation operator for any two bosonic fields
A
and
B
.

2
Mixed statistics

In the previous chapter in Eq. (
19
) it was pointed out that the result of the Gaussian path integral depends on the statistics and number of degrees of freedom associated with the field to be integrated over through the factor
c_{s}
. In the results presented in Refs.
[
53
,
56
]
this problem is solved by leaving
c_{s}
undetermined such that it can be chosen in correspondence with the fields treated in any specific application. However, one still has to raise the question of how to treat cases in which the fluctuation operator couples fluctuations of fields with different statistics and/or different numbers of degrees of freedom. The answer is that in such a case the fluctuation operator has to be diagonalised as discussed in Ref.
[
55
]
. This diagonalisation is equivalent to performing constant shifts in the Gaussian path integral to remove mixed fluctuations. This procedure, in the case of Yukawa theory, was described in Ref.
[
83
]
. As an illustration of such a shift consider the fields
\sigma
and
\rho
and the corresponding second variation

\displaystyle\delta^{2}\LagNoT_{\sigma\rho}

\displaystyle=\delta\rho\Delta_{\rho}\delta\rho+\delta\rho X_{\rho\sigma}\delta\sigma+\delta\sigma X_{\sigma\rho}\delta\rho+\delta\sigma\Delta_{\sigma}\delta\sigma

\displaystyle=(\delta\rho+\delta\sigma X_{\sigma\rho}\Delta^{-1}_{\rho})\Delta_{\rho}(\delta\rho+\Delta^{-1}_{\rho}X_{\rho\sigma}\delta\sigma)+\delta\sigma\left(\Delta_{\sigma}-X_{\sigma\rho}\Delta^{-1}_{\rho}X_{\rho\sigma}\right)\delta\sigma

\displaystyle=\delta\rho^{\prime}\Delta_{\rho}\delta\rho^{\prime}+\delta\sigma\left(\Delta_{\sigma}-X_{\sigma\rho}\Delta^{-1}_{\rho}X_{\rho\sigma}\right)\delta\sigma,

(44)

where in the last line a change of variables from
\delta\rho
to
\delta\rho^{\prime}
, with

\displaystyle\delta\rho^{\prime}=\delta\rho+\delta\sigma X_{\sigma\rho}\Delta^{-1}_{\rho}

(45)

was performed. As this is a constant shift of the integration variable
\delta\rho
it produces a trivial Jacobian. Note that in Eq. (
2
) we introduced shorthand notation for the second variation of the action

\displaystyle\delta^{2}S=\delta\rho_{x}\left(\Delta_{\rho}\right)_{xy}\delta\rho_{y}+\delta\rho_{x}\left(X_{\rho\sigma}\right)_{xy}\delta\sigma_{y}+\delta\sigma_{x}\left(X_{\sigma\rho}\right)_{xy}\delta\rho_{y}+\delta\sigma_{x}\left(\Delta_{\sigma}\right)_{xy}\delta\sigma_{y},

(46)

where

\displaystyle S=\int\rd^{d}x\,\LagNoT,

(47)

by omitting spacetime indices and writing
\LagNoT
instead of
S
. This notation is commonly used in the literature and will be employed in the following, unless the explicit appearance of spacetime indices adds clarity to the presentation.

To cover most cases of interest to particle phenomenology it suffices to treat a theory containing Majorana and Dirac fermions together with real and complex scalar fields as well as real and complex vector fields. The different values of
c_{s}
for the different kinds of fields are summarised in Table
1
.

Particle type

c_{s}

Dirac Fermion

-1

Majorana Fermion

-1/2

Complex Scalar

1

Real Scalar

1/2

Complex Vector

1

Real Vector

1/2

Table 1:
Values of
c_{s}
for the different kinds of fields considered in this work.

As can be seen from the table it is not sufficient to perform shifts to separate the bosonic sector from the fermionic one as the value of
c_{s}
also varies within each individual sector. This is due to the fact that
c_{s}
also accounts for the number of independent degrees of freedom of the field that is integrated out. Therefore, there are many shifts to be performed in order to first separate the fermionic sector from the bosonic sector and then fields with different numbers of degrees of freedom within each sector. In addition the distinction between light and heavy fields has to be kept since they play completely different roles in the computation. After the separation of the different sectors has been achieved through shifts of the form of Eq. (
2
), every sector gives rise to a separate functional determinant, which generates its own contributions to the UOLEA. In order to keep the final expressions compact and simple it is sensible to keep the number of necessary shifts at a minimum. To achieve this, and to treat real and complex scalar fields on the same footing, one could split all complex fields into a real part and an imaginary part and perform the calculation using these as the fundamental fields. However, it is often desirable to maintain the complex fields as they might have some
physical interpretation in the effective theory. We therefore use the
field and its complex conjugate as independent degrees of freedom. The inclusion of vector fields (including gauge bosons) in the bosonic sector is straightforward in Feynman gauge. Each component of the four-vector can be treated as a scalar field itself. The Lorentz indices are then handled in the same way as the internal indices.

In order to treat Dirac and Majorana fermions simultaneously without diagonalising
the fluctuation operator among these it is convenient to treat any
Dirac fermion and its charge conjugate as independent degrees of
freedom. We collect all light and heavy scalars, including the components of vector fields, into the multiplets

\phi
and
\Phi
, respectively, and all light and heavy fermions into
the multiplets
\xi
and
\Xi
, respectively, see Table
2
.

Multiplet

Components

Description

\Xi

\big(\Omega,\ccfield{\Omega},\Lambda\big)^{T}

\Omega
,
\ccfield{\Omega}
: heavy Dirac fermions

\Lambda
: heavy Majorana fermions

\Phi

\big(\Sigma,W_{\mu},\Sigma^{*},W^{*}_{\mu},\Theta,V_{\mu}\big)^{T}

\Sigma
,
\Sigma^{*}
: heavy complex scalars

\Theta
: heavy real scalars

W_{\mu}
,
W^{*}_{\mu}
: heavy complex vector fields

V_{\mu}
: heavy real vector fields

\xi

\big(\omega,\ccfield{\omega},\lambda\big)^{T}

\omega
,
\ccfield{\omega}
: light Dirac fermions

\lambda
: light Majorana fermions

\phi

\big(\sigma,w_{\mu},\sigma^{*},w^{*}_{\mu},\theta,v_{\mu}\big)^{T}

\sigma
,
\sigma^{*}
: light complex scalars

\theta
: light real scalars

w_{\mu}
,
w^{*}_{\mu}
: light complex vector fields

v_{\mu}
: light real vector fields (incl. gauge fields)

Table 2:
Contents of the different multiplets appearing in the calculation.

The charge conjugate of the Dirac spinor
\Omega
is denoted as

\ccfield{\Omega}=\cc\bar{\Omega}^{T}
, with
\cc
being the charge
conjugation matrix.
5
5

5

We choose to work in the chiral representation of the Dirac algebra.
Similarly, we define for a light Dirac spinor

\omega
,
\ccfield{\omega}=\cc\bar{\omega}^{T}
. As will be shown below, with this setup it will be sufficient to perform two shifts of the form of Eq. (
2
). Since collecting the fields into these multiplets is somewhat unconventional and since the starting point of the computation is the second variation of the action, we here elaborate on how this second variation is obtained in the parameterisation that was chosen here. These details were not discussed in Ref.
[
57
]
. Furthermore, vector fields in the bosonic sector were not included and the discussion that follows allows for a demonstration of this inclusion. We start with the bosonic sector where, with the heavy field content given in Table
2
, we have the following second variation for heavy bosons only

\displaystyle\delta^{2}\Lag{B,H}

\displaystyle=-\delta\Sigma^{\dagger}\Delta_{\Sigma}\delta\Sigma-\frac{1}{2}\delta\Theta^{T}\Delta_{\Theta}\delta\Theta-\delta W^{\dagger}_{\mu}\Delta^{\mu\nu}_{W}\delta W_{\nu}-\frac{1}{2}\delta V^{T}_{\mu}\Delta^{\mu\nu}_{V}\delta V_{\nu}

\displaystyle\quad+\delta^{2}\Lag{B,H,int.},

(48)

where

\displaystyle\Delta_{\Sigma}

\displaystyle=-P_{\Sigma}^{2}+M_{\Sigma}^{2}+X_{\Sigma^{*}\Sigma},

(49)

\displaystyle\Delta_{\Theta}

\displaystyle=-P_{\Theta}^{2}+M_{\Theta}^{2}+X_{\Theta\Theta},

(50)

\displaystyle\Delta_{W}^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=P_{W}^{2}g^{\mu\nu}+2[P_{W}^{\mu},P_{W}^{\nu}]-M^{2}_{W}g^{\mu\nu}+X_{W^{*}W}^{\mu\nu},

(51)

\displaystyle\Delta_{V}^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=P_{V}^{2}g^{\mu\nu}+2[P_{V}^{\mu},P_{V}^{\nu}]-M^{2}_{V}g^{\mu\nu}+X_{VV}^{\mu\nu}.

(52)

Here a subscript on
P^{\mu}=iD^{\mu}
indicates the representation on which the covariant derivative acts. For example,
P^{\mu}_{\Sigma}
contains the covariant derivative acting on the (possibly reducible) representation of all heavy complex scalar fields. Furthermore,
\delta^{2}\Lag{B,H,int.}
contains the remaining interactions and can be written as

\displaystyle\delta^{2}\Lag{B,H,int.}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\begin{pmatrix}\delta\Sigma^{T}&\delta W_{\mu}&\delta\Sigma^{\dagger}&\delta W^{\dagger}_{\mu}&\delta\Theta^{T}&\delta V^{T}_{\mu}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle\quad\begin{pmatrix}X_{\Sigma\Sigma}&X_{\Sigma W}^{\nu}&0&X_{\Sigma W^{*}}^{\nu}&X_{\Sigma\Theta}&X_{\Sigma V}^{\nu}\\
X_{W\Sigma}^{\mu}&X_{WW}^{\mu\nu}&X_{W\Sigma^{*}}^{\mu}&0&X_{W\Theta}^{\mu}&X_{WV}^{\mu\nu}\\
0&X_{\Sigma^{*}W}^{\nu}&X_{\Sigma^{*}\Sigma^{*}}&X_{\Sigma^{*}W^{*}}^{\nu}&X_{\Sigma^{*}\Theta}&X_{\Sigma*V}^{\nu}\\
X_{W^{*}\Sigma}^{\mu}&0&X_{W^{*}\Sigma^{*}}^{\mu}&X_{W^{*}W^{*}}^{\mu\nu}&X_{W^{*}\Theta}^{\mu}&X_{W^{*}V}^{\mu\nu}\\
X_{\Theta\Sigma}&X_{\Theta W}^{\nu}&X_{\Theta\Sigma^{*}}&X_{\Theta W^{*}}^{\nu}&0&X_{\Theta V}^{\nu}\\
X_{V\Sigma}^{\mu}&X_{VW}^{\mu\nu}&X_{V\Sigma^{*}}^{\mu}&X_{VW^{*}}^{\mu\nu}&X_{V\Theta}^{\mu}&0\\
\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\delta\Sigma\\
\delta W_{\nu}\\
\delta\Sigma^{*}\\
\delta W^{*}_{\nu}\\
\delta\Theta\\
\delta V_{\nu}\end{pmatrix},

(53)

which can be directly expressed through the multiplet
\Phi
. In order to rewrite the first line of Eq. (
48
) in terms of said multiplet we note that the kinetic contributions of complex fields can be symmetrised through integration by parts. In particular it holds that

\displaystyle\delta\Sigma^{\dagger}\Delta_{\Sigma}\delta\Sigma

\displaystyle=\delta\Sigma^{\dagger}\left(-P_{\Sigma}^{2}+M_{\Sigma}^{2}+X_{\Sigma^{*}\Sigma}\right)\delta\Sigma

\displaystyle=\delta\Sigma^{T}\left(-P^{2}_{\Sigma^{*}}+M_{\Sigma^{*}}^{2}+X_{\Sigma\Sigma^{*}}\right)\delta\Sigma^{*}

\displaystyle=\delta\Sigma^{T}\Delta_{\Sigma^{*}}\delta\Sigma^{*},

(54)

where the covariant derivative now acts on the conjugate representation and we used that
X_{\Sigma\Sigma^{*}}=X^{T}_{\Sigma^{*}\Sigma}
. Similarly, we have that

\displaystyle\delta W^{\dagger}_{\mu}\Delta_{W}^{\mu\nu}\delta W_{\nu}=\delta W^{T}_{\mu}\Delta_{W^{*}}^{\mu\nu}\delta W^{*}_{\nu},

(55)

with

\displaystyle\Delta_{W^{*}}^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=P_{W^{*}}^{2}g^{\mu\nu}+2[P_{W^{*}}^{\mu},P_{W^{*}}^{\nu}]-M^{2}_{W}g^{\mu\nu}+X_{WW^{*}}^{\mu\nu}.

(56)

This allows for Eq. (
48
) to be written as

\displaystyle\delta^{2}\Lag{B,H}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\delta\Phi^{T}\mathbf{\Delta}_{\Phi}\delta\Phi,

(57)

with

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Delta}_{\Phi}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}X_{\Sigma\Sigma}&X_{\Sigma W}^{\nu}&\Delta_{\Sigma^{*}}&X_{\Sigma W^{*}}^{\nu}&X_{\Sigma\Theta}&X_{\Sigma V}^{\nu}\\
X_{W\Sigma}^{\mu}&X_{WW}^{\mu\nu}&X_{W\Sigma^{*}}^{\mu}&\Delta_{W^{*}}^{\mu\nu}&X_{W\Theta}^{\mu}&X_{WV}^{\mu\nu}\\
\Delta_{\Sigma}&X_{\Sigma^{*}W}^{\nu}&X_{\Sigma^{*}\Sigma^{*}}&X_{\Sigma^{*}W^{*}}^{\nu}&X_{\Sigma^{*}\Theta}&X_{\Sigma*V}^{\nu}\\
X_{W^{*}\Sigma}^{\mu}&\Delta_{W}&X_{W^{*}\Sigma^{*}}^{\mu}&X_{W^{*}W^{*}}^{\mu\nu}&X_{W^{*}\Theta}^{\mu}&X_{W^{*}V}^{\mu\nu}\\
X_{\Theta\Sigma}&X_{\Theta W}^{\nu}&X_{\Theta\Sigma^{*}}&X_{\Theta W^{*}}^{\nu}&\Delta_{\Theta}&X_{\Theta V}^{\nu}\\
X_{V\Sigma}^{\mu}&X_{VW}^{\mu\nu}&X_{V\Sigma^{*}}^{\mu}&X_{VW^{*}}^{\mu\nu}&X_{V\Theta}^{\mu}&\Delta_{V}^{\mu\nu}\\
\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{B}\left(-P^{2}_{\Phi}+M^{2}_{\Phi}\right)+\mathbf{\tilde{X}}_{\Phi\Phi},

(58)

where we define

\displaystyle P^{2}_{\Phi}-M^{2}_{\Phi}

\displaystyle\equiv\diag\left(P_{\Sigma}^{2}-M_{\Sigma}^{2},P_{W}^{2}-M_{W}^{2},P_{\Sigma^{*}}^{2}-M_{\Sigma}^{2},P_{W^{*}}^{2}-M_{W}^{2},P_{\Theta}^{2}-M_{\Theta}^{2},P_{V}^{2}-M_{V}^{2}\right),

(59)

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{B}

\displaystyle\equiv\begin{pmatrix}0&0&\mathds{1}&0&0&0\\
0&0&0&-\mathds{1}&0&0\\
\mathds{1}&0&0&0&0&0\\
0&-\mathds{1}&0&0&0&0\\
0&0&0&0&\mathds{1}&0\\
0&0&0&0&0&-\mathds{1}\\
\end{pmatrix},

(60)

as well as

\displaystyle\mathbf{\tilde{X}}_{\Phi\Phi}

\displaystyle\equiv\begin{pmatrix}X_{\Sigma\Sigma}&X_{\Sigma W}^{\nu}&X_{\Sigma\Sigma^{*}}&X_{\Sigma W^{*}}^{\nu}&X_{\Sigma\Theta}&X_{\Sigma V}^{\nu}\\
X_{W\Sigma}^{\mu}&X_{WW}^{\mu\nu}&X_{W\Sigma^{*}}^{\mu}&X_{WW^{*}}^{\mu\nu}&X_{W\Theta}^{\mu}&X_{WV}^{\mu\nu}\\
X_{\Sigma^{*}\Sigma}&X_{\Sigma^{*}W}^{\nu}&X_{\Sigma^{*}\Sigma^{*}}&X_{\Sigma^{*}W^{*}}^{\nu}&X_{\Sigma^{*}\Theta}&X_{\Sigma*V}^{\nu}\\
X_{W^{*}\Sigma}^{\mu}&X_{W^{*}W}&X_{W^{*}\Sigma^{*}}^{\mu}&X_{W^{*}W^{*}}^{\mu\nu}&X_{W^{*}\Theta}^{\mu}&X_{W^{*}V}^{\mu\nu}\\
X_{\Theta\Sigma}&X_{\Theta W}^{\nu}&X_{\Theta\Sigma^{*}}&X_{\Theta W^{*}}^{\nu}&X_{\Theta\Theta}&X_{\Theta V}^{\nu}\\
X_{V\Sigma}^{\mu}&X_{VW}^{\mu\nu}&X_{V\Sigma^{*}}^{\mu}&X_{VW^{*}}^{\mu\nu}&X_{V\Theta}^{\mu}&X_{VV}^{\mu\nu}\\
\end{pmatrix}.

(61)

We here absorb the explicit commutators of covariant derivatives appearing in
\Delta_{W}^{\mu\nu}
,
\Delta_{W^{*}}^{\mu\nu}
and
\Delta_{V}^{\mu\nu}
in the corresponding self-interaction matrices
X_{WW^{*}}^{\mu\nu}
,
X_{W^{*}W}^{\mu\nu}
and
X_{VV}^{\mu\nu}
. Furthermore, in Eq. (
60
) the identity
\mathds{1}
denotes an identity with respect to all indices of the respective multiplets. In the case of vector fields this includes the metric
g^{\mu\nu}
. It is clear that the purely light bosonic fluctuations can be written in exactly the same way by simply replacing upper case letters by lower case ones. Hence, this quadratic fluctuation is given by

\displaystyle\delta^{2}\Lag{B,L}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\delta\phi^{T}\mathbf{\Delta}_{\phi}\delta\phi,

(62)

where

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Delta}_{\phi}=\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{B}\left(-P^{2}_{\phi}+M^{2}_{\phi}\right)+\mathbf{\tilde{X}}_{\phi\phi},

(63)

with

\displaystyle-P^{2}_{\phi}+M^{2}_{\phi}

\displaystyle=-\text{diag}\left(P_{\sigma}^{2}-M_{\sigma}^{2},P_{w}^{2}-M_{w}^{2},P_{\sigma^{*}}^{2}-M_{\sigma}^{2},P_{w^{*}}^{2}-M_{w}^{2},P_{\theta}^{2}-M_{\theta}^{2},P_{v}^{2}-M_{v}^{2}\right),

(64)

and

\displaystyle\mathbf{\tilde{X}}_{\phi\phi}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}X_{\sigma\sigma}&X_{\sigma w}^{\nu}&X_{\sigma\sigma^{*}}&X_{\sigma w^{*}}^{\nu}&X_{\sigma\theta}&X_{\sigma v}^{\nu}\\
X_{w\sigma}^{\mu}&X_{ww}^{\mu\nu}&X_{w\sigma^{*}}^{\mu}&X_{ww^{*}}^{\mu\nu}&X_{w\theta}^{\mu}&X_{wv}^{\mu\nu}\\
X_{\sigma^{*}\sigma}&X_{\sigma^{*}w}^{\nu}&X_{\sigma^{*}\sigma^{*}}&X_{\sigma^{*}w^{*}}^{\nu}&X_{\sigma^{*}\theta}&X_{\sigma*v}^{\nu}\\
X_{w^{*}\sigma}^{\mu}&X_{w^{*}w}&X_{w^{*}\sigma^{*}}^{\mu}&X_{w^{*}w^{*}}^{\mu\nu}&X_{w^{*}\theta}^{\mu}&X_{w^{*}v}^{\mu\nu}\\
X_{\theta\sigma}&X_{\theta w}^{\nu}&X_{\theta\sigma^{*}}&X_{\theta w^{*}}^{\nu}&X_{\theta\theta}&X_{\theta v}^{\nu}\\
X_{v\sigma}^{\mu}&X_{vw}^{\mu\nu}&X_{v\sigma^{*}}^{\mu}&X_{vw^{*}}^{\mu\nu}&X_{v\theta}^{\mu}&X_{vv}^{\mu\nu}\\
\end{pmatrix}.

(65)

We again absorb explicit commutators of
P_{\mu}
in the self-interaction matrices of the vector bosons. Besides the purely heavy and purely light bosonic variations there are also mixed bosonic variations. These do not contain any kinetic terms and originate entirely from the interaction Lagrangian like the terms in Eq. (
53
). They can be directly expressed in terms of
\delta\phi
and
\delta\Phi
as

\displaystyle\delta^{2}\Lag{B,HL}=-\frac{1}{2}\delta\Phi^{T}\mathbf{\tilde{X}}_{\Phi\phi}\delta\phi-\frac{1}{2}\delta\phi^{T}\mathbf{\tilde{X}}_{\phi\Phi}\delta\Phi,

(66)

where

\displaystyle\mathbf{\tilde{X}}_{\Phi\phi}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}X_{\Sigma\sigma}&X_{\Sigma w}^{\nu}&X_{\Sigma\sigma^{*}}&X_{\Sigma w^{*}}^{\nu}&X_{\Sigma\theta}&X_{\Sigma v}^{\nu}\\
X_{W\sigma}^{\mu}&X_{Ww}^{\mu\nu}&X_{W\sigma^{*}}^{\mu}&X_{Ww^{*}}&X_{W\theta}^{\mu}&X_{Wv}^{\mu\nu}\\
X_{\Sigma^{*}\sigma}&X_{\Sigma^{*}w}^{\nu}&X_{\Sigma^{*}\sigma^{*}}&X_{\Sigma^{*}w^{*}}^{\nu}&X_{\Sigma^{*}\theta}&X_{\Sigma^{*}v}^{\nu}\\
X_{W^{*}\sigma}^{\mu}&X_{W^{*}w}&X_{W^{*}\sigma^{*}}^{\mu}&X_{W^{*}w^{*}}^{\mu\nu}&X_{W^{*}\theta}^{\mu}&X_{W^{*}v}^{\mu\nu}\\
X_{\Theta\sigma}&X_{\Theta w}^{\nu}&X_{\Theta\sigma^{*}}&X_{\Theta w^{*}}^{\nu}&X_{\Theta\theta}&X_{\Theta v}^{\nu}\\
X_{V\sigma}^{\mu}&X_{Vw}^{\mu\nu}&X_{V\sigma^{*}}^{\mu}&X_{Vw^{*}}^{\mu\nu}&X_{V\theta}^{\mu}&X_{Vv}\\
\end{pmatrix},

(67)

and

\displaystyle\mathbf{\tilde{X}}_{\phi\Phi}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}X_{\sigma\Sigma}&X_{\sigma W}^{\nu}&X_{\sigma\Sigma^{*}}&X_{\sigma W^{*}}^{\nu}&X_{\sigma\Theta}&X_{\sigma V}^{\nu}\\
X_{w\Sigma}^{\mu}&X_{wW}^{\mu\nu}&X_{w\Sigma^{*}}^{\mu}&X_{wW^{*}}&X_{w\Theta}^{\mu}&X_{wV}^{\mu\nu}\\
X_{\sigma^{*}\Sigma}&X_{\sigma^{*}W}^{\nu}&X_{\sigma^{*}\Sigma^{*}}&X_{\sigma^{*}W^{*}}^{\nu}&X_{\sigma^{*}\Theta}&X_{\sigma^{*}V}^{\nu}\\
X_{w^{*}\Sigma}^{\mu}&X_{w^{*}W}&X_{w^{*}\Sigma^{*}}^{\mu}&X_{w^{*}W^{*}}^{\mu\nu}&X_{w^{*}\Theta}^{\mu}&X_{w^{*}V}^{\mu\nu}\\
X_{\theta\Sigma}&X_{\theta W}^{\nu}&X_{\theta\Sigma^{*}}&X_{\theta W^{*}}^{\nu}&X_{\theta\Theta}&X_{\theta V}^{\nu}\\
X_{v\Sigma}^{\mu}&X_{vW}^{\mu\nu}&X_{v\Sigma^{*}}^{\mu}&X_{vW^{*}}^{\mu\nu}&X_{v\Theta}^{\mu}&X_{vV}\\
\end{pmatrix}.

(68)

The purely bosonic part of the second variation is then

\displaystyle\delta^{2}\Lag{B}=\delta^{2}\Lag{B,H}+\delta^{2}\Lag{B,L}+\delta^{2}\Lag{B,HL}.

(69)

The matrices
\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{AB}
defined here differ from those defined in Ref.
[
57
]
by the inclusion of the vector fields. As in Ref.
[
57
]
these are not the matrices that will appear in the final result since the effects of the presence of
\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{B}
, which does not equal the identity matrix, will be absorbed in the redefinition of these matrices. Note that the appearance of the matrix
\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{B}
is due to the fact that the propagators of the components of the multiplets
\Phi
and
\phi
differ in signs between the scalar fields and the vector fields and that for complex fields, the propagators connect the field and its complex conjugate, rather than connecting the field to itself.

We next turn to the purely fermionic sector, where various signs, stemming from the Grassmann odd
6
6

6

For a brief discussion of Grassmann numbers and the Berezin algebra see Appendix
10
.
nature of the fermionic fields, and various charge conjugation matrices, stemming from the parameterisation chosen to perform the computation, appear in intermediate steps of the calculation. In what follows, we discuss how these signs and matrices enter the computation. We start with purely heavy fermions, including both Dirac fermions
\Omega
and Majorana fermions
\Lambda
. It seems most natural to parameterise the second variation in terms of
\delta\Omega
, the Dirac adjoints
\delta\bar{\Omega}
and the Majorana fermions
\delta\Lambda
. If this parameterisation is chosen, one has to diagonalise terms in the fluctuation operator which couple Dirac and Majorana fermions. Such terms are present for instance in supersymmetric models in the form of fermion-sfermion-gaugino couplings. To avoid this diagonalisation we instead introduce the 8-component spinor

\displaystyle\Psi=\begin{pmatrix}\Omega\\
~~\ccfield{\Omega}\end{pmatrix},

(70)

also introduced in Ref.
[
84
]
as one of three equivalent ways of treating Dirac fermions in the path integral approach. The spinor defined in Eq. (
70
) is referred to as a bispinor and lends its name to the BSUOLEA. For the intents and purposes of the Gaussian path integral this bispinor behaves like a Majorana fermion and eliminates the need for the extra diagonalisation within the fermionic sector. It is then necessary to express the second variation in terms of this bispinor and ultimately in terms of the multiplet
\Xi=\begin{pmatrix}\Psi&\Lambda\end{pmatrix}^{T}
. Consider first the gauge-kinetic terms, mass terms and self-interactions of the Dirac Fermions. These can be written as

\displaystyle\delta\bar{\Omega}\Delta_{\Omega}\delta\Omega

\displaystyle=\delta\bar{\Omega}\left(\not{P}_{\Omega}-M_{\Omega}+X_{\bar{\Omega}\Omega}\right)\delta\Omega

\displaystyle=\delta\bar{\Omega}\left(-\overleftarrow{\not{P}}_{\bar{\Omega}}-M_{\Omega}+X_{\bar{\Omega}\Omega}\right)\delta\Omega

\displaystyle=\delta\Omega^{T}\cc\cc^{-1}\left(\not{P}_{\bar{\Omega}}^{T}+M_{\Omega}-X_{\bar{\Omega}\Omega}^{T}\right)\cc^{-1}\cc\delta\bar{\Omega}^{T}

\displaystyle=\delta\Omega^{T}\cc\left(\not{P}_{\ccfield{\Omega}}-M_{\Omega}+\cc^{-1}X_{\Omega\bar{\Omega}}\cc^{-1}\right)\delta\ccfield{\Omega},

(71)

where from the first to the second line we integrated by parts and from the third to the last line we used that
\cc^{-1}=-\cc
,
\delta\ccfield{\Omega}=C\delta\bar{\Omega}^{T}
and
X_{\bar{\Omega}\Omega}^{T}=-X_{\Omega\bar{\Omega}}
, where the sign comes from the fact that derivatives with respect to fermionic fields anticommute. It then follows that

\displaystyle\delta\bar{\Omega}\Delta_{\Omega}\delta\Omega={}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left(\delta\ccfield{\Omega}\right)^{T}\cc\left(\not{P}_{\Omega}-M_{\Omega}+X_{\bar{\Omega}\Omega}\right)\delta\Omega

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\delta\Omega^{T}\cc\left(\not{P}_{\ccfield{\Omega}}-M_{\Omega}+\cc^{-1}X_{\Omega\bar{\Omega}}\cc^{-1}\right)\delta\ccfield{\Omega}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\delta\Psi^{T}\begin{pmatrix}0&\cc\left(\not{P}_{\ccfield{\Omega}}-M_{\Omega}+\cc^{-1}X_{\Omega\bar{\Omega}}\cc^{-1}\right)\\
\cc\left(\not{P}_{\Omega}-M_{\Omega}+X_{\bar{\Omega}\Omega}\right)&0\end{pmatrix}\delta\Psi,

(72)

which we combine with the term quadratic in Majorana fermion fluctuations

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\delta\Lambda^{T}\Delta_{\Lambda}\delta\Lambda=\frac{1}{2}\delta\Lambda^{T}\cc\left(\not{P}_{\Lambda}-M_{\Lambda}+X_{\Lambda\Lambda}\right)\delta\Lambda,

(73)

and the pure interaction terms
7
7

7

The requirements of Lorentz invariance and renormalisability imply that there are no
X_{\bar{\Omega}\bar{\Omega}}
and
X_{\Omega\Omega}
contributions as long as there is some global symmetry group (a subgroup of which could be gauged) under which the two components of the Dirac spinors transform in conjugate representations. This requirement can be used to define what is meant by a Dirac fermion as opposed to a Majorana fermion, which would transform in a real representation of the same symmetry group, see Ref.
[
85
]
. In any case, extending the computation to include these contributions is straightforward.

\displaystyle\delta^{2}\Lag{F,H,int.}

\displaystyle=\delta\bar{\Omega}X_{\bar{\Omega}\Lambda}\delta\Lambda+\delta\Lambda^{T}X_{\Lambda\Omega}\delta\Omega

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left(\delta\ccfield{\Omega}\right)^{T}\cc X_{\bar{\Omega}\Lambda}\delta\Lambda+\frac{1}{2}\delta\Lambda^{T}X_{\Lambda\bar{\Omega}}\cc^{-1}\delta\ccfield{\Omega}+\frac{1}{2}\delta\Lambda^{T}X_{\Lambda\Omega}\delta\Omega+\frac{1}{2}\delta\Omega^{T}X_{\Omega\Lambda}\delta\Lambda

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\delta\Lambda^{T}\begin{pmatrix}X_{\Lambda\Omega}&X_{\Lambda\bar{\Omega}}\cc^{-1}\end{pmatrix}\delta\Psi+\frac{1}{2}\delta\Psi^{T}\begin{pmatrix}X_{\Omega\Lambda}\\
\cc X_{\bar{\Omega}\Lambda}\end{pmatrix}\delta\Lambda,

(74)

to obtain the full contribution to the second variation coming only from heavy fermions

\displaystyle\delta^{2}\Lag{F,H}=\frac{1}{2}\delta\Xi^{T}\mathbf{\Delta}_{\Xi}\delta\Xi,

(75)

where

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Delta}_{\Xi}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}0&\cc(\not{P}_{\ccfield{\Omega}}-M_{\Omega}+\cc^{-1}X_{\Omega\bar{\Omega}}\cc^{-1})&X_{\Omega\Lambda}\\
\cc(\not{P}_{\Omega}-M_{\Omega}+X_{\bar{\Omega}\Omega})&0&\cc X_{\bar{\Omega}\Lambda}\\
X_{\Lambda\Omega}&X_{\Lambda\bar{\Omega}}\cc^{-1}&\cc(\not{P}_{\Lambda}-M_{\Lambda}+\cc^{-1}X_{\Lambda\Lambda})\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=\cc\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{F}(\not{P}-M_{\Xi})+\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\Xi\Xi},

(76)

with

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{F}

\displaystyle\equiv\begin{pmatrix}0&&\mathds{1}&&0\\
\mathds{1}&&0&&0\\
0&&0&&\mathds{1}\end{pmatrix},\;\not{P}-M_{\Xi}=\text{diag}\left(\not{P}_{\Omega}-M_{\Omega},\,\not{P}_{\ccfield{\Omega}}-M_{\Omega},\,\not{P}_{\Lambda}-M_{\Lambda}\right),

(77)

and

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\Xi\Xi}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}0&&X_{\Omega\bar{\Omega}}\cc^{-1}&&X_{\Omega\Lambda}\\
\cc X_{\bar{\Omega}\Omega}&&0&&\cc X_{\bar{\Omega}\Lambda}\\
X_{\Lambda\Omega}&&X_{\Lambda\bar{\Omega}}\cc^{-1}&&X_{\Lambda\Lambda}\end{pmatrix}.

(78)

By replacing the upper case letters with the corresponding lower case ones, one analogously obtains the second variation for the purely light fermions. We present this result for the sake of completeness

\displaystyle\delta^{2}\Lag{F,L}=\frac{1}{2}\delta\xi^{T}\mathbf{\Delta}_{\xi}\delta\xi,

(79)

where

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Delta}_{\xi}

\displaystyle=\cc\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{F}(\not{P}-M_{\xi})+\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\xi\xi},

(80)

\displaystyle\not{P}-M_{\xi}

\displaystyle=\text{diag}(\not{P}_{\omega}-M_{\omega},\,\not{P}_{\ccfield{\omega}}-M_{\omega},\,\not{P}_{\lambda}-M_{\lambda}),

(81)

and

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\xi\xi}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}0&&X_{\omega\bar{\omega}}\cc^{-1}&&X_{\omega\lambda}\\
\cc X_{\bar{\omega}\omega}&&0&&\cc X_{\bar{\omega}\lambda}\\
X_{\lambda\omega}&&X_{\lambda\bar{\omega}}\cc^{-1}&&X_{\lambda\lambda}\end{pmatrix}.

(82)

As in the bosonic sector there are also couplings that couple heavy fermions to light fermions. These can be treated in complete analogy to the heavy-heavy couplings that have already been discussed and lead to the following contribution to the second variation

\displaystyle\delta^{2}\Lag{F,HL}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\delta\xi^{T}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\xi\Xi}\delta\Xi+\frac{1}{2}\delta\Xi^{T}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\Xi\xi}\delta\xi,

(83)

where the matrices
\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\Xi\xi}
and
\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\xi\Xi}
are given by

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\Xi\xi}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}X_{\Omega\omega}&&X_{\Omega\bar{\omega}}\cc^{-1}&&X_{\Omega\lambda}\\
\cc X_{\bar{\Omega}\omega}&&\cc X_{\bar{\Omega}\bar{\omega}}\cc^{-1}&&\cc X_{\bar{\Omega}\lambda}\\
X_{\Lambda\omega}&&X_{\Lambda\bar{\omega}}\cc^{-1}&&X_{\Lambda\lambda}\end{pmatrix},

(84)

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\xi\Xi}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}X_{\omega\Omega}&&X_{\omega\bar{\Omega}}\cc^{-1}&&X_{\omega\Lambda}\\
\cc X_{\bar{\omega}\Omega}&&\cc X_{\bar{\omega}\bar{\Omega}}\cc^{-1}&&\cc X_{\bar{\omega}\Lambda}\\
X_{\lambda\Omega}&&X_{\lambda\bar{\Omega}}\cc^{-1}&&X_{\lambda\Lambda}\end{pmatrix}.

(85)

Adding all of these contributions we obtain the purely fermionic part of the second variation, which reads

\displaystyle\delta^{2}\Lag{F}

\displaystyle=\delta^{2}\Lag{F,H}+\delta^{2}\Lag{F,L}+\delta^{2}\Lag{F,HL}.

(86)

Finally, there are couplings between the fermions and the bosons leading to contributions of mixed statistics to the second variation. In the case of only heavy fields we have

\displaystyle\delta^{2}\Lag{BF,H}={}

\displaystyle-\delta\bar{\Omega}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\bar{\Omega}\Phi}\delta\Phi+\delta\Phi^{T}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\Phi\Omega}\delta\Omega-\frac{1}{2}\delta\Lambda^{T}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\Lambda\Phi}\delta\Phi+\frac{1}{2}\delta\Phi^{T}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\Phi\Lambda}\delta\Lambda

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\left(\delta\ccfield{\Omega}\right)^{T}\cc\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\bar{\Omega}\Phi}\delta\Phi+\frac{1}{2}\delta\Phi^{T}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\Phi\bar{\Omega}}\cc^{-1}\delta\ccfield{\Omega}+\frac{1}{2}\delta\Phi^{T}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\Phi\Omega}\delta\Omega-\frac{1}{2}\delta\Omega^{T}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\Omega\Phi}\delta\Phi

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\delta\Lambda^{T}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\Lambda\Phi}\delta\Phi+\frac{1}{2}\delta\Phi^{T}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\Phi\Lambda}\delta\Lambda

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\delta\Xi^{T}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\Xi\Phi}\delta\Phi+\frac{1}{2}\delta\Phi^{T}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\Phi\Xi}\delta\Xi,

(87)

where the coupling matrices are given by

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\Xi\Phi}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}X_{\Omega\Sigma}&X_{\Omega W}^{\nu}&X_{\Omega\Sigma^{*}}&X_{\Omega W^{*}}^{\nu}&X_{\Omega\Theta}&X_{\Omega V}^{\nu}\\
\cc X_{\bar{\Omega}\Sigma}&\cc X_{\bar{\Omega}W}^{\nu}&\cc X_{\bar{\Omega}\Sigma^{*}}&\cc X_{\bar{\Omega}W^{*}}^{\nu}&\cc X_{\bar{\Omega}\Theta}&\cc X_{\bar{\Omega}V}^{\nu}\\
X_{\Lambda\Sigma}&X_{\Lambda W}^{\nu}&X_{\Lambda\Sigma^{*}}&X_{\Lambda W^{*}}^{\nu}&X_{\Lambda\Theta}&X_{\Lambda V}^{\nu}\end{pmatrix},

(88)

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\Phi\Xi}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}X_{\Sigma\Omega}&X_{\Sigma\bar{\Omega}}\cc^{-1}&X_{\Sigma\Lambda}\\
X_{W\Omega}^{\nu}&X_{W\bar{\Omega}}^{\nu}\cc^{-1}&X_{W\Lambda}^{\nu}\\
X_{\Sigma^{*}\Omega}&X_{\Sigma^{*}\bar{\Omega}}\cc^{-1}&X_{\Sigma^{*}\Lambda}\\
X_{W^{*}\Omega}^{\nu}&X_{W^{*}\bar{\Omega}}^{\nu}\cc^{-1}&X_{W^{*}\Lambda}^{\nu}\\
X_{\Theta\Omega}&X_{\Theta\bar{\Omega}}\cc^{-1}&X_{\Theta\Lambda}\\
X_{V\Omega}^{\nu}&X_{V\bar{\Omega}}^{\nu}\cc^{-1}&X_{V\Lambda}^{\nu}\\
\end{pmatrix}.

(89)

In going from the first to the second line of Eq. (
87
) we used that
\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\bar{\Omega}\Phi}^{T}=\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\Phi\bar{\Omega}}
and
\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\Phi\Omega}^{T}=\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\Omega\Phi}
as well as the fact that these matrices are Grassmann odd. The remaining boson-fermion couplings can be treated in a similar fashion and the complete mixed contribution to the second variation reads

\displaystyle\delta^{2}\Lag{BF}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\delta\Xi^{T}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\Xi\Phi}\delta\Phi+\frac{1}{2}\delta\Phi^{T}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\Phi\Xi}\delta\Xi-\frac{1}{2}\delta\Xi^{T}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\Xi\phi}\delta\phi+\frac{1}{2}\delta\phi^{T}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\phi\Xi}\delta\Xi

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{2}\delta\xi^{T}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\xi\Phi}\delta\Phi+\frac{1}{2}\delta\Phi^{T}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\Phi\xi}\delta\xi-\frac{1}{2}\delta\xi^{T}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\xi\phi}\delta\phi+\frac{1}{2}\delta\phi^{T}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\phi\xi}\delta\xi,

(90)

where we introduced the mixed bosonic-fermionic, heavy-light coupling matrices

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\Xi\phi}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}X_{\Omega\sigma}&X_{\Omega w}^{\nu}&X_{\Omega\sigma^{*}}&X_{\Omega w^{*}}^{\nu}&X_{\Omega\theta}&X_{\Omega v}^{\nu}\\
\cc X_{\bar{\Omega}\sigma}&\cc X_{\bar{\Omega}w}^{\nu}&\cc X_{\bar{\Omega}\sigma^{*}}&\cc X_{\bar{\Omega}w^{*}}^{\nu}&\cc X_{\bar{\Omega}\theta}&\cc X_{\bar{\Omega}v}^{\nu}\\
X_{\Lambda\sigma}&X_{\Lambda w}^{\nu}&X_{\Lambda\sigma^{*}}&X_{\Lambda w^{*}}^{\nu}&X_{\Lambda\theta}&X_{\Lambda v}^{\nu}\end{pmatrix},

(91)

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\phi\Xi}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}X_{\sigma\Omega}&X_{\sigma\bar{\Omega}}\cc^{-1}&X_{\sigma\Lambda}\\
X_{w\Omega}^{\nu}&X_{w\bar{\Omega}}^{\nu}\cc^{-1}&X_{w\Lambda}^{\nu}\\
X_{\sigma^{*}\Omega}&X_{\sigma^{*}\bar{\Omega}}\cc^{-1}&X_{\sigma^{*}\Lambda}\\
X_{w^{*}\Omega}^{\nu}&X_{w^{*}\bar{\Omega}}^{\nu}\cc^{-1}&X_{w^{*}\Lambda}^{\nu}\\
X_{\theta\Omega}&X_{\theta\bar{\Omega}}\cc^{-1}&X_{\theta\Lambda}\\
X_{v\Omega}^{\nu}&X_{v\bar{\Omega}}^{\nu}\cc^{-1}&X_{v\Lambda}^{\nu}\\
\end{pmatrix},

(92)

with the definitions of
\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\Phi\xi}
and
\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\xi\Phi}
obtained by the replacements
\phi\rightarrow\Phi
and
\Xi\rightarrow\xi
and the definitions of
\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\phi\xi}
and
\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\xi\phi}
obtained by the replacement
\Xi\rightarrow\xi
. The full second variation is then given by

\displaystyle\delta^{2}\Lag{UV}

\displaystyle=\delta^{2}\Lag{B}+\delta^{2}\Lag{F}+\delta^{2}\Lag{BF}.

(93)

Having derived this parameterisation of the second variation, the calculation now proceeds by diagonalising the corresponding fluctuation operator in terms of statistics in order to be able to perform the Gaussian
path integral. As discussed before, the advantage of this parameterisation is that this is the only diagonalisation that has to be performed. We first eliminate terms that mix bosonic fluctuations
and fluctuations of light fermions
\xi
by rewriting the part of the second variation that contains
\xi
-fluctuations as

\displaystyle\delta^{2}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}={}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\delta\xi^{T}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\xi\Xi}\delta\Xi+\frac{1}{2}\delta\Xi^{T}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\Xi\xi}\delta\xi+\frac{1}{2}\delta\xi^{T}\mathbf{\Delta}_{\xi}\delta\xi-\frac{1}{2}\delta\xi^{T}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\xi\Phi}\delta\Phi+\frac{1}{2}\delta\Phi^{T}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\Phi\xi}\delta\xi

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\delta\xi^{T}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\xi\phi}\delta\phi+\frac{1}{2}\delta\phi^{T}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\phi\xi}\delta\xi

(94)

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left(\delta\xi^{T}+\left[\delta\Xi^{T}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\Xi\xi}+\delta\Phi^{T}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\Phi\xi}+\delta\phi^{T}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\phi\xi}\right]\overleftarrow{\mathbf{\Delta}}_{\xi}^{-1}\right)\mathbf{\Delta}_{\xi}

\displaystyle\times\left(\delta\xi+\mathbf{\Delta}_{\xi}^{-1}\left[\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\xi\Xi}\delta\Xi-\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\xi\Phi}\delta\Phi-\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\xi\phi}\delta\phi\right]\right)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\left[\delta\Xi^{T}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\Xi\xi}+\delta\Phi^{T}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\Phi\xi}+\delta\phi^{T}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\phi\xi}\right]\mathbf{\Delta}_{\xi}^{-1}\left[\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\xi\Xi}\delta\Xi-\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\xi\Phi}\delta\Phi-\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\xi\phi}\delta\phi\right].

(95)

In the last step we introduced
\mathbf{\Delta}_{\xi}^{-1}
, which
is the matrix-valued Green’s function of
\mathbf{\Delta}_{\xi}
. Note that, restoring spacetime indices, we have

\left(\mathbf{\Delta}^{-1}_{\xi}\left[\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\xi\Xi}\delta\Xi-\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\xi\Phi}\delta\Phi-\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\xi\phi}\delta\phi\right]\right)_{x}\equiv\left(\mathbf{\Delta}^{-1}_{\xi}\right)_{xy}\left[\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\xi\Xi}\delta\Xi-\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\xi\Phi}\delta\Phi-\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\xi\phi}\delta\phi\right]_{y}.

(96)

Similar to
\mathbf{\Delta}_{\xi}^{-1}
we define

\overleftarrow{\mathbf{\Delta}}_{\xi}^{-1}
in such a way that

\displaystyle f_{y}\left(\overleftarrow{\mathbf{\Delta}}_{\xi}^{-1}\right)_{yx}\left(\overleftarrow{\mathbf{\Delta}}_{\xi}\right)_{x}=f_{x},

(97)

where

\left(\overleftarrow{\mathbf{\Delta}}_{\xi}\right)_{x}=\left[\cc\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{F}(-\overleftarrow{\not{P}}-M_{\xi})+\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\xi\xi}\right]_{x}
and we only integrate over
y
. Next, we shift the light fermion multiplet as

\displaystyle\delta\xi^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\delta\xi+\mathbf{\Delta}_{\xi}^{-1}\left[\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\xi\Xi}\delta\Xi-\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\xi\Phi}\delta\Phi-\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\xi\phi}\delta\phi\right],

(98)

\displaystyle\delta\xi^{\prime T}

\displaystyle=\delta\xi^{T}+\left[\delta\Xi^{T}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\Xi\xi}+\delta\Phi^{T}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\Phi\xi}+\delta\phi^{T}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\phi\xi}\right]\overleftarrow{\mathbf{\Delta}}_{\xi}^{-1},

(99)

under which the path integral measure is invariant. Since
\xi
is a
multiplet of Majorana-like spinors, the two shifts in Eqs. (
98
)
and (
99
) are not independent and it is important to show that Eq. (
99
) indeed defines the transpose of Eq. (
98
). The required relation between the two shifts is proved in Appendix
8
. After the
shifts have been performed we arrive at

\displaystyle\delta^{2}\LagNoT_{\xi}={}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\delta\xi^{\prime T}\mathbf{\Delta}_{\xi}\delta\xi^{\prime}-\frac{1}{2}\delta\Xi^{T}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\Xi\xi}\mathbf{\Delta}_{\xi}^{-1}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\xi\Xi}\delta\Xi+\frac{1}{2}\delta\Xi^{T}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\Xi\xi}\mathbf{\Delta}_{\xi}^{-1}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\xi\Phi}\delta\Phi

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\delta\Xi^{T}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\Xi\xi}\mathbf{\Delta}_{\xi}^{-1}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\xi\phi}\delta\phi+\frac{1}{2}\delta\Phi^{T}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\Phi\xi}\mathbf{\Delta}_{\xi}^{-1}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\xi\Xi}\delta\Xi-\frac{1}{2}\delta\Phi^{T}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\Phi\xi}\mathbf{\Delta}_{\xi}^{-1}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\xi\Phi}\delta\Phi

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\delta\Phi^{T}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\Phi\xi}\mathbf{\Delta}_{\xi}^{-1}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\xi\phi}\delta\phi+\frac{1}{2}\delta\phi^{T}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\phi\xi}\mathbf{\Delta}_{\xi}^{-1}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\xi\Xi}\delta\Xi-\frac{1}{2}\delta\phi^{T}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\phi\xi}\mathbf{\Delta}_{\xi}^{-1}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\xi\Phi}\delta\Phi

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\delta\phi^{T}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\phi\xi}\mathbf{\Delta}_{\xi}^{-1}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\xi\phi}\delta\phi.

(100)

Before proceeding to eliminate terms
that mix bosonic fluctuations and fluctuations of heavy fermions
\Xi
, it is convenient to introduce the matrices

\displaystyle M_{AB}=\begin{cases}\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{AB}&\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{A\xi}\\
\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\xi B}&\mathbf{\Delta}_{\xi}\end{pmatrix},&\text{if $A\neq B$}\\
\begin{pmatrix}\Delta_{A}&\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{A\xi}\\
\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\xi B}&\mathbf{\Delta}_{\xi}\end{pmatrix},&\text{if $A=B$}\end{cases}

(101)

as their Schur complements w.r.t.
\mathbf{\Delta}_{\xi}

\displaystyle M_{AB}/\mathbf{\Delta}_{\xi}=\begin{cases}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{AB}-\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{A\xi}\mathbf{\Delta}_{\xi}^{-1}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\xi B},&\text{if $A\neq B$}\\
\Delta_{A}-\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{A\xi}\mathbf{\Delta}_{\xi}^{-1}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\xi A},&\text{if $A=B$}\end{cases}

(102)

appear in the shifted second variation. We also define
\left(M_{AB}\right)_{\xi}\equiv M_{AB}/\mathbf{\Delta}_{\xi}
as a shorthand for the Schur complement. Then, the shifted second variation is given by

\displaystyle\delta^{2}\LagNoT

\displaystyle=\delta^{2}\bar{\LagNoT}_{\text{B}}+\frac{1}{2}\delta\Xi^{T}\left(M_{\Xi\Xi}\right)_{\xi}\delta\Xi-\frac{1}{2}\delta\Xi^{T}\left(M_{\Xi\Phi}\right)_{\xi}\delta\Phi+\frac{1}{2}\delta\Phi^{T}\left(M_{\Phi\Xi}\right)_{\xi}\delta\Xi

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{2}\delta\Xi^{T}\left(M_{\Xi\phi}\right)_{\xi}\delta\phi+\frac{1}{2}\delta\phi^{T}\left(M_{\phi\Xi}\right)_{\xi}\delta\Xi+\frac{1}{2}\delta\xi^{\prime T}\mathbf{\Delta}_{\xi}\delta\xi^{\prime}.

(103)

In Eq. (
103
) the first term on the r.h.s.,

\delta^{2}\bar{\LagNoT}_{\text{B}}
, is obtained by replacing

\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{AB}
and
\mathbf{\Delta}_{A}
in

\delta^{2}\Lag{B}
with
\left(M_{AB}\right)_{\xi}
in accordance with Eq. (
102
).
By shifting
\delta\Xi
in a similar way to
\delta\xi
,

\displaystyle\delta\Xi^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\delta\Xi-\left(M_{\Xi\Xi}\right)_{\xi}^{-1}\left[\left(M_{\Xi\Phi}\right)_{\xi}\delta\Phi+\left(M_{\Xi\phi}\right)_{\xi}\delta\phi\right],

(104)

\displaystyle\delta\Xi^{\prime T}

\displaystyle=\delta\Xi^{T}+\left[\delta\Phi^{T}\left(M_{\Phi\Xi}\right)_{\xi}+\delta\phi^{T}\left(M_{\phi\Xi}\right)_{\xi}\right]\overleftarrow{\left(M_{\Xi\Xi}\right)_{\xi}}^{-1},

(105)

one finds

\displaystyle\delta^{2}\LagNoT={}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\delta\Phi^{T}\left[\left(M_{\Phi\Phi}\right)_{\xi}-\left(M_{\Phi\Xi}\right)_{\xi}\left(M_{\Xi\Xi}\right)_{\xi}^{-1}\left(M_{\Xi\Phi}\right)_{\xi}\right]\delta\Phi

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\delta\phi^{T}\left[\left(M_{\phi\phi}\right)_{\xi}-\left(M_{\phi\Xi}\right)_{\xi}\left(M_{\Xi\Xi}\right)_{\xi}^{-1}\left(M_{\Xi\phi}\right)_{\xi}\right]\delta\phi

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\delta\Phi^{T}\left[\left(M_{\Phi\phi}\right)_{\xi}-\left(M_{\Phi\Xi}\right)_{\xi}\left(M_{\Xi\Xi}\right)_{\xi}^{-1}\left(M_{\Xi\phi}\right)_{\xi}\right]\delta\phi

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\delta\phi^{T}\left[\left(M_{\phi\Phi}\right)_{\xi}-\left(M_{\phi\Xi}\right)_{\xi}\left(M_{\Xi\Xi}\right)_{\xi}^{-1}\left(M_{\Xi\Phi}\right)_{\xi}\right]\delta\Phi

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\delta\xi^{\prime T}\mathbf{\Delta}_{\xi}\delta\xi^{\prime}+\frac{1}{2}\delta\Xi^{\prime T}\left(M_{\Xi\Xi}\right)_{\xi}\delta\Xi^{\prime}

\displaystyle\equiv{}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\begin{pmatrix}\delta\Phi^{T}&\delta\phi^{T}\end{pmatrix}\fluct_{\text{B}}\begin{pmatrix}\delta\Phi\\
\delta\phi\end{pmatrix}+\frac{1}{2}\delta\xi^{\prime T}\mathbf{\Delta}_{\xi}\delta\xi^{\prime}+\frac{1}{2}\delta\Xi^{\prime T}\left(M_{\Xi\Xi}\right)_{\xi}\delta\Xi^{\prime}

(106)

\displaystyle\equiv{}

\displaystyle\delta^{2}\breve{\LagNoT}_{\text{BF}}+\delta^{2}\breve{\LagNoT}_{\text{F}},

(107)

with

\displaystyle\delta^{2}\breve{\LagNoT}_{\text{BF}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\begin{pmatrix}\delta\Phi^{T}&\delta\phi^{T}\end{pmatrix}\fluct_{\text{B}}\begin{pmatrix}\delta\Phi\\
\delta\phi\end{pmatrix},

(108)

\displaystyle\delta^{2}\breve{\LagNoT}_{\text{F}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\begin{pmatrix}\delta\Xi^{\prime T}&\delta\xi^{\prime T}\end{pmatrix}\fluct_{\text{F}}\begin{pmatrix}\delta\Xi^{\prime}\\
\delta\xi^{\prime}\end{pmatrix},

(109)

where we defined

\displaystyle\fluct_{\text{F}}

\displaystyle\equiv\begin{pmatrix}\left(M_{\Xi\Xi}\right)_{\xi}&0\\
0&\mathbf{\Delta}_{\xi}\end{pmatrix},

(110)

\displaystyle\fluct_{\text{B}}

\displaystyle\equiv\begin{pmatrix}\left(\breve{M}_{\Phi\Phi}\right)_{\left(M_{\Xi\Xi}\right)_{\xi}}&\left(\breve{M}_{\Phi\phi}\right)_{\left(M_{\Xi\Xi}\right)_{\xi}}\\
\left(\breve{M}_{\phi\Phi}\right)_{\left(M_{\Xi\Xi}\right)_{\xi}}&\left(\breve{M}_{\phi\phi}\right)_{\left(M_{\Xi\Xi}\right)_{\xi}}\end{pmatrix},

(111)

and

\displaystyle\breve{M}_{AB}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}\left(M_{AB}\right)_{\xi}&\left(M_{A\Xi}\right)_{\xi}\\
\left(M_{\Xi B}\right)_{\xi}&\left(M_{\Xi\Xi}\right)_{\xi}\end{pmatrix}.

(112)

At this point there are no terms including both a bosonic and a
fermionic fluctuation and the path integrals over bosons and fermions
can be performed separately. This concludes the treatment of the UV theory with the result

\displaystyle\hat{\Gamma}^{\text{1\Loop}}_{\text{L,UV}}

\displaystyle=\frac{i}{2}\log\det\fluct_{\text{B}}-\frac{i}{2}\log\det\fluct_{\text{F}},

(113)

where the hat on the generating functional indicates that it is local, which is achieved by inserting local expansions of all heavy background fields as discussed in Chapter
1
. Following the same route as in Chapter
1
we would now like to compute the EFT side of Eq. (
1
) and impose the matching condition. We expect that this cancels the soft region contributions to Eq. (
113
). It is not straightforward to show this using the form of
\hat{\Gamma}^{\text{1\Loop}}_{\text{L,UV}}
given here. We instead note that this one-loop contribution to the generating functional, in general, is a superdeterminant as shown in Ref.
[
86
]
and further discussed in Ref.
[
55
]
, which can be rewritten in terms of sub-determinants in different ways. We here chose an expression that turns the superdeterminant into two determinants of Grassmann even matrices, completely separating fields of different statistics. In the final expression, however, there is no manifest separation of heavy and light fields and the soft region cancellation is obscured. In the next section, we will express the same superdeterminant in a different way, which manifestly separates heavy and light fields and allows for a prove of the soft region cancellation. For the remainder of this section we will simply assume that, after imposing the matching condition, all that is left is the hard region contribution to Eq. (
113
). Using that
\log\det A=\Tr\log A
and performing steps analogous to Eq. (
55
) we find

\displaystyle\Lag{EFT}^{1\Loop}[\bg{\phi},\bg{\xi}]

\displaystyle=\frac{i}{2}\tr\left.\int\frac{\rd^{d}q}{(2\pi)^{d}}\left(\log\fluct_{\text{B}}-\log\fluct_{\text{F}}\right)\right|^{P^{\mu}\rightarrow P^{\mu}-q^{\mu}}_{\text{hard,trunc}}.

(114)

We would now like to absorb the matrices
\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{F}
,
\cc
and
\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{B}
in redefinitions of the coupling matrices
\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{AB}
in order for them not to appear explicitly in the final BSUOLEA operators. To this end, we first note that, with the exception of the explicit appearance of
\mathbf{\Delta}_{\xi}
in
\fluct_{\text{F}}
,
\mathbf{\Delta}_{\xi}
only enters the final result through
\left(M_{AB}\right)_{\xi}
and hence through its inverse. We may write
\left.\mathbf{\Delta}_{\xi}^{-1}\right|^{P_{\mu}\to P_{\mu}-q_{\mu}}_{\text{hard}}\equiv\mathbf{\Delta}_{\xi}^{-1}(q)
as

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Delta}_{\xi}^{-1}(q)

\displaystyle=\left[\cc\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{F}(\not{P}_{\xi}-\not{q}-M_{\xi})+\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\xi\xi}\right]^{-1}

\displaystyle=\left[\mathds{1}-\left(-\not{q}\right)^{-1}\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{F}\cc^{-1}\left(-\cc\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{F}\not{P}_{\xi}+\cc\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{F}M_{\xi}-\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\xi\xi}\right)\right]^{-1}\left(-\not{q}\right)^{-1}\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{F}\cc^{-1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\left[\left(-\not{q}\right)^{-1}\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{F}\cc^{-1}\left(-\cc\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{F}\not{P}_{\xi}+\cc\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{F}M_{\xi}-\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\xi\xi}\right)\right]^{m}\left(-\not{q}\right)^{-1}\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{F}\cc^{-1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\left[\left(-\not{q}\right)^{-1}\left(-\not{P}_{\xi}+M_{\xi}-\mathbf{X}_{\xi\xi}\right)\right]^{m}\left(-\not{q}\right)^{-1}\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{F}\cc^{-1},

(115)

where we defined

\displaystyle\mathbf{X}_{\xi\xi}\equiv\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{F}\cc^{-1}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\xi\xi}.

(116)

Then Eq. (
102
) becomes

\displaystyle\left(M_{AB}\right)_{\xi}=\begin{cases}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{AB}-\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{A\xi}\sum_{n}\left[\left(-\not{q}\right)^{-1}\left(M_{\xi}-\mathbf{X}_{\xi\xi}-\not{P}_{\xi}\right)\right]^{n}\left(-\not{q}\right)^{-1}\mathbf{X}_{\xi B},\,\text{if }A\neq B\\
\mathbf{\Delta}_{A}-\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{A\xi}\sum_{n}\left[\left(-\not{q}\right)^{-1}\left(M_{\xi}-\mathbf{X}_{\xi\xi}-\not{P}_{\xi}\right)\right]^{n}\left(-\not{q}\right)^{-1}\mathbf{X}_{\xi A},~~\text{if }A=B\end{cases}

(117)

where we introduced

\mathbf{X}_{\xi B}\equiv\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{F}\cc^{-1}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\xi B}
and the sum over
n
runs over all non-negative integers. Thus, in any Schur complement w.r.t.
\mathbf{\Delta}_{\xi}
the explicit appearance of
\cc
and
\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{F}
can be avoided by replacing the matrices
\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\xi B}
by the matrices
\mathbf{X}_{\xi B}
. Next we note that
\mathbf{\Delta}_{\Xi}
only enters the result through
\left(\breve{M}_{AB}\right)_{\left(M_{\Xi\Xi}\right)_{\xi}}
except for the explicit appearance of
\left(M_{\Xi\Xi}\right)_{\xi}
in
\fluct_{\text{F}}
. Considering that

\displaystyle\left(M_{\Xi\Xi}\right)_{\xi}^{-1}(q)\equiv{}

\displaystyle\left.\left(M_{\Xi\Xi}\right)_{\xi}^{-1}\right|^{P^{\mu}\rightarrow P^{\mu}-q^{\mu}}_{\text{hard}}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\Bigg[\cc\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{F}\left(-\not{q}-M_{\Xi}\right)+\cc\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{F}\not{P}_{\Xi}+\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\Xi\Xi}

\displaystyle-\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\Xi\xi}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\left[\left(-\not{q}\right)^{-1}\left(-\mathbf{X}_{\xi\xi}-\not{P}_{\xi}+M_{\xi}\right)\right]^{n}\left(-\not{q}\right)^{-1}\mathbf{X}_{\xi\Xi}\Bigg]^{-1}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\Big\{\mathcal{K}_{\Xi}^{-1}\left(-\mathbf{X}_{\Xi\Xi}-\not{P}_{\Xi}\right)

\displaystyle-\mathcal{K}_{\Xi}^{-1}\mathbf{X}_{\Xi\xi}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\left[\left(-\not{q}\right)^{-1}\left(M_{\xi}-\mathbf{X}_{\xi\xi}-\not{P}_{\xi}\right)\right]^{n}\not{q}^{-1}\mathbf{X}_{\xi\Xi}\Big\}^{m}\mathcal{K}_{\Xi}^{-1}\cc^{-1}\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{F},

(118)

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{\Xi}

\displaystyle\equiv\left(-\not{q}-M_{\Xi}\right),

(119)

\displaystyle\mathbf{X}_{\Xi\xi}

\displaystyle\equiv\cc^{-1}\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{F}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\Xi\xi},

(120)

we can again absorb
\cc^{-1}
and
\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{F}
since
\left(\breve{M}_{AB}\right)_{\left(M_{\Xi\Xi}\right)_{\xi}}
always contains

\displaystyle\cc^{-1}\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{F}\left(M_{\Xi B}\right)_{\xi}

\displaystyle=\cc^{-1}\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{F}\left(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\Xi B}-\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\Xi\xi}\mathbf{\Delta}_{\xi}^{-1}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\xi B}\right)=\left(\mathbf{X}_{\Xi B}-\mathbf{X}_{\Xi\xi}\mathbf{\Delta}_{\xi}^{-1}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\xi B}\right),

(121)

where

\displaystyle\mathbf{X}_{\Xi B}=\cc^{-1}\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{F}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\Xi B}.

(122)

It is clear from this discussion that in the evaluation of
\fluct_{\text{B}}
no explicit factors of
\cc
and
\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{F}
appear in the final result. We can further evaluate the contribution from
\fluct_{\text{B}}
by using Eq. (
22
) to write

\displaystyle\log\det\fluct_{\text{B}}={}

\displaystyle\log\det\left(\left(\breve{M}_{\Phi\Phi}\right)_{\left(M_{\Xi\Xi}\right)_{\xi}}-\left(\breve{M}_{\Phi\phi}\right)_{\left(M_{\Xi\Xi}\right)_{\xi}}\left(\breve{M}_{\phi\phi}\right)_{\left(M_{\Xi\Xi}\right)_{\xi}}^{-1}\left(\breve{M}_{\phi\Phi}\right)_{\left(M_{\Xi\Xi}\right)_{\xi}}\right)

\displaystyle+\log\det\left(\breve{M}_{\phi\phi}\right)_{\left(M_{\Xi\Xi}\right)_{\xi}}.

(123)

In Chapter
1
the contribution from the second line vanished completely in the hard region. Here this is not the case since
\left(\breve{M}_{\phi\phi}\right)_{\left(M_{\Xi\Xi}\right)_{\xi}}
depends on the heavy fermions
\Xi
. Expanding this Schur complement out in terms of the original matrices we have

\displaystyle\left.\log\det\left(\breve{M}_{\phi\phi}\right)_{\left(M_{\Xi\Xi}\right)_{\xi}}\right|_{\text{hard}}={}

\displaystyle\log\det\left[\left(M_{\phi\phi}\right)_{\xi}-\left.\left(M_{\phi\Xi}\right)_{\xi}\left(M_{\Xi\Xi}\right)^{-1}_{\xi}\left(M_{\Xi\phi}\right)_{\xi}\right]\right|_{\text{hard}}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\left.\log\det\left(M_{\phi\phi}\right)_{\xi}\right|_{\text{hard}}

\displaystyle+\left.\log\det\left[\mathds{1}-\left(M_{\phi\phi}\right)_{\xi}^{-1}\left(M_{\phi\Xi}\right)_{\xi}\left(M_{\Xi\Xi}\right)^{-1}_{\xi}\left(M_{\Xi\phi}\right)_{\xi}\right]\right|_{\text{hard}}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle-\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n}\Tr\left.\left[\left(M_{\phi\phi}\right)_{\xi}^{-1}\left(M_{\phi\Xi}\right)_{\xi}\left(M_{\Xi\Xi}\right)^{-1}_{\xi}\left(M_{\Xi\phi}\right)_{\xi}\right]^{n}\right|_{\text{hard}}^{P_{\mu}\rightarrow P_{\mu}-q_{\mu}},

(124)

where the first term in the second line of the right hand side vanishes in the hard
region as it only contains contributions from light fields and the trace is a functional trace which includes the loop integration as discussed in Chapter
1
. This trace can now be evaluated by inserting
\left(M_{\phi\phi}\right)_{\xi}^{-1}(q)\equiv\left.\left(M_{\phi\phi}\right)_{\xi}^{-1}\right|^{P_{\mu}\rightarrow P_{\mu}-q_{\mu}}_{\text{hard}}
, given by

\displaystyle\left(M_{\phi\phi}\right)_{\xi}^{-1}(q)

\displaystyle=\left[\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{B}\left(-q^{2}+2q\cdot P_{\phi}-P_{\phi}^{2}+M_{\phi}^{2}\right)+\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\phi\phi}-\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\phi\xi}\mathbf{\Delta}_{\xi}^{-1}(q)\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\xi\phi}\right]^{-1}

\displaystyle=-\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\left(q^{-2}\left[2q\cdot P_{\phi}-P_{\phi}^{2}+M_{\phi}^{2}+\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{B}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\phi\phi}-\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{B}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\phi\xi}\mathbf{\Delta}_{\xi}^{-1}(q)\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\xi\phi}\right]\right)^{k}q^{-2}\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{B},

(125)

where
\mathbf{\Delta}_{\xi}^{-1}(q)
is given in Eq. (
115
), as well as Eq. (
118
) and the appropriate expressions for
\left(M_{\Xi\phi}\right)_{\xi}
and
\left(M_{\phi\Xi}\right)_{\xi}
. We abstain from explicitly writing out the resulting, somewhat lengthy, expression here. It should be noted that since
\left(M_{\phi\phi}\right)_{\xi}^{-1}
always appears in the combination
\left(M_{\phi\phi}\right)_{\xi}^{-1}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\phi B}
we can absorb the
\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{B}
arising from
\left(M_{\phi\phi}\right)_{\xi}^{-1}
if we define
\mathbf{X}_{\phi B}\equiv\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{B}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\phi B}
, where
B
may be any field, including
\phi
. This assures that no explicit factors of
\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{B}
appear in the final operators that are generated from Eq. (
124
). To evaluate the first line of Eq. (
123
) we first note that

\displaystyle\left(\breve{M}_{\phi\phi}\right)_{\left(M_{\Xi\Xi}\right)_{\xi}}^{-1}

\displaystyle=\left[\mathbf{\Delta}_{\phi}-\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\phi\xi}\mathbf{\Delta}_{\xi}^{-1}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\xi\phi}-\left(M_{\phi\Xi}\right)_{\xi}\left(M_{\Xi\Xi}\right)_{\xi}^{-1}\left(M_{\Xi\phi}\right)_{\xi}\right]^{-1}

(126)

can be computed in exactly the same way as
\left(M_{\phi\phi}\right)_{\xi}^{-1}
in Eq. (
125
) and can be obtained from that result by simply replacing
\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\phi\phi}-\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\phi\xi}\mathbf{\Delta}_{\xi}^{-1}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\xi\phi}
by

\displaystyle\mathbf{F}_{\phi\phi}=\left.\left(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\phi\phi}-\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\phi\xi}\mathbf{\Delta}_{\xi}^{-1}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\xi\phi}-\left(M_{\phi\Xi}\right)_{\xi}\left(M_{\Xi\Xi}\right)_{\xi}^{-1}\left(M_{\Xi\phi}\right)_{\xi}\right)\right|^{P_{\mu}\rightarrow P_{\mu}-q_{\mu}},

(127)

which means that

\displaystyle\left(\breve{M}_{\phi\phi}\right)_{\left(M_{\Xi\Xi}\right)_{\xi}}^{-1}(q)

\displaystyle=-\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\left(q^{-2}\left[2q\cdot P_{\phi}-P_{\phi}^{2}+M_{\phi}^{2}+\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{B}\mathbf{F}_{\phi\phi}\right]\right)^{k}q^{-2}\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{B}.

(128)

All of the matrices that enter
\mathbf{F}_{\phi\phi}
have already been written out explicitly and by inserting these into Eq. (
128
) one obtains an explicit expression for
\left(\breve{M}_{\phi\phi}\right)_{\left(M_{\Xi\Xi}\right)_{\xi}}^{-1}
. This expression is again rather lengthy and is therefore not given explicitly. Note that it is again possible to absorb all appearances of
\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{B}
by defining
\mathbf{X}_{\phi B}\equiv\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{B}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\phi B}
. With Eq. (
128
) at hand all the matrices entering

\displaystyle\mathbf{F}_{\Phi\Phi}\equiv{}

\displaystyle\left(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\Phi\Phi}-\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\Phi\xi}\mathbf{\Delta}_{\xi}^{-1}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\xi\Phi}-\left(M_{\Phi\Xi}\right)_{\xi}\left(M_{\Xi\Xi}\right)_{\xi}^{-1}\left(M_{\Xi\Phi}\right)_{\xi}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.-\left(\breve{M}_{\Phi\phi}\right)_{\left(M_{\Xi\Xi}\right)_{\xi}}\left(\breve{M}_{\phi\phi}\right)_{\left(M_{\Xi\Xi}\right)_{\xi}}^{-1}\left(\breve{M}_{\phi\Phi}\right)_{\left(M_{\Xi\Xi}\right)_{\xi}}\right)\right|^{P_{\mu}\rightarrow P_{\mu}-q_{\mu}}

(129)

have been given explicitly and the first line of Eq. (
123
) can be written as

\displaystyle\Tr\log\left.\left[\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{B}\left(-q^{2}+2q\cdot P_{\Phi}-P_{\Phi}^{2}+M_{\Phi}^{2}\right)+\mathbf{F}_{\Phi\Phi}\right]\right|_{\text{hard}}

\displaystyle=\Tr\log\left.\left[\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{B}\left(-q^{2}+M_{\Phi}^{2}\right)\right]\right|_{\text{hard}}

\displaystyle\quad+\Tr\log\left.\left[\mathds{1}-\left(q^{2}-M_{\Phi}^{2}\right)^{-1}\left(2q\cdot P_{\Phi}-P_{\Phi}^{2}+\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{B}\mathbf{F}_{\Phi\Phi}\right)\right]\right|_{\text{hard}}.

Finally, the first term yields an infinite constant, which can be ignored and in the second term we can expand the log to yield

\displaystyle\Tr\log\left.\left[\mathds{1}-\left(q^{2}-M_{\Phi}^{2}\right)^{-1}\left(2q\cdot P_{\Phi}-P_{\Phi}^{2}+\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{B}\mathbf{F}_{\Phi\Phi}\right)\right]\right|_{\text{hard}}

\displaystyle=-\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n}\Tr\left.\left[\left(q^{2}-M_{\Phi}^{2}\right)^{-1}\left(2q\cdot P_{\Phi}-P_{\Phi}^{2}+\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{B}\mathbf{F}_{\Phi\Phi}\right)\right]^{n}\right|_{\text{hard}}.

(130)

By defining
\mathbf{X}_{\Phi B}\equiv\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{B}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\Phi B}
we can again remove all explicit factors of
\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{B}
. This concludes the discussion of the contributions to the effective Lagrangian originating from
\fluct_{\text{B}}
. We briefly summarise the results below. We have shown that the full contribution to the effective action can be expressed as

\displaystyle\Lag{EFT,B}^{1\Loop}={}

\displaystyle\left(-\frac{i}{2}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n}\Tr\left.\left[\left(q^{2}-M_{\Phi}^{2}\right)^{-1}\left(2q\cdot P_{\Phi}-P_{\Phi}^{2}+\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{B}\mathbf{F}_{\Phi\Phi}\right)\right]^{n}\right|_{\text{hard}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.-\frac{i}{2}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n}\Tr\left.\left[\left(M_{\phi\phi}\right)_{\xi}^{-1}\left(M_{\phi\Xi}\right)_{\xi}\left(M_{\Xi\Xi}\right)^{-1}_{\xi}\left(M_{\Xi\phi}\right)_{\xi}\right]^{n}\right|_{\text{hard}}^{P_{\mu}\rightarrow P_{\mu}-q_{\mu}}\right)_{\text{trunc}},

(131)

with
\mathbf{F}_{\Phi\Phi}
,
\left(\breve{M}_{\phi\phi}\right)^{-1}_{\left(M_{\Xi\Xi}\right)_{\xi}}
,
\left(M_{\phi\phi}\right)_{\xi}^{-1}
,
\left(M_{\Xi\Xi}\right)_{\xi}^{-1}
and
\mathbf{\Delta}_{\xi}^{-1}
explicitly given in Eqs. (
129
), (
128
), (
125
), (
118
) and (
115
), respectively. To perform the evaluation of Eq. (
131
) is a tedious task, which is not feasible by hand. Therefore, a Mathematica code was developed that performs these expansions up to a desired mass dimension. In this code, the coupling matrices in the bosonic sector are assumed to contain up to one open covariant derivative and those in the fermionic sector are assumed to not contain any open covariant derivatives as discussed in Section
1
. The evaluation of the loop integrals is not a problem since they can be reduced to basis integrals for which a general formula is known, see e.g. Refs.
[
53
,
56
,
61
]
and Appendix
9
.

To complete the calculation we need to compute the contribution from
\fluct_{\text{F}}
which reads

\displaystyle\log\det\fluct_{\text{F}}=\log\det\left(M_{\Xi\Xi}\right)_{\xi}+\log\det\mathbf{\Delta}_{\xi}.

(132)

Again, we are only interested in the contribution from the hard region
where the light only part
\log\det\mathbf{\Delta}_{\xi}
vanishes. Hence we only need to consider

\left(M_{\Xi\Xi}\right)_{\xi}
. We find

\displaystyle\Tr\log\Big(

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Delta}_{\Xi}(q)-\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\Xi\xi}\mathbf{\Delta}_{\xi}^{-1}(q)\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\xi\Xi}\Big)

\displaystyle=\Tr\log\left(\cc\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{F}\mathcal{K}_{\Xi}+\cc\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{F}\not{P}_{\Xi}+\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\Xi\Xi}-\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\Xi\xi}\mathbf{\Delta}_{\xi}^{-1}(q)\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\xi\Xi}\right)

\displaystyle=\Tr\log\left(\cc\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{F}\mathcal{K}_{\Xi}\right)+\Tr\log\left[\mathds{1}-\mathcal{K}_{\Xi}^{-1}\left(-\not{P}_{\Xi}-\mathbf{X}_{\Xi\Xi}+\mathbf{X}_{\Xi\xi}\mathbf{\Delta}_{\xi}^{-1}(q)\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\xi\Xi}\right)\right],

(133)

where the first term on the r.h.s. of Eq. (
133
) is
absorbed in the normalisation of the path integral. Expanding the logarithm yields

\displaystyle\Lag{EFT,F}^{1\Loop}

\displaystyle=\left.-\frac{i}{2}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n}\Tr\left[\mathcal{K}_{\Xi}^{-1}\left(-\not{P}_{\Xi}-\mathbf{X}_{\Xi\Xi}+\mathbf{X}_{\Xi\xi}\mathbf{\Delta}_{\xi}^{-1}(q)\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\xi\Xi}\right)\right]^{n}\right|_{\text{hard,trunc}}^{P_{\mu}\rightarrow P_{\mu}-q_{\mu}},

(134)

where
\mathbf{\Delta}_{\xi}^{-1}(q)
from Eq. (
115
) has to be inserted. This trace is also implemented in a Mathematica code that generates all terms up to a given mass dimension. The full computation for the sum

\displaystyle\Lag{EFT}^{1\Loop}=\Lag{EFT,B}^{1\Loop}+\Lag{EFT,F}^{1\Loop}

(135)

has been performed and operators up to mass dimension six have been retained. The full result, which constitutes the BSUOLEA and suffices to match any renormalisable, Lorentz invariant quantum field theory containing scalar fields, vector fields and spin-
1/2
fermions to an EFT with operators up to mass dimension six, can be found at
[
87
]
. As was already mentioned in the previous chapter, it can be shown that in the final expressions every
P_{\mu}
can be written in a commutator of the form

[P_{\mu},\bullet]
, as shown in Refs.
[
50
,
51
]
. This has the interpretation that
P_{\mu}
acts on whatever is to its right in the commutator, since the covariant derivative satisfies the Leibniz rule. For example,
[P_{\mu},U_{\Phi\Phi}]=\left(P_{\mu}U_{\Phi\Phi}\right)+U_{\Phi\Phi}P_{\mu}-U_{\Phi\Phi}P_{\mu}=\left(P_{\mu}U_{\Phi\Phi}\right)
, where in the first term in the intermediate step and in the final result the covariant derivative only acts within the parentheses. If the covariant derivatives are not brought into commutators they have to be interpreted as acting on everything to their right, which makes the use of the operators obscure and difficult. To combine all
P^{\mu}
operators into commutators one can either explicitly use
the Baker-Campbell-Hausdorff formula in the calculation, introducing an extra integration as was done in Ref.
[
53
]
, or construct a basis for these commutators and
then solve a system of equations to fix the coefficients of the basis
elements as was pointed out in Ref.
[
61
]
. The Mathematica code that was used to expand the traces and compute the corresponding universal integral coefficients can also be used to find such bases and solve the system of equations to translate expressions with open covariant derivatives into expressions where every covariant derivative appears in a commutator. This was used to transform the final BSUOLEA result into a form without open covariant derivatives.

3
Proof of soft region cancellation

We now turn to the proof of the soft region cancellation, which was postponed in the previous section. As was discussed in Section
1
we do not address the subtleties that can arise when working in the broken phase of the EFT and we are not aware of a complete proof of the soft region cancellation that extends to this case. The basic idea of the proof presented here follows the steps outlined in Section
3
, however, as mentioned before it is not entirely trivial to make the cancellation manifest due to the presence of mixed statistics. To the best of our knowledge, the proof presented here, which is an extension of the proof for the scalar case given in Ref.
[
61
]
, has not been presented elsewhere. In order to proof the cancellation of the soft contributions to
\Gamma^{1\Loop}_{\text{L,UV}}
with the genuine one-loop EFT contributions to
\Gamma_{\text{EFT}}
, it is necessary to relate
Q_{\text{EFT}}
to
Q_{\text{UV}}
. To this end, we consider the equations

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\left.\funcDer{}{\xi_{y}}\right|\left(\funcDer{\barLag}{\classical{\hat{\Phi}}}\right)_{z}=\left.\funcDer{\classical{\hat{\Phi}}^{x}}{\xi_{y}}\right|\left(\left.\funcDer{{}^{2}\barLag}{\classical{\hat{\Phi}}^{2}}\right|\right)_{xz}+\left.\funcDer{\classical{\hat{\Xi}}^{x}}{\xi_{y}}\right|\left(\left.\secondfuncDer{\barLag}{\classical{\hat{\Xi}}}{\classical{\hat{\Phi}}}\right|\right)_{xz}+\left(\left.\secondfuncDer{\barLag}{\xi}{\classical{\hat{\Phi}}}\right|\right)_{yz},

(136)

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\left.\funcDer{}{\xi_{y}}\right|\left(\funcDer{\barLag}{\classical{\hat{\Xi}}}\right)_{z}=\left.\funcDer{\classical{\hat{\Xi}}^{x}}{\xi_{y}}\right|\left(\left.\funcDer{{}^{2}\barLag}{\classical{\hat{\Xi}}^{2}}\right|\right)_{xz}+\left.\funcDer{\classical{\hat{\Phi}}^{x}}{\xi_{y}}\right|\left(\left.\secondfuncDer{\barLag}{\classical{\hat{\Phi}}}{\classical{\hat{\Xi}}}\right|\right)_{xz}+\left(\left.\secondfuncDer{\barLag}{\xi}{\classical{\hat{\Xi}}}\right|\right)_{yz},

(137)

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\left.\funcDer{}{\phi_{y}}\right|\left(\funcDer{\barLag}{\classical{\hat{\Phi}}}\right)_{z}=\left.\funcDer{\classical{\hat{\Phi}}^{x}}{\phi_{y}}\right|\left(\left.\funcDer{{}^{2}\barLag}{\classical{\hat{\Phi}}^{2}}\right|\right)_{xz}+\left.\funcDer{\classical{\hat{\Xi}}^{x}}{\phi_{y}}\right|\left(\left.\secondfuncDer{\barLag}{\classical{\hat{\Xi}}}{\classical{\hat{\Phi}}}\right|\right)_{xz}+\left(\left.\secondfuncDer{\barLag}{\phi}{\classical{\hat{\Phi}}}\right|\right)_{yz},

(138)

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\left.\funcDer{}{\phi_{y}}\right|\left(\funcDer{\barLag}{\classical{\hat{\Xi}}}\right)_{z}=\left.\funcDer{\classical{\hat{\Xi}}^{x}}{\phi_{y}}\right|\left(\left.\funcDer{{}^{2}\barLag}{\classical{\hat{\Xi}}^{2}}\right|\right)_{xz}+\left.\funcDer{\classical{\hat{\Xi}}^{x}}{\phi_{y}}\right|\left(\left.\secondfuncDer{\barLag}{\classical{\hat{\Xi}}}{\classical{\hat{\Phi}}}\right|\right)_{xz}+\left(\left.\secondfuncDer{\barLag}{\phi}{\classical{\hat{\Xi}}}\right|\right)_{yz},

(139)

where
\barLag\equiv S_{\text{UV}}[\classical{\hat{\Phi}}[\phi,\xi],\classical{\hat{\Xi}}[\phi,\xi],\phi,\xi]
and derivatives with a vertical bar are evaluated at
\phi=\bg{\phi}
and
\xi=\bg{\xi}
. Eqs. (
136
)–(
139
) are analogous to Eq. (
3
).
Defining

\displaystyle N_{AB}=\begin{cases}\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{AB}&\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{A\Xi}\\
\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\Xi B}&\mathbf{\Delta}_{\Xi}\end{pmatrix},&\text{if $A\neq B$}\\
\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{\Delta}_{A}&\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{A\Xi}\\
\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\Xi B}&\mathbf{\Delta}_{\Xi}\end{pmatrix},&\text{if $A=B$}\end{cases},

(140)

these equations can be rewritten as

\displaystyle\left.\funcDer{\classical{\hat{\Phi}}^{x}}{\xi_{y}}\right|

\displaystyle=-\LocalSchur{\xi}{\Phi}_{yz}\LocalSchur{\Phi}{\Phi}_{zx}^{-1},

(141)

\displaystyle\left.\funcDer{\classical{\hat{\Xi}}^{x}}{\xi_{y}}\right|

\displaystyle=\LocalSchur{\xi}{\Phi}_{yz}\LocalSchur{\Phi}{\Phi}_{zu}^{-1}\left(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\Phi\Xi}\right)_{uw}\left(\hat{\mathbf{\Delta}}_{\Xi}^{-1}\right)_{wx}-\left(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\xi\Xi}\right)_{yz}\left(\hat{\mathbf{\Delta}}_{\Xi}^{-1}\right)_{zx},

(142)

\displaystyle\left.\funcDer{\classical{\hat{\Phi}}^{x}}{\phi_{y}}\right|

\displaystyle=-\LocalSchur{\phi}{\Phi}_{yz}\LocalSchur{\Phi}{\Phi}_{zx}^{-1},

(143)

\displaystyle\left.\funcDer{\classical{\hat{\Xi}}^{x}}{\phi_{y}}\right|

\displaystyle=\LocalSchur{\phi}{\Phi}_{yz}\LocalSchur{\Phi}{\Phi}_{zu}^{-1}\left(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\Phi\Xi}\right)_{uw}\left(\hat{\mathbf{\Delta}}_{\Xi}^{-1}\right)_{wx}-\left(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\phi\Xi}\right)_{yz}\left(\hat{\mathbf{\Delta}}_{\Xi}^{-1}\right)_{zx},

(144)

where we used that

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{AB}=-\left.\secondfuncDer{\barLag}{A}{B}\right|,

(145)

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Delta}_{A}=-\left.\funcDer{{}^{2}\barLag}{A^{2}}\right|.

(146)

Notice that, as introduced in Chapter
1
, we denote objects that are expanded in inverse masses to obtain local expressions with hats. We prove Eqs. (
141
) and (
142
), the remaining two equations follow analogously. Inserting Eqs. (
145
) and (
146
) into Eqs. (
136
) and (
137
) we obtain

\displaystyle\left.\funcDer{\classical{\hat{\Xi}}^{x}}{\xi_{y}}\right|\left(\hat{\mathbf{\Delta}}_{\Xi}\right)_{xz}

\displaystyle=-\left.\funcDer{\classical{\hat{\Phi}}^{x}}{\xi_{y}}\right|\left(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\Phi\Xi}\right)_{xz}-\left(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\xi\Xi}\right)_{yz},

(147)

\displaystyle\left.\funcDer{\classical{\hat{\Phi}}^{x}}{\xi_{y}}\right|\left(\hat{\mathbf{\Delta}}_{\Phi}\right)_{xz}

\displaystyle=-\left.\funcDer{\classical{\hat{\Xi}}^{x}}{\xi_{y}}\right|\left(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\Xi\Phi}\right)_{xz}-\left(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\xi\Phi}\right)_{yz},

(148)

from which we get

\displaystyle\left.\funcDer{\classical{\hat{\Xi}}^{u}}{\xi_{y}}\right|

\displaystyle=-\left.\funcDer{\classical{\hat{\Phi}}^{x}}{\xi_{y}}\right|\left(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\Phi\Xi}\right)_{xz}\left(\hat{\mathbf{\Delta}}_{\Xi}\right)_{zu}^{-1}-\left(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\xi\Xi}\right)_{yz}\left(\hat{\mathbf{\Delta}}_{\Xi}\right)_{zu}^{-1},

(149)

and

\displaystyle\left.\funcDer{\classical{\hat{\Phi}}^{x}}{\xi_{y}}\right|

\displaystyle=\left[\left(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\xi\Xi}\right)\left(\hat{\mathbf{\Delta}}_{\Xi}\right)^{-1}\left(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\Xi\Phi}\right)-\left(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\xi\Phi}\right)\right]_{yz}\left[\hat{\mathbf{\Delta}}_{\Phi}-\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\Phi\Xi}\hat{\mathbf{\Delta}}_{\Xi}^{-1}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\Xi\Phi}\right]^{-1}_{zx},

(150)

where Eq. (
150
) is obtained by inserting Eq. (
149
) into Eq. (
148
). Expressing Eq. (
150
) through the appropriate Schur complements we obtain Eq. (
141
). Inserting that result back into Eq. (
149
) and expressing this through Schur complements we obtain Eq. (
142
).
We then have

\displaystyle\left(\mathbf{\Delta}_{\xi}^{\text{EFT}}\right)_{xy}

\displaystyle=-\left.\secondfuncDer{S^{\text{tree}}_{\text{EFT}}}{\xi_{x}}{\xi_{y}}\right|=-\left.\funcDer{}{\xi_{x}}\right|\funcDer{\barLag}{\xi_{y}}=\left.\funcDer{\classical{\hat{\Phi}}^{z}}{\xi_{x}}\right|\left(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\Phi\xi}\right)_{zy}+\left.\funcDer{\classical{\hat{\Xi}}^{z}}{\xi_{x}}\right|\left(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\Xi\xi}\right)_{zy}+\left(\hat{\mathbf{\Delta}}_{\xi}\right)_{xy}

\displaystyle=-\LocalSchur{\xi}{\Phi}_{xw}\LocalSchur{\Phi}{\Phi}^{-1}_{wz}\LocalSchur{\Phi}{\xi}_{zy}+\LocalSchur{\xi}{\xi}_{xy}

(151)

and similarly

\displaystyle\left(\mathbf{\Delta}_{\phi}^{\text{EFT}}\right)_{xy}

\displaystyle=-\LocalSchur{\phi}{\Phi}_{xw}\LocalSchur{\Phi}{\Phi}^{-1}_{wz}\LocalSchur{\Phi}{\phi}_{zy}+\LocalSchur{\phi}{\phi}_{xy},

(152)

\displaystyle\left(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\phi\xi}^{\text{EFT}}\right)_{xy}

\displaystyle=-\LocalSchur{\phi}{\Phi}_{xw}\LocalSchur{\Phi}{\Phi}^{-1}_{wz}\LocalSchur{\Phi}{\xi}_{zy}+\LocalSchur{\phi}{\xi}_{xy},

(153)

\displaystyle\left(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\xi\phi}^{\text{EFT}}\right)_{xy}

\displaystyle=-\LocalSchur{\xi}{\Phi}_{xw}\LocalSchur{\Phi}{\Phi}^{-1}_{wz}\LocalSchur{\Phi}{\phi}_{zy}+\LocalSchur{\xi}{\phi}_{xy}.

(154)

This completely determines
Q_{\text{EFT}}
in terms of the local operator expansions of the quantities appearing in
Q_{\text{UV}}
as

\displaystyle\fluct_{\text{EFT}}=\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{\Delta}_{\phi}^{\text{EFT}}&-\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\phi\xi}^{\text{EFT}}\\
\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\xi\phi}^{\text{EFT}}&-\mathbf{\Delta}_{\xi}^{\text{EFT}}\end{pmatrix},

(155)

where

\displaystyle\delta^{2}\Lag{EFT}=-\frac{1}{2}\begin{pmatrix}\delta\phi^{T}&\delta\xi^{T}\end{pmatrix}\fluct_{\text{EFT}}\begin{pmatrix}\delta\phi\\
\delta\xi\end{pmatrix}.

(156)

The contribution to the one-loop generating functional of 1PI correlation functions is given by
8
8

8

A discussion of Gaussian functional integrals expressed as superdeterminants can be found in Refs.
[
55
,
86
]
. The sign difference introduced between
\fluct_{\text{EFT}}
and
\tilde{\fluct}_{\text{EFT}}
is due to the parameterisation chosen here.

\displaystyle\Gamma^{1\Loop}_{\EFT}

\displaystyle=S^{1\Loop}_{\EFT}+\frac{i}{2}\log\Sdet\,\tilde{\fluct}_{\text{EFT}},

(157)

where
\Sdet\,\tilde{\fluct}_{\text{EFT}}
denotes the superdeterminant of the supermatrix

\displaystyle\tilde{\fluct}_{\text{EFT}}=\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{\Delta}_{\phi}^{\text{EFT}}&\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\phi\xi}^{\text{EFT}}\\
\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\xi\phi}^{\text{EFT}}&\mathbf{\Delta}_{\xi}^{\text{EFT}}\end{pmatrix}.

(158)

For some details relating to supermatrices see Appendix
10
.
\Gamma^{1\Loop}_{\EFT}
has to be equal to the corresponding one-loop contribution to the generating functional of 1LPI correlation functions in the UV theory, which we reconsider now. Introducing the vector of all fluctuations

\displaystyle\delta\chi=\begin{pmatrix}\delta\Phi\\
\delta\phi\\
\delta\Xi\\
\delta\xi\end{pmatrix},

(159)

the second variation of the UV Lagrangian can be written as

\displaystyle\delta^{2}\Lag{UV}=-\frac{1}{2}\delta\chi^{T}\fluct_{\text{UV}}\delta\chi,

(160)

where
\fluct_{\text{UV}}
is the complete fluctuation operator of the UV theory, which is a supermatrix and can be expressed in analogy to
\fluct_{\text{EFT}}
by collecting the terms contributing to the second variation of the action into matrix form. The corresponding Gaussian path integral simply yields the superdeterminant of the supermatrix
\tilde{\fluct}_{\text{UV}}

\displaystyle\int\measure\delta\chi\exp{\left[-\frac{i}{2}\delta\chi^{T}Q_{\text{UV}}\delta\chi\right]}=\Sdet\,\tilde{\fluct}_{\text{UV}},

(161)

which can be expressed in many equivalent ways, one of which was explicitly derived and subsequently used in the previous section.
\tilde{\fluct}_{\text{UV}}
differs from
\fluct_{\text{UV}}
by the appropriate signs to yield the correct superdeterminant as pointed out in footnote
8
and in analogy to
\tilde{\fluct}_{\text{EFT}}
. We now derive an alternative form of the same quantity using steps analogous to those used in the previous section with the goal to separate fluctuations of heavy fields from fluctuations of light fields. Using the shift invariance of the integral measure we may write

\displaystyle\delta^{2}\Lag{UV}\supset{}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left[\delta\Xi^{T}+\left(\delta\Phi^{T}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\Phi\Xi}+\delta\phi^{T}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\phi\Xi}+\delta\xi^{T}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\xi\Xi}\right)\overleftarrow{\mathbf{\Delta}}_{\Xi}^{-1}\right]\mathbf{\Delta}_{\Xi}

\displaystyle\times\left[\delta\Xi-\mathbf{\Delta}_{\Xi}^{-1}\left(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\Xi\Phi}\delta\Phi+\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\Xi\phi}\delta\phi-\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\Xi\xi}\delta\xi\right)\right]

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\left(\delta\Phi^{T}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\Phi\Xi}+\delta\phi^{T}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\phi\Xi}+\delta\xi^{T}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\xi\Xi}\right)\mathbf{\Delta}_{\Xi}^{-1}\left(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\Xi\Phi}\delta\Phi+\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\Xi\phi}\delta\phi-\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\Xi\xi}\delta\xi\right)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\delta\Phi^{T}\mathbf{\Delta}_{\Phi}\delta\Phi-\frac{1}{2}\delta\phi^{T}\mathbf{\Delta}_{\phi}\delta\phi+\frac{1}{2}\delta\xi^{T}\mathbf{\Delta}_{\xi}\delta\xi-\frac{1}{2}\delta\Phi^{T}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\Phi\phi}\delta\phi-\frac{1}{2}\delta\phi^{T}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\phi\Phi}\delta\Phi

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\delta\xi^{T}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\xi\Phi}\delta\Phi-\frac{1}{2}\delta\xi^{T}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\xi\phi}\delta\phi+\frac{1}{2}\delta\Phi^{T}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\Phi\xi}\delta\xi+\frac{1}{2}\delta\phi^{T}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\phi\xi}\delta\xi

\displaystyle\longrightarrow

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\delta\Phi^{T}\Schur{\Phi}{\Phi}\delta\Phi-\frac{1}{2}\delta\phi^{T}\Schur{\Phi}{\Phi}\delta\phi+\frac{1}{2}\delta\xi^{T}\Schur{\xi}{\xi}\delta\xi

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\delta\Phi^{T}\Schur{\Phi}{\phi}\delta\phi-\frac{1}{2}\delta\phi^{T}\Schur{\phi}{\Phi}\delta\Phi-\frac{1}{2}\delta\xi^{T}\Schur{\xi}{\Phi}\delta\Phi
