,
\dot{\rho}
,
\dot{\sigma}

Table 1:
Index naming conventions used throughout this chapter.

1
Integrating out the top quark from the SM

We consider the corrections to the Higgs
tadpole and mass parameter that arise when integrating out the top
quark from the SM. This example is purposely chosen to be very simple in order to illustrate the use of the BSUOLEA. We restrict the interaction Lagrangian
to only one coupling

\displaystyle\Lag{SM}\supset-\frac{g_{t}}{\sqrt{2}}h\bar{t}t,

(1)

where
h
denotes the physical Higgs field,
t
is the top quark and

g_{t}
is the top Yukawa coupling. The relevant operators of the BSUOLEA of Eq. (
168
) are given by

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\kappa}\Lag{EFT}^{1\Loop}=\tr\Bigg\{

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}M_{\Xi i}M_{\Xi j}^{3}\ZI^{13}_{ij}[P_{\mu},(\mathbf{U}_{\Xi\Xi})_{ij}][P^{\mu},(\mathbf{U}_{\Xi\Xi})_{ji}]

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\ZI[q^{4}]^{22}_{ij}\gamma^{\nu}[P_{\mu},(\mathbf{U}_{\Xi\Xi})_{ij}]\gamma_{\nu}[P^{\mu},(\mathbf{U}_{\Xi\Xi})_{ji}]

\displaystyle-\ZI[q^{4}]^{22}_{ij}\gamma^{\nu}[P_{\nu},(\mathbf{U}_{\Xi\Xi})_{ij}]\gamma_{\mu}[P^{\mu},(\mathbf{U}_{\Xi\Xi})_{ji}]

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}M_{\Xi i}\ZI^{1}_{i}(\mathbf{U}_{\Xi\Xi})_{ii}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4}M_{\Xi i}M_{\Xi j}\ZI^{11}_{ij}(\mathbf{U}_{\Xi\Xi})_{ij}(\mathbf{U}_{\Xi\Xi})_{ji}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4}\ZI[q^{2}]^{11}_{ij}\gamma^{\mu}(\mathbf{U}_{\Xi\Xi})_{ij}\gamma_{\mu}(\mathbf{U}_{\Xi\Xi})_{ji}\Bigg\},

(2)

where
M_{\Xi i}
denotes the
i
th component of
M_{\Xi}=\begin{pmatrix}m_{t}&m_{t}\end{pmatrix}^{T}
with
m_{t}
being the mass of the top quark and
\Xi=\begin{pmatrix}t_{\dot{\alpha}I}&\ccfield{t}_{\dot{\alpha}I}\end{pmatrix}^{T}
. Note that
M_{\Xi 1}=M_{\Xi 2}
and thus we are in the case of a degenerate mass as mentioned in Section
1
. The matrix
\mathbf{U}_{\Xi\Xi}
is given by

\displaystyle(\mathbf{U}_{\Xi\Xi})_{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}IJ}=\begin{pmatrix}(U_{\bar{t}t})_{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}IJ}&0\\
0&\cc^{-1}_{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\rho}}(U_{t\bar{t}})_{\dot{\rho}\dot{\sigma}IJ}\cc^{-1}_{\dot{\sigma}\dot{\beta}}\end{pmatrix}=-\frac{g_{t}}{\sqrt{2}}h\delta_{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}}\delta_{IJ}\mathbf{1}_{2},

(3)

where
\mathbf{1}_{2}
is the
2\times 2
identity matrix. In Eq. (
2
) we
included terms with two covariant derivatives in order to obtain the
field-redefinition of the Higgs field that is necessary to canonically
normalise the corresponding Higgs field,
\hat{h}
, in the effective
theory. Since this redefinition arises from the correction to the
kinetic term only, we can set
P^{\mu}=i\partial^{\mu}
. In order to illustrate the handling of spinor indices, the appearance of derivatives and mass-degenerate coefficients we compute the contribution of the operator in the third line of Eq. (
2
) in detail. Since the masses are degenerate we have that

\displaystyle\ZI[q^{4}]^{22}_{11}=\ZI[q^{4}]^{22}_{12}=\ZI[q^{4}]^{22}_{21}=\ZI[q^{4}]^{22}_{22}=\ZI[q^{4}]^{4}_{t},

(4)

meaning that the integral coefficient is independent of the indices. For the operator structure we find

\displaystyle-\tr\{\gamma^{\nu}[P_{\nu},(\mathbf{U}_{\Xi\Xi})_{ij}]\gamma_{\mu}[P^{\mu},(\mathbf{U}_{\Xi\Xi})_{ji}]\}

\displaystyle=\frac{g_{t}^{2}}{2}\tr\{\gamma^{\nu}_{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}}\partial_{\nu}\left(h\delta_{\dot{\beta}\dot{\rho}}\delta_{IJ}\mathbf{1}_{2}\right)\gamma^{\mu}_{\dot{\rho}\dot{\sigma}}\partial_{\mu}\left(h\delta_{\dot{\sigma}\dot{\alpha}}\delta_{JI}\mathbf{1}_{2}\right)\}

\displaystyle=12g_{t}^{2}\partial_{\mu}h\partial^{\mu}h,

(5)

which together with Eq. (
4
) yields the contribution

\displaystyle 12g_{t}^{2}\ZI[q^{4}]^{4}_{t}(\partial_{\mu}h)(\partial^{\mu}h)=-\frac{1}{2}g_{t}^{2}\log\frac{m_{t}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\partial_{\mu}h\partial^{\mu}h,

(6)

where
\mu
is the matching scale and we used

\displaystyle\ZI[q^{4}]^{4}_{t}=\frac{1}{12\eps}-\frac{1}{24}\log\frac{m_{t}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}.

(7)

We are here allowed to drop the
\eps
-dependence of the integral since no
\eps
-dependence arises from the operator structure. This is not the case for the second line of Eq. (
2
), where the contraction of the gamma matrices produces a
d=4-\eps
. Proceeding in a similar fashion for the remaining contributions to Eq. (
2
) we find

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\kappa}\Lag{EFT}^{1\Loop}={}

\displaystyle-3g_{t}^{2}\left(m_{t}^{4}\ZI^{4}_{t}-2d\ZI[q^{4}]^{4}_{t}-4\ZI[q^{4}]^{4}_{t}\right)(\partial_{\mu}h)(\partial^{\mu}h)

\displaystyle-3g_{t}^{2}\left(\ZI^{2}_{t}m_{t}^{2}+d\ZI[q^{2}]^{2}_{t}\right)h^{2}-\frac{12}{\sqrt{2}}g_{t}m_{t}\ZI^{1}_{t}h.

(8)

Introducing the canonically normalised field
\hat{h}
which is related to
h

through

\displaystyle\hat{h}=\left(1+\frac{1}{2}\delta Z_{h}\right)h,

(9)

one can read off
\delta Z_{h}
to be

\displaystyle\delta Z_{h}=-6g_{t}^{2}\left(m_{t}^{4}\ZI^{4}_{t}-2d\ZI[q^{4}]^{4}_{t}-4\ZI[q^{4}]^{4}_{t}\right)=-6g_{t}^{2}\left(m_{t}^{4}\ZI^{4}_{t}-12\ZI[q^{4}]^{4}_{t}+\frac{1}{6}\right).

(10)

The loop functions that appear in Eqs. (
8
) and
(
10
) can be calculated using Eqs. (
2
)-(
4
) and are given by

\displaystyle\ZI^{1}_{t}

\displaystyle=2\ZI[q^{2}]^{2}_{t}=m_{t}^{2}\left(\frac{2}{\eps}+1-\log\frac{m_{t}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right),

(11)

\displaystyle\ZI^{2}_{t}

\displaystyle=24\ZI[q^{4}]^{4}_{t}=\frac{2}{\eps}-\log\frac{m_{t}^{2}}{\mu^{2}},

(12)

\displaystyle\ZI^{4}_{t}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{6m_{t}^{4}}.

(13)

2
MSSM threshold correction to the quartic Higgs coupling

In this section we reproduce the one-loop threshold
correction of the quartic Higgs coupling
\lambda
when matching the MSSM to the
SM at the one-loop level in the unbroken phase. This result was first reported in Ref.
[
105
]
and as
discussed in that reference there are several contributions
of distinct origins. The scalar contribution

\Delta\lambda^{1\Loop,\phi}
arises from interactions of the SM-like
Higgs with heavy Higgs bosons, squarks and sleptons, and the relevant
interaction Lagrangian is given by

\displaystyle\LagNoT_{\phi}={}

\displaystyle-\frac{g_{t}^{2}}{2}h^{2}(\st{L}^{*}\st{L}+\st{R}^{*}\st{R})-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}g_{t}X_{t}h(\st{L}^{*}\st{R}+\st{L}\st{R}^{*})

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{8}c_{2\beta}h^{2}\left[\left(g_{2}^{2}-\frac{g_{1}^{2}}{5}\right)\su^{*}_{Li}\su_{Li}+\frac{4}{5}g_{1}^{2}\su_{Ri}^{*}\su_{Ri}-\left(g_{2}^{2}+\frac{g_{1}^{2}}{5}\right)\sd_{Li}^{*}\sd_{Li}-\frac{2}{5}g_{1}^{2}\sd_{Ri}^{*}\sd_{Ri}\right]

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{8}c_{2\beta}h^{2}\left[\left(g_{2}^{2}+\frac{3}{5}g_{1}^{2}\right)\sneu^{*}_{Li}\sneu_{Li}-\left(g_{2}^{2}-\frac{3}{5}g_{1}^{2}\right)\sel_{Li}^{*}\sel_{Li}-\frac{6}{5}g_{1}^{2}\sel_{Ri}^{*}\sel_{Ri}\right]

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{16}c_{2\beta}^{2}\left(\frac{3}{5}g_{1}^{2}+g_{2}^{2}\right)h^{2}A^{2}-\frac{1}{8}\left((1+s_{2\beta}^{2})g_{2}^{2}-\frac{3}{5}g_{1}^{2}c_{2\beta}^{2}\right)h^{2}H^{-}H^{+}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{16}\left(\frac{3}{5}g_{1}^{2}+g_{2}^{2}\right)(3s_{2\beta}^{2}-1)h^{2}H^{2}-\frac{1}{8}\left(\frac{3}{5}g_{1}^{2}+g_{2}^{2}\right)s_{2\beta}c_{2\beta}h^{3}H

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{8}\left(\frac{3}{5}g_{1}^{2}+g_{2}^{2}\right)s_{2\beta}c_{2\beta}h^{2}(G^{-}H^{+}+H^{-}G^{+})+\frac{1}{8}\left(\frac{3}{5}g_{1}^{2}+g_{2}^{2}\right)s_{2\beta}c_{2\beta}h^{2}G^{0}A.

(14)

Here the three generations
of left- and right-handed squarks are denoted as

\su_{Li}
,
\su_{Ri}
,
\sd_{Li}
,
\sd_{Ri}
, where

\st{L}\equiv\su_{L3}
and
\st{R}\equiv\su_{R3}
are the left-
and right-handed stops. Correspondingly, the three generations of left- and right-handed sleptons are denoted as
\sel_{Li}
,

\sel_{Ri}
,
\sneu_{Li}
. The couplings
g_{1}
and
g_{2}
are the GUT-normalised electroweak gauge
couplings,
X_{t}
is the stop mixing parameter, and
g_{t}=y_{t}s_{\beta}

with
y_{t}
being the MSSM top Yukawa coupling and
s_{\beta}=\sin(\beta)
. The angle
\beta
should not be
regarded as a ratio of vacuum expectation values, but as the
fine-tuned mixing angle which rotates the two MSSM Higgs doublets,

H_{u}
and
H_{d}
, into two new doublets,
\mathcal{H}
and
\mathcal{A}
, with

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}

\displaystyle=-c_{\beta}\epstensor H^{*}_{d}+s_{\beta}H_{u},

(15)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}

\displaystyle=s_{\beta}\epstensor H^{*}_{d}+c_{\beta}H_{u},

(16)

since we are working in the unbroken phase. See Ref.
[
105
]
for details. In Eqs. (
15
) and (
16
)
\epstensor
is the antisymmetric tensor with
\epstensor_{12}=1
and
c_{\beta}=\cos(\beta)
,
s_{2\beta}=\sin(2\beta)
and
c_{2\beta}=\cos(2\beta)
. The SM-like Higgs doublet
\mathcal{H}
gives rise to the SM-like physical Higgs field
h=\sqrt{2}\,\Re(\mathcal{H}^{0})
, where
\mathcal{H}^{0}
is the neutral component of
\mathcal{H}
and
\Re(\mathcal{H}^{0})
its real part. The fields
G^{0}
and
G^{\pm}
are Goldstone bosons arising from the same Higgs doublet. The heavy Higgs
bosons of the MSSM,
H
,
A
and
H^{\pm}
, originate from the heavy doublet

\mathcal{A}
.

The fermionic contribution
\Delta\lambda^{1\Loop,\chi}
to the
threshold correction of
\lambda
originates from interactions of the
Higgs boson with charginos
\tilde{\chi}^{+}_{i}
(
i=1,2
) and
neutralinos
\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{i}
(
i=1,\ldots,4
) described by the
interaction Lagrangian

\displaystyle\LagNoT_{\chi}={}

\displaystyle-\frac{g_{2}}{\sqrt{2}}hc_{\beta}(\overline{\tilde{\chi}^{+}_{1}}P_{R}\tilde{\chi}^{+}_{2}+\overline{\tilde{\chi}^{+}_{2}}P_{L}\tilde{\chi}^{+}_{1})-\frac{g_{2}}{\sqrt{2}}hs_{\beta}(\overline{\tilde{\chi}^{+}_{2}}P_{R}\tilde{\chi}^{+}_{1}+\overline{\tilde{\chi}^{+}_{1}}P_{L}\tilde{\chi}^{+}_{2})

\displaystyle+i\frac{g_{Y}}{2\sqrt{2}}(c_{\beta}-s_{\beta})h\overline{\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}}\gamma^{5}\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{3}-\frac{g_{Y}}{2\sqrt{2}}(c_{\beta}+s_{\beta})h\overline{\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}}\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{4}

\displaystyle-i\frac{g_{2}}{2\sqrt{2}}(c_{\beta}-s_{\beta})h\overline{\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{2}}\gamma^{5}\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{3}+\frac{g_{2}}{2\sqrt{2}}(c_{\beta}+s_{\beta})h\overline{\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{2}}\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{4},

(17)

where
\overline{\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{i}}=(\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{i})^{T}\cc
and

g_{Y}=\sqrt{3/5}\,g_{1}
.

To calculate the one-loop threshold correction for
\lambda
, the
following contributions with purely scalar and purely fermionic
operators of the BSUOLEA are relevant,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\kappa}\Lag{EFT}^{1\Loop}=\tr\Bigg\{

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\ZI^{1}_{i}(\mathbf{U}_{\Phi\Phi})_{ii}+\frac{1}{2}\ZI[q^{2}]^{22}_{ij}[P_{\mu},(\mathbf{U}_{\Phi\Phi})_{ij}][P^{\mu},(\mathbf{U}_{\Phi\Phi})_{ji}]

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{4}\ZI^{11}_{ij}(\mathbf{U}_{\Phi\Phi})_{ij}(\mathbf{U}_{\Phi\Phi})_{ji}+\frac{1}{6}\ZI^{111}_{ijk}(\mathbf{U}_{\Phi\Phi})_{ij}(\mathbf{U}_{\Phi\Phi})_{jk}(\mathbf{U}_{\Phi\Phi})_{ki}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{8}\ZI^{1111}_{ijkl}(\mathbf{U}_{\Phi\Phi})_{ij}(\mathbf{U}_{\Phi\Phi})_{jk}(\mathbf{U}_{\Phi\Phi})_{kl}(\mathbf{U}_{\Phi\Phi})_{li}+\frac{1}{2}\ZI^{1}_{i}(\mathbf{U}_{\Phi\phi})_{ij}(\mathbf{U}_{\phi\Phi})_{ji}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{8}M_{\Xi i}M_{\Xi j}M_{\Xi k}M_{\Xi l}\ZI^{1111}_{ijkl}(\mathbf{U}_{\Xi\Xi})_{ij}(\mathbf{U}_{\Xi\Xi})_{jk}(\mathbf{U}_{\Xi\Xi})_{kl}(\mathbf{U}_{\Xi\Xi})_{li}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}M_{\Xi i}M_{\Xi j}\ZI[q^{2}]^{1111}_{ijkl}(\mathbf{U}_{\Xi\Xi})_{ij}(\mathbf{U}_{\Xi\Xi})_{jk}\gamma^{\mu}(\mathbf{U}_{\Xi\Xi})_{kl}\gamma_{\mu}(\mathbf{U}_{\Xi\Xi})_{li}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4}M_{\Xi i}M_{\Xi k}\ZI[q^{2}]^{1111}_{ijkl}(\mathbf{U}_{\Xi\Xi})_{ij}\gamma^{\mu}(\mathbf{U}_{\Xi\Xi})_{jk}(\mathbf{U}_{\Xi\Xi})_{kl}\gamma_{\mu}(\mathbf{U}_{\Xi\Xi})_{li}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{8}g_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\ZI[q^{4}]^{1111}_{ijkl}\gamma^{\mu}(\mathbf{U}_{\Xi\Xi})_{ij}\gamma^{\nu}(\mathbf{U}_{\Xi\Xi})_{jk}\gamma^{\rho}(\mathbf{U}_{\Xi\Xi})_{kl}\gamma^{\sigma}(\mathbf{U}_{\Xi\Xi})_{li}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{4}M_{\Xi i}M_{\Xi j}^{3}\ZI^{13}_{ij}[P_{\mu},(\mathbf{U}_{\Xi\Xi})_{ij}][P^{\mu},(\mathbf{U}_{\Xi\Xi})_{ji}]

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\ZI[q^{4}]^{22}_{ij}\gamma^{\nu}[P_{\mu},(\mathbf{U}_{\Xi\Xi})_{ij}]\gamma_{\nu}[P^{\mu},(\mathbf{U}_{\Xi\Xi})_{ji}]

\displaystyle-\ZI[q^{4}]^{22}_{ij}\gamma^{\nu}[P_{\nu},(\mathbf{U}_{\Xi\Xi})_{ij}]\gamma_{\mu}[P^{\mu},(\mathbf{U}_{\Xi\Xi})_{ji}]\Bigg\}.

(18)

The operators containing covariant
derivatives are again included to obtain the field-redefinition of the
Higgs field, which propagates into every Higgs coupling that has a
non-vanishing tree-level contribution once the Higgs field is cannonically normalised in the EFT. Therefore this field-redefinition contributes to the threshold correction of the quartic
coupling.

Next, we compute the derivative matrices as defined in Appendix
7
. We start with

\displaystyle\mathbf{U}_{\Phi\Phi}=\begin{pmatrix}U_{\Sigma^{*}\Sigma}&&U_{\Sigma^{*}\Sigma^{*}}&&U_{\Sigma^{*}\Theta}\\
U_{\Sigma\Sigma}&&U_{\Sigma\Sigma^{*}}&&U_{\Sigma\Theta}\\
U_{\Theta\Sigma}&&U_{\Theta\Sigma^{*}}&&U_{\Theta\Theta}\end{pmatrix}

(19)

and define

\displaystyle\Sigma=\begin{pmatrix}\su_{Li}&\su_{Ri}&\sd_{Li}&\sd_{Ri}&\sel_{Li}&\sel_{Ri}&\sneu_{Li}&H^{+}\end{pmatrix}^{T},\text{ }\Theta=\begin{pmatrix}A&H\end{pmatrix}^{T},

(20)

with the corresponding masses

\displaystyle M_{\Sigma}=\begin{pmatrix}m_{\sq_{i}}&m_{\su_{i}}&m_{\sq_{i}}&m_{\sd_{i}}&m_{\slep_{i}}&m_{\sel_{i}}&m_{\sneu_{i}}&m_{A}\end{pmatrix}^{T},\text{ }M_{\Theta}=\begin{pmatrix}m_{A}&m_{A}\end{pmatrix}^{T},

(21)

where we did not include the derivatives w.r.t. vector bosons since there are no heavy vector bosons in the theory. The non-vanishing
derivatives with respect to two heavy scalar fields read

\displaystyle U_{\su_{Li}^{*}\su_{Lj}}

\displaystyle=U_{\su_{Li}\su_{Lj}^{*}}=\frac{1}{8}c_{2\beta}h^{2}\delta_{ij}\left(g_{2}^{2}-\frac{1}{5}g_{1}^{2}\right)+\delta_{3i}\delta_{3j}\frac{g_{t}^{2}}{2}h^{2},

(22)

\displaystyle U_{\su_{Ri}^{*}\su_{Rj}}

\displaystyle=U_{\su_{Ri}\su_{Rj}^{*}}=\frac{1}{10}c_{2\beta}h^{2}\delta_{ij}g_{1}^{2}+\delta_{3i}\delta_{3j}\frac{g_{t}^{2}}{2}h^{2},

(23)

\displaystyle U_{\sd_{Li}^{*}\sd_{Lj}}

\displaystyle=U_{\sd_{Li}\sd_{Lj}^{*}}=-\frac{1}{8}c_{2\beta}h^{2}\delta_{ij}\left(g_{2}^{2}+\frac{1}{5}g_{1}^{2}\right),

(24)

\displaystyle U_{\sd_{Ri}^{*}\sd_{Rj}}

\displaystyle=U_{\sd_{Ri}\sd_{Rj}^{*}}=\frac{1}{20}c_{2\beta}h^{2}\delta_{ij}g_{1}^{2},

(25)

\displaystyle U_{\sel_{Li}^{*}\sel_{Lj}}

\displaystyle=U_{\sel_{Li}\sel_{Lj}^{*}}=\frac{1}{8}c_{2\beta}h^{2}\delta_{ij}\left(g_{2}^{2}-\frac{3}{5}g_{1}^{2}\right),

(26)

\displaystyle U_{\sel_{Ri}^{*}\sel_{Rj}}

\displaystyle=U_{\sel_{Ri}\sel_{Rj}^{*}}=-\frac{1}{20}c_{2\beta}h^{2}\delta_{ij}g_{1}^{2},

(27)

\displaystyle U_{\sneu_{Li}^{*}\sneu_{Lj}}

\displaystyle=U_{\sneu_{Li}\sneu_{Lj}^{*}}=\frac{1}{8}c_{2\beta}h^{2}\delta_{ij}\left(g_{2}^{2}+\frac{3}{5}g_{1}^{2}\right),

(28)

\displaystyle U_{H^{+}H^{-}}

\displaystyle=U_{H^{-}H^{+}}=\frac{1}{8}h^{2}\left[(1+s_{2\beta}^{2})g_{2}^{2}-\frac{3}{5}g_{1}^{2}c_{2\beta}^{2}\right],

(29)

\displaystyle U_{AA}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{16}c_{2\beta}^{2}\left(\frac{3}{5}g_{1}^{2}+g_{2}^{2}\right)h^{2},

(30)

\displaystyle U_{HH}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{16}(2s_{2\beta}^{2}-1)\left(\frac{3}{5}g_{1}^{2}+g_{2}^{2}\right)h^{2},

(31)

\displaystyle U_{\su_{Li}^{*}\su_{Rj}}

\displaystyle=U_{\su_{Li}\su_{Rj}^{*}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\delta_{3i}\delta_{3j}g_{t}X_{t}h.

(32)

Given these derivatives we find that
\mathbf{U}_{\Phi\Phi}
is
block-diagonal, where the blocks are given by

\displaystyle U_{\Sigma^{*}\Sigma}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}U_{\su_{Li}^{*}\su_{Lj}}&U_{\su_{Li}^{*}\su_{Rj}}&\mathbf{0}_{1\times 6}\\
U_{\su_{Ri}^{*}\su_{Lj}}&U_{\su_{Ri}^{*}\su_{Rj}}&\mathbf{0}_{1\times 6}\\
\mathbf{0}_{6\times 1}&\mathbf{0}_{6\times 1}&U_{\Pi^{*}\Pi}\end{pmatrix},

(33)

\displaystyle U_{\Pi^{*}\Pi}

\displaystyle=\diag(U_{\sd_{Li}^{*}\sd_{Lj}},U_{\sd_{Ri}^{*}\sd_{Rj}},U_{\sel_{Li}^{*}\sel_{Lj}},U_{\sel_{Ri}^{*}\sel_{Rj}},U_{\sneu_{Li}^{*}\sneu_{Lj}},U_{H^{+}H^{-}})\,,

(34)

\displaystyle U_{\Sigma\Sigma^{*}}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}U_{\su_{Li}\su_{Lj}^{*}}&U_{\su_{Li}\su_{Rj}^{*}}&\mathbf{0}_{1\times 6}\\
U_{\su_{Ri}\su_{Lj}^{*}}&U_{\su_{Ri}\su_{Rj}^{*}}&\mathbf{0}_{1\times 6}\\
\mathbf{0}_{6\times 1}&\mathbf{0}_{6\times 1}&U_{\Pi\Pi^{*}}\end{pmatrix},

(35)

\displaystyle U_{\Pi\Pi^{*}}

\displaystyle=\diag(U_{\sd_{Li}\sd_{Lj}^{*}},U_{\sd_{Ri}\sd_{Rj}^{*}},U_{\sel_{Li}\sel_{Lj}^{*}},U_{\sel_{Ri}\sel_{Rj}^{*}},U_{\sneu_{Li}\sneu_{Lj}^{*}},U_{H^{-}H^{+}})\,,

(36)

\displaystyle U_{\Theta\Theta}

\displaystyle=\diag(U_{AA},U_{HH})\,,

(37)

with
\mathbf{0}_{m\times n}
being the
m\times n
matrix of only
zeros. We next calculate
\mathbf{U}_{\phi\Phi}
and

\mathbf{U}_{\Phi\phi}
, which contain derivatives with respect to one
heavy and one light scalar field. As discussed in Chapter
1
the derivatives w.r.t. the fields
are evaluated at the background field configurations, and the heavy
background fields are expressed in terms of the light ones using a
local operator expansion. In the case under consideration here the heavy background fields are
suppressed by at least
1/M^{2}
and since we are not interested in these
suppressed contributions, we set the heavy background fields to zero. Defining the light scalar field
multiplets as

\displaystyle\sigma=(G^{+})\,,\text{ }\theta=\begin{pmatrix}h&G^{0}\end{pmatrix}^{T},

(38)

the non-vanishing derivatives are found to be

\displaystyle U_{Hh}

\displaystyle=U_{hH}=\frac{3}{8}\left(\frac{3}{5}g_{1}^{2}+g_{2}^{2}\right)s_{2\beta}c_{2\beta}h^{2},

(39)

\displaystyle U_{AG^{0}}

\displaystyle=U_{G^{0}A}=-\frac{1}{8}\left(\frac{3}{5}g_{1}^{2}+g_{2}^{2}\right)s_{2\beta}c_{2\beta}h^{2},

(40)

\displaystyle U_{H^{+}G^{-}}

\displaystyle=U_{H^{-}G^{+}}=-\frac{1}{8}\left(\frac{3}{5}g_{1}^{2}+g_{2}^{2}\right)s_{2\beta}c_{2\beta}h^{2}.

(41)

We then find that
\mathbf{U}_{\Phi\phi}
is block-diagonal with the blocks being

\displaystyle U_{\Sigma^{*}\sigma}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{0}_{7\times 1}\\
U_{H^{-}G^{+}}\end{pmatrix},

(42)

\displaystyle U_{\Sigma\sigma^{*}}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{0}_{7\times 1}\\
U_{H^{+}G^{-}}\end{pmatrix},

(43)

\displaystyle U_{\Theta\theta}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}0&U_{AG^{0}}\\
U_{Hh}&0\end{pmatrix}.

(44)

Similarly,
\mathbf{U}_{\phi\Phi}
is block-diagonal where the blocks are given by

\displaystyle U_{\sigma^{*}\Sigma}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{0}_{1\times 7}&U_{G^{-}H^{+}}\end{pmatrix},

(45)

\displaystyle U_{\sigma\Sigma^{*}}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{0}_{1\times 7}&U_{G^{+}H^{-}}\end{pmatrix},

(46)

\displaystyle U_{\theta\Theta}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}0&U_{hH}\\
U_{G^{0}A}&0\end{pmatrix}.

(47)

Finally, we need the derivatives with respect to two heavy fermions to
construct the matrix
\mathbf{U}_{\Xi\Xi}
. Both Dirac and Majorana fermions are present and hence we define

\displaystyle\Omega=\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{\chi}^{+}_{1}&\tilde{\chi}^{+}_{2}\end{pmatrix}^{T},\text{ }\Lambda=\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}&\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{2}&\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{3}&\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{4}\end{pmatrix}^{T},

(48)

with the corresponding masses
M_{\Xi}=(M_{2}\;\mu\;M_{1}\;M_{2}\;\mu\;\mu)^{T}
,
and find that the matrix
\mathbf{U}_{\Xi\Xi}
is also block-diagonal with the
non-vanishing entries

\displaystyle U_{\bar{\Omega}\Omega}

\displaystyle=\cc^{-1}U^{T}_{\Omega\bar{\Omega}}\cc^{-1}=-\frac{g_{2}}{\sqrt{2}}h\begin{pmatrix}0&c_{\beta}P_{R}+s_{\beta}P_{L}\\
c_{\beta}P_{L}+s_{\beta}P_{R}&0\end{pmatrix},

(49)

\displaystyle\cc^{-1}U_{\Lambda\Lambda}

\displaystyle=\frac{h}{2\sqrt{2}}\begin{pmatrix}0&0&ig_{Y}(c_{\beta}-s_{\beta})\gamma^{5}&-g_{Y}(c_{\beta}+s_{\beta})\\
0&0&-ig_{2}(c_{\beta}-s_{\beta})\gamma^{5}&g_{2}(c_{\beta}+s_{\beta})\\
ig_{Y}(c_{\beta}-s_{\beta})\gamma^{5}&-ig_{2}(c_{\beta}-s_{\beta})\gamma^{5}&0&0\\
-g_{Y}(c_{\beta}+s_{\beta})&g_{2}(c_{\beta}+s_{\beta})&0&0\end{pmatrix}.

(50)

Inserting all of these derivatives into Eq. (
18
) and summing over all indices we find the following field-redefinition of the SM-like Higgs boson

\displaystyle h={}

\displaystyle\left(1-\frac{1}{2}\delta Z_{h}\right)\hat{h},

(51)

\displaystyle\delta Z_{h}={}

\displaystyle-6g_{t}^{2}X_{t}^{2}\ZI[q^{2}]_{\sq\su}^{22}+\frac{s_{2\beta}}{2}\mu\left(g^{2}_{Y}M_{1}\mu^{2}\ZI^{13}_{1\mu}+g^{2}_{Y}M_{1}^{3}\ZI^{31}_{1\mu}-3g^{2}_{2}M_{2}\mu^{2}\ZI^{13}_{2\mu}-3g^{2}_{2}M_{2}^{3}\ZI^{31}_{2\mu}\right)

\displaystyle+2(2+d)\left(-g_{Y}^{2}\ZI[q^{4}]^{22}_{1\mu}+3g_{2}^{2}\ZI[q^{4}]^{22}_{2\mu}\right),

(52)

where the subscripts
1
and
2
of the loop functions are shorthand for
M_{1}
and
M_{2}
, respectively. After canonically normalising the Higgs boson in the EFT the effective Lagrangian reads

\displaystyle\Lag{EFT}^{1\Loop}=\frac{1}{2}(\partial\hat{h})^{2}-\frac{\lambda}{8}\hat{h}^{4}+\cdots

(53)

with

\displaystyle\lambda

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\left(\frac{3}{5}g_{1}^{2}+g_{2}^{2}\right)c_{2\beta}^{2}+\kappa\Delta\lambda^{1\Loop},

(54)

\displaystyle\Delta\lambda^{1\Loop}

\displaystyle=\Delta\lambda^{1\Loop,\text{reg}}+\Delta\lambda^{1\Loop,\phi}+\Delta\lambda^{1\Loop,\chi},

(55)

and

\displaystyle\Delta\lambda^{1\Loop,\phi}={}

\displaystyle g_{t}^{4}\left[-3X_{t}^{4}\ZI_{\sq\sq\su\su}^{1111}-6X_{t}^{2}\left(\ZI_{\sq\sq\su}^{111}+\ZI_{\sq\su\su}^{111}\right)-3\left(\ZI_{\sq\sq}^{11}+\ZI_{\su\su}^{11}\right)\right]

\displaystyle+\frac{3}{10}g_{t}^{2}c_{2\beta}\Big\{X_{t}^{2}\left[2c_{2\beta}\left(3g_{1}^{2}+5g_{2}^{2}\right)\ZI[q^{2}]_{\sq\su}^{22}+\left(g_{1}^{2}-5g_{2}^{2}\right)\ZI_{\sq\sq\su}^{111}-4g_{1}^{2}\ZI_{\sq\su\su}^{111}\right]

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~+\left(g_{1}^{2}-5g_{2}^{2}\right)\ZI_{\sq\sq}^{11}-4g_{1}^{2}\ZI_{\su\su}^{11}\Big\}

\displaystyle-\frac{c_{2\beta}^{2}}{200}\sum_{i=1}^{3}\Big[3\left(g_{1}^{4}+25g_{2}^{4}\right)\ZI_{\sq_{i}\sq_{i}}^{11}+24g_{1}^{4}\ZI_{\su_{i}\su_{i}}^{11}+6g_{1}^{4}\ZI_{\sd_{i}\sd_{i}}^{11}

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~+\left(9g_{1}^{4}+25g_{2}^{4}\right)\ZI_{\slep_{i}\slep_{i}}^{11}+18g_{1}^{4}\ZI_{\sel_{i}\sel_{i}}^{11}\Big]

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{200}\Big\{6c_{2\beta}^{2}\left(c_{2\beta}^{2}-1\right)\left(3g_{1}^{2}+5g_{2}^{2}\right)^{2}\ZI_{A0}^{11}-\Big[9\left(3c_{2\beta}^{4}-3c_{2\beta}^{2}+1\right)g_{1}^{4}

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~+30\left(3c_{2\beta}^{4}-4c_{2\beta}^{2}+1\right)g_{1}^{2}g_{2}^{2}+25\left(3c_{2\beta}^{4}-5c_{2\beta}^{2}+3\right)g_{2}^{4}\Big]\ZI_{AA}^{11}\Big\},

(56)

\displaystyle\Delta\lambda^{1\Loop,\chi}={}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4}\Big\{-d\big(2g_{Y}^{4}M_{1}^{2}\ZI[q^{2}]^{22}_{1\mu}+2g_{2}^{4}M_{2}^{2}\ZI[q^{2}]^{22}_{2\mu}+g_{Y}^{4}\mu^{2}\ZI[q^{2}]^{22}_{1\mu}

\displaystyle\qquad~~~~~~~~-g_{Y}^{4}\mu^{2}c_{4\beta}\ZI[q^{2}]^{22}_{1\mu}+g_{2}^{4}\mu^{2}\ZI[q^{2}]^{22}_{2\mu}-g_{2}^{4}\mu^{2}c_{4\beta}\ZI[q^{2}]^{22}_{2\mu}

\displaystyle\qquad~~~~~~~~+4g_{Y}^{2}g_{2}^{2}M_{1}M_{2}\ZI[q^{2}]^{112}_{12\mu}+2g_{Y}^{2}g_{2}^{2}\mu^{2}\ZI[q^{2}]^{112}_{12\mu}-2g_{Y}^{2}g_{2}^{2}\mu^{2}c_{4\beta}\ZI[q^{2}]^{112}_{12\mu}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad~~-d(2+d)\big(2g_{Y}^{4}\ZI[q^{4}]^{22}_{1\mu}+2g_{2}^{4}\ZI[q^{4}]^{22}_{2\mu}+4g_{Y}^{2}g_{2}^{2}\ZI[q^{4}]^{112}_{12\mu}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad~~-g_{2}^{4}\big[2d(2+d)(3+c_{4\beta})\ZI[q^{4}]^{22}_{2\mu}+16c_{\beta}s_{\beta}(dM_{2}\ZI[q^{2}]^{22}_{2\mu}(\mu+M_{2}c_{\beta}s_{\beta})

\displaystyle\qquad~~~~~~~~~+\mu\{M_{2}^{2}\mu c_{\beta}\ZI^{22}_{2\mu}s_{\beta}+d\ZI[q^{2}]^{22}_{2\mu}(M_{2}+\mu c_{\beta}s_{\beta})\})\big]

\displaystyle\qquad~~-4d\mu\big(2g_{Y}^{4}M_{1}\ZI[q^{2}]^{22}_{1\mu}+2g_{2}^{4}M_{2}\ZI[q^{2}]^{22}_{2\mu}+2g_{Y}^{2}g_{2}^{2}M_{1}\ZI[q^{2}]^{112}_{12\mu}

\displaystyle\qquad~~~~~~~~~~~+2g_{Y}^{2}g_{2}^{2}M_{2}\ZI[q^{2}]^{112}_{12\mu}\big)s_{2\beta}

\displaystyle\qquad~~-2\mu^{2}\big(g_{Y}^{4}M_{1}^{2}\ZI^{22}_{1\mu}+g_{2}^{2}M_{2}(g_{2}^{2}M_{2}\ZI^{22}_{2\mu}+g_{Y}^{2}M_{1}2\ZI^{112}_{12\mu}+)\big)s_{2\beta}^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad~~-2g_{2}^{2}(g_{Y}^{2}+g_{2}^{2})c_{2\beta}^{2}\big(-4(2+d)\ZI[q^{4}]^{22}_{2\mu}+M_{2}\mu(\mu^{2}\ZI^{13}_{2\mu}+M_{2}^{2}\ZI^{31}_{2\mu})s_{2\beta}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad~~-(g_{Y}^{2}+g_{2}^{2})c_{2\beta}^{2}\big(-4(2+d)g_{Y}^{2}\ZI[q^{4}]^{22}_{1\mu}-4(2+d)g_{2}^{2}\ZI[q^{4}]^{22}_{2\mu}

\displaystyle\qquad~~~~~~+\mu\{g_{Y}^{2}M_{1}\mu^{2}\ZI^{13}_{1\mu}+g_{Y}^{2}M_{1}^{3}\ZI^{31}_{1\mu}+g_{2}^{2}M_{2}(\mu^{2}\ZI^{31}_{2\mu}+M_{2}^{2}\ZI^{31}_{2\mu})\}s_{2\beta}\big)\Big\}.

(57)

Here
\lambda
is expressed entirely in terms of the MSSM gauge couplings,
in contrast to Ref.
[
105
]
and we use the notation
m_{\sq}\equiv m_{\sq_{3}}
as well as
m_{\su}\equiv m_{\su_{3}}
.

It is sensible to regularise the MSSM using
DRED, whereas the SM is more naturally
regularised in DREG. The change in regularisation leads to further contributions to
the threshold correction denoted by
\Delta\lambda^{1\Loop,\text{reg}}
, which can be obtained using the
DRED–DREG translating operators presented in Section
4
. In this case the contribution originates from the operator

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\kappa}\eps\LagNoT_{\EFT,\eps}^{1\Loop}=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{ij}(\check{\mathbf{U}}^{\mu}_{\epsilon\epsilon\nu})_{ij}(\check{\mathbf{U}}^{\nu}_{\epsilon\epsilon\mu})_{ji},

(58)

of Eq. (
185
), where in the MSSM we have the following couplings of epsilon scalars to the
SM-like doublet
\mathcal{H}
,

\displaystyle\LagNoT_{\eps\mathcal{H}}=\mathcal{H}^{*}_{a}\epsdim{g}_{\mu\nu}\left(g_{2}^{2}T^{A}_{ab}T^{B}_{bc}\epsdim{a}^{A\mu}\epsdim{a}^{B\nu}+\sqrt{\frac{3}{5}}g_{1}g_{2}T^{A}_{ac}\epsdim{a}^{A\mu}\epsdim{b}^{\nu}+\frac{3}{20}g_{1}^{2}\epsdim{b}^{\mu}\epsdim{b}^{\nu}\delta_{ac}\right)\mathcal{H}_{c}.

(59)

The fields

\epsdim{a}^{A\mu}
and
\epsdim{b}^{\mu}
denote the epsilon scalars
corresponding to
SU(2)_{L}
and
U(1)_{Y}
, respectively, and the matrices
T^{A}
are the generators of the fundamental representation of
SU(2)_{L}
. From this Lagrangian the matrix
\epsdim{\mathbf{U}}^{\mu\nu}_{\eps\eps}
is calculated to be

\displaystyle\epsdim{\mathbf{U}}^{\mu\nu}_{\eps\eps}=-\epsdim{g}^{\mu\nu}\begin{pmatrix}\mathcal{H}^{*}_{a}g_{2}^{2}\{T^{A},T^{B}\}_{ac}\mathcal{H}_{c}&\sqrt{\frac{3}{5}}g_{1}g_{2}\mathcal{H}^{*}_{a}T^{A}_{ac}\mathcal{H}_{c}\\
\sqrt{\frac{3}{5}}g_{1}g_{2}\mathcal{H}^{*}_{a}T^{A}_{ac}\mathcal{H}_{c}&\frac{3}{10}g_{1}^{2}\mathcal{H}^{*}_{a}\mathcal{H}_{a}\end{pmatrix},

(60)

and inserting this into Eq. (
58
) we obtain

\displaystyle\Delta\lambda^{1\Loop,\text{reg}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{9}{100}g_{1}^{4}-\frac{3}{10}g_{1}^{2}g_{2}^{2}-\frac{3}{4}g_{2}^{4}.

(61)

We do not find the term proportional to
c_{2\beta}^{2}
given in Ref.
[
105
]
since this term only arises once the
tree-level expression for
\lambda
is expressed in terms of SM gauge
couplings. Up to terms
arising from this conversion the one-loop threshold corrections presented here agree
with the results of Ref.
[
105
]
.

3
Integrating out stops and the gluino from the MSSM

In this section we reproduce known threshold corrections from
the MSSM to the SMEFT from
heavy stops and the gluino in the gaugeless limit (
g_{1}=g_{2}=0
). We work in
the unbroken phase and set all Yukawa couplings to zero, except for the
one of the top quark. In this application contributions from BSUOLEA operators with mixed statistics arise and the BSUOLEA must be carefully applied.

We consider the following part of the MSSM Lagrangian

\displaystyle\begin{split}\Lag{MSSM}\supset{}&|\partial\st{L}|^{2}-\mstL|\st{L}|^{2}+|\partial\st{R}|^{2}-\mstR|\st{R}|^{2}+\frac{1}{2}(\gluino{\dot{A}})^{T}\cc(i\not{\partial}-m_{\gluino{}})\gluino{\dot{A}}\\
&-\frac{y_{t}s_{\beta}}{\sqrt{2}}h\bar{t}t-\frac{y_{t}^{2}s_{\beta}^{2}}{2}h^{2}\left(|\st{L}|^{2}+|\st{R}|^{2}\right)-\frac{y_{t}s_{\beta}X_{t}}{\sqrt{2}}h\left(\st{L}^{*}\st{R}+\hc\right)\\
&-\sqrt{2}g_{3}\left[\bar{t}P_{R}\gluino{\dot{A}}T^{\dot{A}}\st{L}-\bar{t}P_{L}\gluino{\dot{A}}T^{\dot{A}}\st{R}+\st{L}^{*}(\gluino{\dot{A}})^{T}T^{\dot{A}}\cc P_{L}t-\st{R}^{*}(\gluino{\dot{A}})^{T}T^{\dot{A}}\cc P_{R}t\right],\end{split}

(62)

where we use the same notation as in the previous section,
g_{3}

is the strong gauge coupling and
T^{\dot{A}}
are the generators of the fundamental representation of
SU(3)_{c}
. The top
quark,
t
, is defined as a Dirac fermion built from
the upper component of the left-handed quark-doublet
q_{L}
and the
right-handed top
t_{R}
. In Eq. (
62
) it was used that the gluino,
\gluino{\dot{A}}
, is a Majorana spinor which satisfies
\overline{\gluino{\dot{A}}}=(\ccfield{(\gluino{\dot{A}})})^{T}\cc=(\gluino{\dot{A}})^{T}\cc
.

After the heavy stops and the gluino have been integrated out the Lagrangian of
the effective theory reads

\displaystyle\Lag{SMEFT}\supset-\frac{y_{t}s_{\beta}}{\sqrt{2}}h\bar{t}t+\Lag{SMEFT}^{\text{1\Loop}}.

(63)

In our limit the one-loop term
\Lag{SMEFT}^{\text{1\Loop}}
receives
contributions from the following BSUOLEA operators

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\kappa}\Lag{EFT}^{1\Loop}\supset{}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\ZI^{1}_{i}(\mathbf{U}_{\Phi\Phi})_{ii}+\frac{1}{4}\ZI^{11}_{ik}(\mathbf{U}_{\Phi\Phi})_{ik}(\mathbf{U}_{\Phi\Phi})_{ki}+\frac{1}{6}\ZI^{111}_{lik}(\mathbf{U}_{\Phi\Phi})_{ik}(\mathbf{U}_{\Phi\Phi})_{kl}(\mathbf{U}_{\Phi\Phi})_{li}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{8}\ZI^{1111}_{likn}(\mathbf{U}_{\Phi\Phi})_{ik}(\mathbf{U}_{\Phi\Phi})_{kl}(\mathbf{U}_{\Phi\Phi})_{ln}(\mathbf{U}_{\Phi\Phi})_{ni}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{10}\ZI^{11111}_{iklnp}(\mathbf{U}_{\Phi\Phi})_{ik}(\mathbf{U}_{\Phi\Phi})_{kl}(\mathbf{U}_{\Phi\Phi})_{ln}(\mathbf{U}_{\Phi\Phi})_{np}(\mathbf{U}_{\Phi\Phi})_{pi}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{12}\ZI^{111111}_{iklnpr}(\mathbf{U}_{\Phi\Phi})_{ik}(\mathbf{U}_{\Phi\Phi})_{kl}(\mathbf{U}_{\Phi\Phi})_{ln}(\mathbf{U}_{\Phi\Phi})_{np}(\mathbf{U}_{\Phi\Phi})_{pr}(\mathbf{U}_{\Phi\Phi})_{ri}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\ZI[q^{2}]^{22}_{ki}[P_{\mu},(\mathbf{U}_{\Phi\Phi})_{ik}][P^{\mu},(\mathbf{U}_{\Phi\Phi})_{ki}]

\displaystyle-\ZI[q^{2}]^{21}_{il}(\mathbf{U}_{\Phi\Xi})_{il}\gamma^{\mu}[P_{\mu},(\mathbf{U}_{\Xi\Phi})_{li}]

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}M_{\Xi_{k}}\ZI^{111}_{ikl}(\mathbf{U}_{\Phi\Xi})_{ik}(\mathbf{U}_{\Xi\Phi})_{kl}(\mathbf{U}_{\Phi\Phi})_{li},

(64)

where we set
P_{\mu}\equiv i\partial_{\mu}
and thus omit contributions from gauge
bosons. We identify
\Sigma=(\st{L},\st{R})
as
the heavy scalar multiplet and
\Lambda=(\gluino{\dot{A}})
as the multiplet of heavy Majorana fermions. From Eq. (
62
) we then obtain the following
non-vanishing derivatives

\displaystyle(U_{\st{L}^{*}\st{L}})_{IJ}

\displaystyle=(U_{\st{L}\st{L}^{*}})_{IJ}=(U_{\st{R}^{*}\st{R}})_{IJ}=(U_{\st{R}\st{R}^{*}})_{IJ}=\frac{1}{2}(y_{t}s_{\beta}h)^{2}\delta_{IJ},

(65)

\displaystyle(U_{\st{L}^{*}\st{R}})_{IJ}

\displaystyle=(U_{\st{L}\st{R}^{*}})_{IJ}=(U_{\st{R}^{*}\st{L}})_{IJ}=(U_{\st{R}\st{L}^{*}})_{IJ}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}y_{t}s_{\beta}hX_{t}\delta_{IJ},

(66)

\displaystyle(U_{\st{L}\gluino{\dot{A}}})_{I\dot{\alpha}}^{\dot{A}}

\displaystyle=(U_{\gluino{\dot{A}}\st{L}})_{I\dot{\alpha}}^{\dot{A}}=-\sqrt{2}g_{3}(\bar{t}_{J}P_{R})_{\dot{\alpha}}T^{\dot{A}}_{JI},

(67)

\displaystyle(U_{\st{R}\gluino{\dot{A}}})_{I\dot{\alpha}}^{\dot{A}}

\displaystyle=(U_{\gluino{\dot{A}}\st{R}})_{I\dot{\alpha}}^{\dot{A}}=\sqrt{2}g_{3}(\bar{t}_{J}P_{L})_{\dot{\alpha}}T^{\dot{A}}_{JI},

(68)

\displaystyle(U_{\gluino{\dot{A}}\st{L}^{*}})_{I\dot{\alpha}}^{\dot{A}}

\displaystyle=(U_{\st{L}^{*}\gluino{\dot{A}}})_{I\dot{\alpha}}^{\dot{A}}=\sqrt{2}g_{3}T^{\dot{A}}_{IJ}(\cc P_{L}t_{J})_{\dot{\alpha}},

(69)

\displaystyle(U_{\gluino{\dot{A}}\st{R}^{*}})_{I\dot{\alpha}}^{\dot{A}}

\displaystyle=(U_{\st{R}^{*}\gluino{A}})_{I\dot{\alpha}}^{\dot{A}}=-\sqrt{2}g_{3}T^{\dot{A}}_{IJ}(\cc P_{R}t_{J})_{\dot{\alpha}},

(70)

where the flipped
sign in Eqs. (
67
)–(
68
) is due to one
anti-commutation of the spinor
\bar{t}
with the derivative w.r.t. the spinor
\gluino{\dot{A}}
. The derivative matrices are given by

\displaystyle\mathbf{U}_{\Phi\Phi}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}U_{\Sigma^{*}\Sigma}&U_{\Sigma^{*}\Sigma^{*}}\\
U_{\Sigma\Sigma}&U_{\Sigma\Sigma^{*}}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}(U_{\st{L}^{*}\st{L}})_{IJ}&(U_{\st{L}^{*}\st{R}})_{IJ}&0&0\\
(U_{\st{R}^{*}\st{L}})_{IJ}&(U_{\st{R}^{*}\st{R}})_{IJ}&0&0\\
0&0&(U_{\st{L}\st{L}^{*}})_{IJ}&(U_{\st{L}\st{R}^{*}})_{IJ}\\
0&0&(U_{\st{R}\st{L}^{*}})_{IJ}&(U_{\st{R}\st{R}^{*}})_{IJ}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=\delta_{IJ}\;\mathbf{1}_{2}\otimes\begin{pmatrix}\frac{1}{2}(y_{t}s_{\beta}h)^{2}&\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}y_{t}s_{\beta}hX_{t}\\
\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}y_{t}s_{\beta}hX_{t}&\frac{1}{2}(y_{t}s_{\beta}h)^{2}\end{pmatrix},

(71)

\displaystyle\mathbf{U}_{\Phi\Xi}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}U_{\Sigma^{*}\Lambda}\\
U_{\Sigma\Lambda}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}(U_{\st{L}^{*}\gluino{\dot{A}}})_{I\dot{\alpha}}^{\dot{A}}\\
(U_{\st{R}^{*}\gluino{\dot{A}}})_{I\dot{\alpha}}^{\dot{A}}\\
(U_{\st{L}\gluino{\dot{A}}})_{I\dot{\alpha}}^{\dot{A}}\\
(U_{\st{R}\gluino{\dot{A}}})_{i\dot{\alpha}}^{\dot{A}}\end{pmatrix}=\sqrt{2}g_{3}\begin{pmatrix}T^{\dot{A}}_{IJ}(\cc P_{L}t_{J})_{\dot{\alpha}}\\
-T^{\dot{A}}_{IJ}(\cc P_{R}t_{J})_{\dot{\alpha}}\\
-(\bar{t}_{J}P_{R})_{\dot{\alpha}}T^{\dot{A}}_{JI}\\
(\bar{t}_{J}P_{L})_{\dot{\alpha}}T^{\dot{A}}_{JI}\end{pmatrix},

(72)

\displaystyle\mathbf{U}_{\Xi\Phi}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}\cc^{-1}U_{\Lambda\Sigma}&\cc^{-1}U_{\Lambda\Sigma^{*}}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=(\cc^{-1})_{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}}\begin{pmatrix}(U_{\gluino{\dot{A}}\st{L}})_{I\dot{\beta}}^{\dot{A}}&(U_{\gluino{\dot{A}}\st{R}})_{I\dot{\beta}}^{\dot{A}}&(U_{\gluino{\dot{A}}\st{L}^{*}})_{I\dot{\beta}}^{\dot{A}}&(U_{\gluino{\dot{A}}\st{R}^{*}})_{I\dot{\beta}}^{\dot{A}}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{2}g_{3}(\cc^{-1})_{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}}\begin{pmatrix}-(\bar{t}_{J}P_{R})_{\dot{\beta}}T^{\dot{A}}_{JI}&(\bar{t}_{J}P_{L})_{\dot{\beta}}T^{\dot{A}}_{JI}&T^{\dot{A}}_{IJ}(\cc P_{L}t_{J})_{\dot{\beta}}&-T^{\dot{A}}_{IJ}(\cc P_{R}t_{J})_{\dot{\beta}}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{2}g_{3}\begin{pmatrix}-(\bar{t}_{J}P_{R}(\cc^{-1})^{T})_{\dot{\alpha}}T^{\dot{A}}_{JI}&(\bar{t}_{J}P_{L}(\cc^{-1})^{T})_{\dot{\alpha}}T^{\dot{A}}_{JI}&T^{\dot{A}}_{IJ}(P_{L}t_{J})_{\dot{\alpha}}&-T^{\dot{A}}_{IJ}(P_{R}t_{J})_{\dot{\alpha}}\end{pmatrix}.

(73)

Inserting these matrices into Eq. (
64
) and summing over all fields and colors we
obtain

\displaystyle\Lag{EFT}^{\text{1\Loop}}

\displaystyle=c_{t}h\bar{t}t+c_{L}\bar{t}i\not{\partial}P_{L}t+c_{R}\bar{t}i\not{\partial}P_{R}t+c_{2}^{\prime}(\partial h)^{2}+c_{2}h^{2}+c_{4}h^{4}+c_{6}h^{6}+\cdots,

(74)

where

\displaystyle c_{t}

\displaystyle=-\frac{4\sqrt{2}}{3}\kappa g_{3}^{2}y_{t}s_{\beta}m_{\gluino{}}X_{t}\ZI^{111}_{\gluino{}\sq\su},

(75)

\displaystyle\begin{split}c_{L}&=\frac{16}{3}\kappa g_{3}^{2}\ZI[q^{2}]^{21}_{\su\gluino{}},\end{split}

(76)

\displaystyle c_{R}

\displaystyle=c_{L}|_{\sq\to\su},

(77)

\displaystyle c_{2}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=-3\kappa(y_{t}s_{\beta})^{2}X_{t}^{2}\ZI[q^{2}]^{22}_{\sq\su},

(78)

\displaystyle c_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{3}{2}\kappa(y_{t}s_{\beta})^{2}\left[\ZI^{1}_{\sq}+\ZI^{1}_{\su}+X_{t}^{2}\ZI^{11}_{\sq\su}\right],

(79)

\displaystyle c_{4}

\displaystyle=\frac{3}{8}\kappa(y_{t}s_{\beta})^{4}\left[\ZI^{11}_{\sq\sq}+\ZI^{11}_{\su\su}+2X_{t}^{2}(\ZI^{111}_{\sq\sq\su}+\ZI^{111}_{\sq\su\su})+X_{t}^{4}\ZI^{1111}_{\sq\sq\su\su}\right],

(80)

\displaystyle\begin{split}c_{6}&=\frac{1}{8}\kappa(y_{t}s_{\beta})^{6}\big[\ZI^{111}_{\sq\sq\sq}+\ZI^{111}_{\su\su\su}+3X_{t}^{2}(\ZI^{1111}_{\sq\sq\sq\su}+\ZI^{1111}_{\sq\sq\su\su}+\ZI^{1111}_{\sq\su\su\su})\\
&~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~+3X_{t}^{4}(\ZI^{11111}_{\sq\sq\sq\su\su}+\ZI^{11111}_{\sq\sq\su\su\su})+X_{t}^{6}\ZI^{111111}_{\sq\sq\sq\su\su\su}\big].\end{split}

(81)

In order to canonically normalise both the top quark field and the Higgs field present in
\Lag{SMEFT}
we introduce
\hat{t}_{L},\,\hat{t}_{R}
and
\hat{h}
defined through

\displaystyle h

\displaystyle=\left(1-\frac{1}{2}\delta Z_{h}\right)\hat{h},

(82)

\displaystyle t_{L}

\displaystyle=\left(1-\frac{1}{2}\delta Z_{L}\right)\hat{t}_{L},

(83)

\displaystyle t_{R}

\displaystyle=\left(1-\frac{1}{2}\delta Z_{R}\right)\hat{t}_{R},

(84)

where the field-redefinitions
\delta Z_{h/L/R}
are given by

\displaystyle\delta Z_{h}

\displaystyle=2c_{2}^{\prime},

(85)

\displaystyle\delta Z_{L}

\displaystyle=c_{L},

(86)

\displaystyle\delta Z_{R}

\displaystyle=c_{R}.

(87)

Expressing the SMEFT Lagrangian through these fields as

\displaystyle\Lag{SMEFT}\supset-\frac{g_{t}}{\sqrt{2}}\hat{h}\bar{\hat{t}}\hat{t}+\frac{m^{2}}{2}\hat{h}^{2}-\frac{\lambda}{8}\hat{h}^{4}-\frac{\tilde{c}_{6}}{8}\hat{h}^{6},

(88)

we find that the SMEFT parameters are given by

\displaystyle g_{t}

\displaystyle=y_{t}s_{\beta}\left[1-\frac{1}{2}(c_{L}+c_{R})-c_{2}^{\prime}-\frac{\sqrt{2}c_{t}}{y_{t}s_{\beta}}\right],

(89)

\displaystyle m^{2}

\displaystyle=2c_{2},

(90)

\displaystyle\lambda

\displaystyle=-8c_{4},

(91)

\displaystyle\tilde{c}_{6}

\displaystyle=-8c_{6},

(92)

which agrees with the results calculated in Refs.
[
105
,
106
,
107
,
53
]
.
2
2

2

It
was noted in Ref.
[
106
]
that the logarithmic term in
the last line of Eq. (D.4) in Ref.
[
53
]
should come with a
minus sign.

4
Integrating out the gluino from the MSSM with light stops

In this section we calculate some of the terms that arise when
integrating out the gluino from the MSSM, which is a relevant scenario when there is a large hierarchy between the gluino mass and
the stop masses in the MSSM. This example is also a direct
application of operators where Majorana and Dirac fermions appear in loops at the same time.

We consider the following part of the MSSM Lagrangian

\displaystyle\Lag{MSSM}\supset{}

\displaystyle|\partial\st{L}|^{2}-\mstL|\st{L}|^{2}+|\partial\st{R}|^{2}-\mstR|\st{R}|^{2}+\frac{1}{2}(\gluino{\dot{A}})^{T}\cc(i\not{\partial}-m_{\gluino{}})\gluino{\dot{A}}

\displaystyle-\sqrt{2}g_{3}\left(\bar{t}P_{R}\gluino{\dot{A}}T^{\dot{A}}\st{L}-\bar{t}P_{L}\gluino{\dot{A}}T^{\dot{A}}\st{R}+\st{L}^{*}(\gluino{\dot{A}})^{T}T^{\dot{A}}\cc P_{L}t-\st{R}^{*}(\gluino{\dot{A}})^{T}T^{\dot{A}}\cc P_{R}t\right)

\displaystyle+\left(-y_{t}^{2}+\frac{g_{3}^{2}}{2}\right)(\st{L}^{*}\st{R})(\st{L}\st{R}^{*})-\frac{g_{3}^{2}}{6}|\st{L}|^{2}|\st{R}|^{2},

(93)

where we use the same notation as in the previous section.
We determine the one-loop Wilson coefficients of the operators

\displaystyle\Lag{EFT}^{1\Loop}\supset{}

\displaystyle c_{t_{L}}\bar{t}_{L}i\not{\partial}t_{L}+c_{t_{R}}\bar{t}_{R}i\not{\partial}t_{R}+c_{\st{L}}\partial_{\mu}\st{L}^{*}\partial^{\mu}\st{L}-\delta m_{\sq}^{2}|\st{L}|^{2}+c_{\st{R}}\partial_{\mu}\st{R}^{*}\partial^{\mu}\st{R}-\delta m_{\su}^{2}|\st{R}|^{2}

\displaystyle+c^{L}_{41}\left(\st{LI}^{*}\st{LI}\right)^{2}+c^{L}_{42}\left(\st{LI}^{*}\st{LJ}\right)\left(\st{LJ}^{*}\st{LI}\right)+c^{R}_{4}\left(\st{R}^{*}\st{R}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle+c^{LR}_{41}\left(\st{LI}^{*}\st{LI}\right)\left(\st{RJ}^{*}\st{RJ}\right)+c^{LR}_{42}\left(\st{LI}^{*}\st{LJ}\right)\left(\st{RJ}^{*}\st{RI}\right)+c_{G}G^{\dot{A}}_{\mu\nu}G_{\dot{A}}^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle+[c^{LL}_{51}(\bar{t}_{LI}T^{\dot{A}}_{IJ}\st{LJ})(\ccfield{t}_{RK}T^{\dot{A}}_{KM}\st{LM})+c^{LL}_{52}(\st{LI}^{*}T^{\dot{A}}_{IJ}\overline{\ccfield{t_{RJ}}})(\st{LK}^{*}T^{\dot{A}}_{KM}t_{LM})+(L\leftrightarrow R)]

\displaystyle+[c^{LR}_{51}(\bar{t}_{LI}T^{\dot{A}}_{IJ}\st{LJ})(\st{RK}^{*}T^{\dot{A}}_{KM}t_{RM})+c^{LR}_{52}(\st{LI}\st{RI}^{*})(\bar{t}_{LJ}t_{RJ})+(L\leftrightarrow R)]

\displaystyle+c_{61}^{L}(\tilde{t}_{LI}^{*}\tilde{t}_{LI})^{3}+c_{62}^{L}(\tilde{t}_{LI}^{*}\tilde{t}_{LI})(\tilde{t}_{LJ}^{*}\tilde{t}_{LK})(\tilde{t}_{LK}^{*}\tilde{t}_{LJ})+c_{63}^{L}(\tilde{t}_{LI}^{*}\tilde{t}_{LJ})(\tilde{t}_{LJ}^{*}\tilde{t}_{LK})(\tilde{t}_{LK}^{*}\tilde{t}_{LI})

\displaystyle+c_{6}^{R}(\tilde{t}_{RI}^{*}\tilde{t}_{RI})^{3}+[c_{61}^{LR}(\tilde{t}_{LI}^{*}\tilde{t}_{LI})^{2}(\tilde{t}_{RI}^{*}\tilde{t}_{RI})+c_{62}^{LR}(\tilde{t}_{LI}^{*}\tilde{t}_{LI})(\tilde{t}_{LJ}^{*}\tilde{t}_{LK})(\tilde{t}_{RK}^{*}\tilde{t}_{RJ})

\displaystyle+c_{63}^{LR}(\tilde{t}_{LI}^{*}\tilde{t}_{LJ})(\tilde{t}_{LJ}^{*}\tilde{t}_{LI})(\tilde{t}_{RK}^{*}\tilde{t}_{RK})+c_{64}^{LR}(\tilde{t}_{LI}^{*}\tilde{t}_{LJ})(\tilde{t}_{LJ}^{*}\tilde{t}_{LK})(\tilde{t}_{RK}^{*}\tilde{t}_{RI})

\displaystyle+c_{61}^{RL}(\tilde{t}_{RI}^{*}\tilde{t}_{RI})^{2}(\tilde{t}_{LI}^{*}\tilde{t}_{LI})+c_{62}^{RL}(\tilde{t}_{RI}^{*}\tilde{t}_{RI})(\tilde{t}_{RJ}^{*}\tilde{t}_{RK})(\tilde{t}_{LK}^{*}\tilde{t}_{LJ})]

\displaystyle+[c_{61}^{L^{\mu}L_{\mu}}\left(\bar{t}_{LI}\gamma^{\mu}t_{LI}\right)\left(\bar{t}_{LJ}\gamma_{\mu}t_{LJ}\right)+c_{62}^{L^{\mu}L_{\mu}}\left(\bar{t}_{LI}\gamma^{\mu}t_{LJ}\right)\left(\bar{t}_{LJ}\gamma_{\mu}t_{LI}\right)+(L\leftrightarrow R)]

\displaystyle+c_{61}^{(LR)^{\mu}(RL)_{\mu}}\left(\overline{\ccfield{t_{RI}}}\gamma^{\mu}t_{RJ}\right)\left(\bar{t}_{RJ}\gamma_{\mu}\ccfield{t}_{RI}\right)+c_{62}^{(LR)^{\mu}(RL)_{\mu}}\left(\overline{\ccfield{t_{RJ}}}\gamma^{\mu}t_{RI}\right)\left(\bar{t}_{RJ}\gamma_{\mu}\ccfield{t}_{RI}\right)

\displaystyle+[c_{61}^{LL}\left(\overline{\ccfield{t_{RI}}}t_{LI}\right)\left(\bar{t}_{LJ}\ccfield{t}_{RJ}\right)+c_{62}^{LL}\left(\overline{\ccfield{t_{RI}}}t_{LJ}\right)\left(\bar{t}_{LJ}\ccfield{t}_{RI}\right)+(L\leftrightarrow R)]

\displaystyle+c_{61}^{(LR)(RL)}\left(\bar{t}_{RI}t_{LJ}\right)\left(\bar{t}_{LJ}t_{RI}\right)+c_{62}^{(LR)(RL)}\left(\bar{t}_{RJ}t_{LI}\right)\left(\bar{t}_{LJ}t_{RI}\right),

(94)

which represent all one-loop stop interactions in
the gaugeless limit and in the unbroken phase, excluding operators with covariant derivatives. Note that the terms with the couplings
c_{41}^{L}
and
c_{42}^{L}
have the same structure as presented in Eq. (
94
). However, these terms arise from the two distinct
SU(2)_{L}
and
SU(3)_{c}
invariant combinations
(\tilde{q}^{\dagger}_{LI}\tilde{q}_{LI})(\tilde{q}^{\dagger}_{LJ}\tilde{q}_{LJ})
and

(\tilde{q}^{\dagger}_{LI}\tilde{q}_{LJ})(\tilde{q}^{\dagger}_{LJ}\tilde{q}_{LI})
,
where
\tilde{q}_{L}
is the
SU(2)_{L}
squark doublet and the
SU(2)_{L}
indices are contracted within parentheses. Therefore, these couplings can, in principle, take different values.

The dimension five operators have contributions already at tree level,
which stem from the insertion of the classical gluino field

\classical{\hat{\gluino{}}}
into the Lagrangian of the MSSM. As discussed in Chapter
1
, the classical gluino field is determined from the equation of motion

\displaystyle[\cc(i\not{\partial}-m_{\gluino{}})]_{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}}(\gluinocl)_{\dot{\beta}}^{\dot{A}}=\sqrt{2}g_{3}\left(-\bar{t}_{L\dot{\alpha}}T^{\dot{A}}\st{L}+\bar{t}_{R\dot{\alpha}}T^{\dot{A}}\st{R}+\st{L}^{*}T^{\dot{A}}(\cc t_{L})_{\dot{\alpha}}-\st{R}^{*}T^{\dot{A}}(\cc t_{R})_{\dot{\alpha}}\right),

(95)

which yields

\displaystyle(\gluinocl)_{\dot{\beta}}^{\dot{A}}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{2}g_{3}(i\not{\partial}-m_{\gluino{}})_{\dot{\beta}\dot{\alpha}}^{-1}\left[-(\bar{t}_{L}\cc)_{\dot{\alpha}}T^{\dot{A}}\st{L}+(\bar{t}_{R}\cc)_{\dot{\alpha}}T^{\dot{A}}\st{R}+\st{L}^{*}T^{\dot{A}}t_{L\dot{\alpha}}-\st{R}^{*}T^{\dot{A}}t_{R\dot{\alpha}}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{\sqrt{2}g_{3}}{m_{\gluino{}}}\left[(\bar{t}_{L}\cc)_{\dot{\beta}}T^{\dot{A}}\st{L}-(\bar{t}_{R}\cc)_{\dot{\beta}}T^{\dot{A}}\st{R}-\st{L}^{*}T^{\dot{A}}t_{L\dot{\beta}}+\st{R}^{*}T^{\dot{A}}t_{R\dot{\beta}}+\cdots\right],

(96)

where the ellipsis designate higher order terms of

\mathcal{O}(\partial/m_{\gluino{}})
with at least one
derivative. Inserting Eq. (
96
) into Eq. (
93
) one finds
the tree-level values of
c_{5i}^{AB}
(
A,B\in\{L,R\}
) to be

\displaystyle c_{51}^{LL,\tree}

\displaystyle=c_{52}^{LL,\tree}=c_{51}^{RR,\tree}=c_{52}^{RR,\tree}=\frac{g^{2}_{3}}{m_{\gluino{}}},

(97)

\displaystyle c_{51}^{LR,\tree}

\displaystyle=c_{51}^{RL,\tree}=-\frac{2g^{2}_{3}}{m_{\gluino{}}},

(98)

\displaystyle c_{52}^{LR,\tree}

\displaystyle=c_{52}^{RL,\tree}=0.

(99)

At the one-loop level the relevant contributions from the BSUOLEA are

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\kappa}\Lag{EFT}^{1\Loop}=\tr\Big\{

\displaystyle(-\ZI[q^{4}]^{31}_{\gluino{}0}+\frac{m^{2}_{\gluino{}}}{12}\ZI[q^{2}]^{22}_{\gluino{}0})\gamma_{\mu}[P^{\nu},\mathbf{U}_{\Xi\xi}]\gamma^{\mu}[P_{\nu},\mathbf{U}_{\xi\Xi}]

\displaystyle+(-2\ZI[q^{4}]^{31}_{\gluino{}0}+\frac{m^{2}_{\gluino{}}}{6}\ZI[q^{2}]^{22}_{\gluino{}0})\gamma_{\mu}[P^{\mu},\mathbf{U}_{\Xi\xi}]\gamma^{\nu}[P_{\nu},\mathbf{U}_{\xi\Xi}]

\displaystyle+(-\ZI[q^{2}]^{12}_{\gluino{}0}-2m^{2}_{\phi_{i}}\ZI[q^{2}]^{13}_{\gluino{}0})(\mathbf{U}_{\phi\Xi})_{i}\gamma^{\mu}[P_{\mu},(\mathbf{U}_{\Xi\phi})_{i}]

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{4}\ZI[q^{2}]^{22}_{\gluino{}0}(\mathbf{U}_{\phi\Xi})_{i}\gamma^{\mu}(\mathbf{U}_{\Xi\phi})_{j}(\mathbf{U}_{\phi\Xi})_{j}\gamma_{\mu}(\mathbf{U}_{\Xi\phi})_{i}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}m_{\gluino{}}\ZI^{12}_{\gluino{}0}(\mathbf{U}_{\phi\phi})_{ij}(\mathbf{U}_{\phi\Xi})_{j}(\mathbf{U}_{\Xi\phi})_{i}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{4}m^{2}_{\gluino{}}\ZI^{22}_{\gluino{}0}(\mathbf{U}_{\phi\Xi})_{i}(\mathbf{U}_{\Xi\phi})_{j}(\mathbf{U}_{\phi\Xi})_{j}(\mathbf{U}_{\Xi\phi})_{i}-\frac{1}{2}\ZI[q^{2}]^{11}_{{\gluino{}}0}\gamma^{\mu}\mathbf{U}_{\Xi\xi}\gamma_{\mu}\mathbf{U}_{\xi\Xi}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4}m^{2}_{\gluino{}}\ZI[q^{2}]^{22}_{\gluino{}0}\mathbf{U}_{\Xi\xi}\gamma^{\mu}\mathbf{U}_{\xi\Xi}\mathbf{U}_{\Xi\xi}\gamma_{\mu}\mathbf{U}_{\xi\Xi}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4}\ZI[q^{4}]^{22}_{\gluino{}0}g_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\mathbf{U}_{\Xi\xi}\gamma^{\mu}\mathbf{U}_{\xi\Xi}\gamma^{\nu}\mathbf{U}_{\Xi\xi}\gamma^{\rho}\mathbf{U}_{\xi\Xi}\gamma^{\sigma}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}m^{2}_{\gluino{}}\ZI[q^{4}]^{33}_{\gluino{}0}g_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\mathbf{U}_{\Xi\xi}\gamma^{\mu}\mathbf{U}_{\xi\Xi}\mathbf{U}_{\Xi\xi}\gamma^{\nu}\mathbf{U}_{\xi\Xi}\gamma^{\rho}\mathbf{U}_{\Xi\xi}\gamma^{\sigma}\mathbf{U}_{\xi\Xi}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{6}\ZI[q^{6}]^{33}_{\gluino{}0}g_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma\kappa\lambda}\mathbf{U}_{\Xi\xi}\gamma^{\mu}\mathbf{U}_{\xi\Xi}\gamma^{\nu}\mathbf{U}_{\Xi\xi}\gamma^{\rho}\mathbf{U}_{\xi\Xi}\gamma^{\sigma}\mathbf{U}_{\Xi\xi}\gamma^{\kappa}\mathbf{U}_{\xi\Xi}\gamma^{\lambda}

(100)

where

is the combination of metric tensors which
is totally symmetric in all indices, see
Appendix
9
. In Eq. (

) we omitted the multi-indices of the fermionic fields as both the light and heavy fermionic multiplets only contain one field. The derivatives with respect to the stops
and the gluino were already calculated in the previous section and are given by

\displaystyle\mathbf{U}_{\phi\Xi}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}U_{\sigma^{*}\Lambda}\\
U_{\sigma\Lambda}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}(U_{\st{L}^{*}\gluino{\dot{A}}})_{I\dot{\alpha}}^{\dot{A}}\\

(101)

and the results agree when the effect of the sbottom quarks is neglected.

As in Section
2
it is again convenient
to use DRED as a regulator in the MSSM. Since supersymmetry is explicitly broken once the gluino is
integrated out from the theory, it is natural to regularise the EFT in
DREG. This switch in regularisation again introduces further
contributions to the couplings of the EFT coming from the epsilon
scalars, which can be computed using Eq. (
185
).
We obtain the following
additional contributions

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\kappa}(\delta m^{2}_{\sq})_{\eps}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\kappa}(\delta m^{2}_{\su})_{\eps}=-\frac{4}{3}g_{3}^{2}m_{\eps}^{2},

(141)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\kappa}(c_{t_{L}})_{\eps}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\kappa}(c_{t_{R}})_{\eps}=\frac{4}{3}g_{3}^{2},

(142)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{72}g_{3}^{4},

(143)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\kappa}(c^{L}_{42})_{\eps}

\displaystyle=\frac{7}{24}g_{3}^{4},

(144)

\displaystyle=\frac{11}{36}g_{3}^{4},

(145)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\kappa}(c^{LR}_{41})_{\eps}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{36}g_{3}^{4},

(146)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\kappa}(c^{LR}_{42})_{\eps}

\displaystyle=\frac{7}{12}g_{3}^{4},

(147)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\kappa}(c^{LL}_{52})_{\eps}=\frac{1}{\kappa}(c^{RR}_{51})_{\eps}=\frac{1}{\kappa}(c^{RR}_{52})_{\eps}=\frac{3g_{3}^{4}}{2m_{\gluino{}}}d,

(148)

(149)

\displaystyle=-\frac{g_{3}^{2}}{4}.

(150)

Notice that the one-loop DRED–DREG conversion corrections to the
coefficients of the dimension five operators arise from the term

\displaystyle\left(\epsdim{U}_{\eps t}\right)^{\mu}\gamma^{\nu}U_{\bar{t}\gluino{\dot{A}}}\gamma_{\nu}\left(\epsdim{U}_{\gluino{\dot{A}}\eps}\right)_{\mu}.

(151)

Here
\left(\epsdim{U}_{\gluino{\dot{A}}\eps}\right)^{\mu}
has an explicit

\gluino{\dot{A}}
,

\displaystyle\left(\epsdim{U}_{\gluino{\dot{A}}\eps}\right)^{\mu}=ig_{3}\epsdim{\gamma}^{\mu}f^{\dot{A}\dot{B}\dot{C}}\gluino{\dot{C}},

(152)

which must be eliminated by inserting the classical field from Eq. (
96
).

5
Matching of the SSM to the SMEFT

In this section we consider the SSM, which is the SM with an additional real scalar field
S
, transforming as a singlet under the SM gauge group. We assume the singlet to be heavy and integrate it out of the theory using the BSUOLEA to obtain SMEFT operators up to mass dimension six. The final result is then expressed in the Warsaw basis. This particular matching was first computed in Ref.
[
49
]
using the UOLEA presented in Ref.
[
56
]
supplemented by Feynman diagrammatic techniques for loops of mixed statistics and loops including gauge bosons. The matching was also re-computed using Feynman diagrammatic techniques in Ref.
[
48
]
for the purpose of studying the effect of SMEFT operators on one-loop probes of pseudo Nambu-Goldstone boson dark matter. We here, for the first time, perform the full matching using the UOLEA approach, illustrating that the extensions that were presented in this thesis are needed even for one of the simplest SM extensions of interest to particle phenomenology. Furthermore, this section can be viewed as both a check of the BSUOLEA and its implementation into
\tofu
as well as an illustration of how to consistently treat a UV theory that contains the SM field content and gauge group in the BSUOLEA framework.

As the SSM is a chiral gauge theory it is appropriately described using Weyl fermions when working in the unbroken phase. Strictly speaking, we only considered Dirac and Majorana fermions in this thesis. However, for the purpose of a perturbative calculation Weyl fermions may be implemented as either Dirac fermions or Majorana fermions, as the chiral nature of the interactions will always project out the physically relevant components of these four-component spinors.
3
3

3

A discussion of this and related issues can be found in Ref.
[
109
]
. Interestingly, in non-perturbative considerations Weyl fermions cannot be treated as either Dirac fermions or Majorana fermions.
We here choose to treat them as Majorana fermions. To this end we introduce the left-handed two-component spinors
q,\bar{u},\bar{d},l
and
\bar{e}
, where the bar on
u,d,e
is part of the name and does not signify any kind of conjugation. These spinors all carry flavour indices. The charges under the SM gauge group
SU(3)_{c}\times SU(2)_{L}\times U(1)_{Y}
carried by these fields are summarised in Table
2
below, where we also include the Higgs doublet
H
and the singlet
S
.

SU(2)_{L}

U(1)_{Y}

SU(3)_{c}

q

2

1/6

3

\bar{u}

1

-2/3

\bar{3}

\bar{d}

1

1/3

\bar{3}

l

2

-1/2

1

\bar{e}

1

1

1

H

2

1/2

1

S

1

0

1

Table 2:
Gauge charges of the fields in the SSM.

Note that the
U(1)_{Y}
charges of the barred fields are opposite to those of the corresponding SM fields since the barred fields are left-handed and the corresponding SM fields are related to these by Hermitian conjugation. We then introduce the Majorana spinors

\displaystyle\Psi_{q}=\begin{pmatrix}q\,\\
q^{\dagger}\end{pmatrix},\Psi_{u}=\begin{pmatrix}\bar{u}\;\\
\bar{u}^{\dagger}\end{pmatrix},\Psi_{d}=\begin{pmatrix}\bar{d}\;\\
\bar{d}^{\dagger}\end{pmatrix},\Psi_{l}=\begin{pmatrix}l\,\\
l^{\dagger}\end{pmatrix},\Psi_{e}=\begin{pmatrix}\bar{e}\;\\
\bar{e}^{\dagger}\end{pmatrix},

(153)

which transform in chiral representations of the SM gauge group. Observe that the fields
e=\bar{e}^{\dagger}
,
d=\bar{d}^{\dagger}
and
u=\bar{u}^{\dagger}
are the usual right-handed spinors introduced in the SM. The generators for the
U(1)_{Y}
- and
SU(2)_{L}
-transformation of
\Psi_{f}
can be written as

\displaystyle Y_{\Psi_{f}}=Y_{f}P_{L}-Y_{f}P_{R}\text{\; and \;}

\displaystyle T^{A}_{\Psi_{f}}=T^{A}_{f}P_{L}+\bar{T}^{A}_{f}P_{R},

(154)

respectively, where
T^{A}_{f}
is the usual generator of
SU(2)_{L}
in the representation under which the fermion
f\in\{q,d,u,l,e\}
transforms and
\bar{T}^{A}_{f}
is the generator of the corresponding conjugate representation. Furthermore,
Y_{f}
is the hypercharge of the left-handed component of
\Psi_{f}
. As already mentioned, the details of this construction are inconsequential to the computation since the SSM interactions are chirality preserving and therefore always project out the physical components of these spinors.

With these preparations we may write the Lagrangian of the SSM as

\displaystyle\LagNoT

\displaystyle=\LagNoT_{S}+(D_{\mu}H)^{\dagger}(D^{\mu}H)+\mu_{h}^{2}|H|^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{h}|H|^{4}-\frac{1}{4}B^{\mu\nu}B_{\mu\nu}-\frac{1}{4}W^{A\mu\nu}W^{A}_{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{i}{2}\sum\limits_{i\in\{q,u,d,l,e\}}\bar{\Psi}_{i}\not{D}\Psi_{i}-\left(\bar{\Psi}_{q}y_{u}\tilde{H}P_{R}\Psi_{u}+\bar{\Psi}_{q}y_{d}HP_{R}\Psi_{d}+\bar{\Psi}_{l}y_{e}HP_{R}\Psi_{e}+\text{h.c.}\right),

(155)

with

\displaystyle\LagNoT_{S}=\frac{1}{2}\left(\partial_{\mu}S\right)\left(\partial^{\mu}S\right)-\frac{1}{2}M^{2}S^{2}-A|H|^{2}S-\frac{\kappa}{2}|H|^{2}S^{2}-\frac{\mu}{3!}S^{3}-\frac{\lambda_{S}}{4!}S^{4}.

(156)

In Eq. (
5
) we introduced
\tilde{H}_{a}=\epstensor_{ab}H^{*}_{b}
with
\epstensor_{ab}
the totally antisymmetric symbol with two indices and
\epstensor_{12}=1
. The field strength tensors
W^{A}_{\mu\nu}
and
B_{\mu\nu}
correspond to the gauge-kinetic terms of the
SU(2)_{L}
and
U(1)_{Y}
gauge bosons, respectively. The corresponding gauge couplings will be denoted by
g_{1}
and
g_{2}
. We ignore QCD as it is not relevant for the matching presented here.

We start by briefly describing the tree-level matching. To perform this matching we need to compute the classical field
\classical{S}
, which is determined from the equation of motion of the singlet

\displaystyle\left(-\Box-M^{2}\right)S=A|H|^{2}+\kappa|H|^{2}S+\frac{\mu}{2}S^{2}+\frac{\lambda_{S}}{3!}S^{3}.

(157)

This can be solved iteratively in an expansion in
1/M
. Care has to be taken as the parameters
A
and
\mu
are dimensionful and are taken to be of
\mathcal{O}(M)
. The lowest order solution is

\displaystyle\classical{S}^{\left(1\right)}=-\frac{A}{M^{2}}|H|^{2},

(158)

which inserted into the r.h.s. of Eq. (
157
) allows for the determination of
\classical{S}^{\left(2\right)}

\displaystyle\left(-\Box-M^{2}\right)\classical{S}^{\left(2\right)}

\displaystyle=A|H^{2}|-\frac{A\kappa}{M^{2}}|H|^{4}+\mathcal{O}\left(M^{-3}\right)

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\classical{S}^{\left(2\right)}

\displaystyle=-\frac{A}{M^{2}}|H|^{2}+\frac{A}{M^{4}}\Box|H|^{2}+\frac{A\kappa}{M^{4}}|H|^{4}.

(159)

By repeatedly inserting the obtained solution into the r.h.s. of Eq. (
157
) one can determine the classical solution to the desired order in
M^{-1}
. The final result is given by

\displaystyle\classical{\hat{S}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{A}{M^{2}}|H|^{2}+\left(\frac{A\kappa}{M^{4}}-\frac{A^{2}\mu}{2M^{6}}\right)|H|^{4}+\frac{A}{M^{4}}\Box|H|^{2}-\left(\frac{A\kappa}{M^{6}}-\frac{A^{2}\mu}{M^{8}}\right)|H|^{2}\Box|H|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad-\left(\frac{A\kappa}{M^{6}}-\frac{A^{3}\lambda_{\phi}+9A^{2}\kappa\mu}{6M^{8}}+\frac{A^{3}\mu^{2}}{2M^{1}0}\right)|H|^{6},

(160)

which was already reported in Ref.
[
48
]
. Inserting this into the UV Lagrangian and truncating at mass dimension six one finds the tree-level results reported in that reference. The computation of the classical heavy fields and the tree-level matching is also fully automated in
\tofu
.

We now move to the one-loop matching. The multiplets defined in Table
2
containing the different kinds of fields are in the case at hand

\displaystyle\Phi

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}S\end{pmatrix},

(161)

\displaystyle\xi

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}\Psi_{qi}^{\dot{\alpha}a}&\Psi_{ui}^{\dot{\alpha}}&\Psi_{di}^{\dot{\alpha}}&\Psi_{li}^{\dot{\alpha}a}&\Psi_{ei}^{\dot{\alpha}}\end{pmatrix}^{T},

(162)

\displaystyle\phi

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}H_{a}&H^{*}_{a}&B_{\mu}&W^{A}_{\mu}\end{pmatrix}^{T},

(163)

where there is no multiplet
\Xi
since there are no heavy fermions present. Also, since we are treating all of the fermions as Majorana fermions we have
\xi=\lambda
. Next, the relevant derivative matrices have to be computed. The matrices associated with at least one derivative w.r.t. a heavy field originate from
\LagNoT_{S}
and are given by

\displaystyle\mathbf{U}_{\Phi\Phi}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}\kappa|H|^{2}+\mu\classical{\hat{S}}+\frac{\lambda_{S}}{2}\classical{\hat{S}}\end{pmatrix},

(164)

\displaystyle\mathbf{U}_{\phi\Phi}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}AH_{a}+\kappa H_{a}\classical{\hat{S}}\\
AH^{*}_{a}+\kappa H^{*}_{a}\classical{\hat{S}}\\
0\\
0\end{pmatrix},

(165)

\displaystyle\mathbf{U}_{\Phi\phi}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}AH^{*}_{a}+\kappa H^{*}_{a}\classical{\hat{S}}&AH_{a}+\kappa H_{a}\classical{\hat{S}}&0&0\end{pmatrix}.

(166)

In Eqs. (
164
)–(
166
) the expression of Eq. (
5
) has to be inserted. Many of the contributions to the remaining matrices originate entirely from the SM part of the Lagrangian and will be present in any application in which the UV theory contains the SM. We therefore present these matrices in their entirety starting with
\mathbf{U}_{\phi\phi}
, which is given by

\displaystyle\mathbf{U}_{\phi\phi}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}\left(U_{H^{*}H}\right)_{ab}&\left(U_{H^{*}H^{*}}\right)_{ab}&\left(U_{H^{*}B}\right)_{a\nu}&\left(U_{H^{*}W}\right)_{a\nu B}\\
\left(U_{HH}\right)_{ab}&\left(U_{HH^{*}}\right)_{ab}&\left(U_{HB}\right)_{a\nu}&\left(U_{HW}\right)_{a\nu B}\\
-\left(U_{BH}\right)_{\mu b}&-\left(U_{BH^{*}}\right)_{\mu b}&-\left(U_{BB}\right)_{\mu\nu}&-\left(U_{BW}\right)_{\mu\nu B}\\
-\left(U_{WH}\right)_{\mu Ab}&-\left(U_{WH^{*}}\right)_{\mu Ab}&-\left(U_{WB}\right)_{\mu A\nu}&-\left(U_{WW}\right)_{\mu A\nu B}\end{pmatrix},

(167)

where

\displaystyle\left(U_{H^{*}H}\right)_{ab}

\displaystyle=\lambda_{h}|H|^{2}\delta_{ab}+\lambda_{h}H_{a}H^{*}_{b}+A\delta_{ab}\classical{\hat{S}}+\frac{\kappa}{2}\delta_{ab}\classical{\hat{S}}^{2},

(168)

\displaystyle\left(U_{H^{*}H^{*}}\right)_{ab}

\displaystyle=\lambda_{h}H_{a}H_{b},

(169)

\displaystyle\left(U_{H^{*}B}\right)_{a\mu}

\displaystyle=\left(U_{BH^{*}}\right)_{\mu a}=\frac{g_{1}}{2}[P_{\mu},H_{a}],

(170)

\displaystyle\left(U_{H^{*}W}\right)_{a\mu A}

\displaystyle=\left(U_{WH^{*}}\right)_{\mu Aa}=g_{2}T^{A}_{ab}[P_{\mu},H_{b}],

(171)

\displaystyle U_{HH}

\displaystyle=\lambda_{h}H^{*}_{a}H^{*}_{b},

(172)

\displaystyle\left(U_{HH^{*}}\right)_{ab}

\displaystyle=\lambda_{h}|H|^{2}\delta_{ab}+\lambda_{h}H_{b}H^{*}_{a}+A\delta_{ab}\classical{\hat{S}}+\frac{\kappa}{2}\delta_{ab}\classical{\hat{S}}^{2},

(173)

\displaystyle\left(U_{HB}\right)_{a\mu}

\displaystyle=\left(U_{BH}\right)_{\mu a}=-\frac{g_{1}}{2}[P_{\mu},H^{*}_{a}],

(174)

\displaystyle\left(U_{HW}\right)_{a\mu A}

\displaystyle=\left(U_{WH}\right)_{\mu Aa}=-g_{2}T^{A}_{ca}[P_{\mu},H^{*}_{c}],

(175)

\displaystyle\left(U_{BB}\right)_{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=-\frac{g_{1}^{2}}{2}|H|^{2}g_{\mu\nu},

(176)

\displaystyle\left(U_{BW}\right)_{\mu\nu A}

\displaystyle=\left(U_{WB}\right)_{\mu A\nu}=-g_{1}g_{2}H^{*}_{a}T^{A}_{ab}H_{b}g_{\mu\nu},

(177)

\displaystyle\left(U_{WW}\right)_{\mu A\nu B}

\displaystyle=-g_{2}^{2}H^{*}_{a}\{T^{A},T^{B}\}_{ab}H_{b}g_{\mu\nu}.

(178)

The matrices with derivatives of mixed statistics are

\displaystyle\mathbf{U}_{\xi\phi}

\displaystyle=\cc^{-1}_{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}}\begin{pmatrix}\left(U_{\Psi_{q}H}\right)_{\dot{\beta}aib}&\left(U_{\Psi_{q}H^{*}}\right)_{\dot{\beta}aib}&\left(U_{\Psi_{q}B}\right)_{\dot{\beta}ai\mu}&\left(U_{\Psi_{q}W}\right)_{\dot{\beta}ai\mu A}\\
\left(U_{\Psi_{u}H}\right)_{\dot{\beta}ib}&\left(U_{\Psi_{u}H^{*}}\right)_{\dot{\beta}ib}&\left(U_{\Psi_{u}B}\right)_{\dot{\beta}i\mu}&\left(U_{\Psi_{u}W}\right)_{\dot{\beta}ai\mu A}\\
\left(U_{\Psi_{d}H}\right)_{\dot{\beta}ib}&\left(U_{\Psi_{d}H^{*}}\right)_{\dot{\beta}ib}&\left(U_{\Psi_{d}B}\right)_{\dot{\beta}i\mu}&\left(U_{\Psi_{d}W}\right)_{\dot{\beta}ai\mu A}\\
\left(U_{\Psi_{l}H}\right)_{\dot{\beta}aib}&\left(U_{\Psi_{l}H^{*}}\right)_{\dot{\beta}aib}&\left(U_{\Psi_{l}B}\right)_{\dot{\beta}ai\mu}&\left(U_{\Psi_{l}W}\right)_{\dot{\beta}ai\mu A}\\
\left(U_{\Psi_{e}H}\right)_{\dot{\beta}ib}&\left(U_{\Psi_{e}H^{*}}\right)_{\dot{\beta}ib}&\left(U_{\Psi_{e}B}\right)_{\dot{\beta}i\mu}&\left(U_{\Psi_{e}W}\right)_{\dot{\beta}ai\mu A}\end{pmatrix},

(179)

and

\displaystyle\mathbf{U}_{\phi\xi}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}\left(U_{H^{*}\Psi_{q}}\right)_{a\dot{\beta}bi}&\left(U_{H^{*}\Psi_{u}}\right)_{a\dot{\beta}i}&\left(U_{H^{*}\Psi_{d}}\right)_{a\dot{\beta}i}&\left(U_{H^{*}\Psi_{l}}\right)_{a\dot{\beta}bi}&\left(U_{H^{*}\Psi_{e}}\right)_{a\dot{\beta}i}\\
\left(U_{H\Psi_{q}}\right)_{a\dot{\beta}bi}&\left(U_{H\Psi_{u}}\right)_{a\dot{\beta}i}&\left(U_{H\Psi_{d}}\right)_{a\dot{\beta}i}&\left(U_{H\Psi_{l}}\right)_{a\dot{\beta}bi}&\left(U_{H\Psi_{e}}\right)_{a\dot{\beta}i}\\
-\left(U_{B\Psi_{q}}\right)^{\mu}_{\dot{\beta}bi}&-\left(U_{B\Psi_{u}}\right)^{\mu}_{\dot{\beta}i}&-\left(U_{B\Psi_{d}}\right)^{\mu}_{\dot{\beta}i}&-\left(U_{B\Psi_{l}}\right)^{\mu}_{\dot{\beta}bi}&-\left(U_{B\Psi_{e}}\right)^{\mu}_{\dot{\beta}i}\\
-\left(U_{W\Psi_{q}}\right)^{\mu A}_{\dot{\beta}bi}&-\left(U_{W\Psi_{u}}\right)^{\mu A}_{\dot{\beta}i}&-\left(U_{W\Psi_{d}}\right)^{\mu A}_{\dot{\beta}i}&-\left(U_{W\Psi_{l}}\right)^{\mu A}_{\dot{\beta}bi}&-\left(U_{W\Psi_{e}}\right)^{\mu A}_{\dot{\beta}i}\end{pmatrix},

(180)

with

\displaystyle\left(U_{\Psi_{q}H}\right)_{\dot{\beta}akb}

\displaystyle=\left(U_{H\Psi_{q}}\right)_{b\dot{\beta}ak}=-\left(y^{*}_{u}\right)^{ki}\Psi_{ui}^{\dot{\alpha}}\cc_{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\rho}}\epsilon_{ab}P_{L}^{\dot{\rho}\dot{\beta}}+y_{d}^{ki}\cc^{\dot{\beta}\dot{\rho}}P_{R}^{\dot{\rho}\dot{\sigma}}\Psi_{di}^{\dot{\sigma}}\delta_{ab},

(181)

\displaystyle\left(U_{\Psi_{q}H^{*}}\right)_{\dot{\beta}akb}

\displaystyle=\left(U_{H^{*}\Psi_{q}}\right)_{b\dot{\beta}ak}=y_{u}^{kj}\cc_{\dot{\beta}\dot{\rho}}\epsilon_{ab}P_{R}^{\dot{\rho}\dot{\sigma}}\Psi_{uj}^{\dot{\sigma}}-\left(y_{d}^{*}\right)^{ki}\Psi_{di}^{\dot{\alpha}}\cc^{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\rho}}P_{L}^{\dot{\rho}\dot{\beta}}\delta_{ab},

(182)

\displaystyle\left(U_{\Psi_{u}H}\right)_{\dot{\beta}kb}

\displaystyle=\left(U_{H\Psi_{u}}\right)_{b\dot{\beta}k}=\left(y^{*}_{u}\right)^{ik}\cc_{\dot{\beta}\dot{\rho}}\epsilon_{ab}P_{L}^{\dot{\rho}\dot{\sigma}}\Psi_{qi}^{\dot{\sigma}a},

(183)

\displaystyle\left(U_{\Psi_{u}H^{*}}\right)_{\dot{\beta}kb}

\displaystyle=\left(U_{H^{*}\Psi_{u}}\right)_{b\dot{\beta}k}=-y_{u}^{jk}\Psi_{qj}^{\dot{\alpha}a}\cc_{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\rho}}\epsilon_{ab}P_{R}^{\dot{\rho}\dot{\beta}},

(184)

\displaystyle\left(U_{\Psi_{d}H}\right)_{\dot{\beta}kb}

\displaystyle=\left(U_{H\Psi_{d}}\right)_{b\dot{\beta}k}=-y_{d}^{jk}\Psi_{qj}^{\dot{\alpha}b}\cc_{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\rho}}P_{R}^{\dot{\rho}\dot{\beta}},

(185)

\displaystyle\left(U_{\Psi_{d}H^{*}}\right)_{\dot{\beta}kb}

\displaystyle=\left(U_{H^{*}\Psi_{d}}\right)_{b\dot{\beta}k}=\left(y^{*}_{d}\right)^{ik}\cc_{\dot{\beta}\dot{\rho}}P_{L}^{\dot{\rho}\dot{\sigma}}\Psi_{qi}^{\dot{\sigma}b},

(186)

\displaystyle\left(U_{\Psi_{l}H}\right)_{\dot{\beta}akb}

\displaystyle=\left(U_{H\Psi_{l}}\right)_{b\dot{\beta}ak}=y_{e}^{ki}\cc^{\dot{\beta}\dot{\rho}}P_{R}^{\dot{\rho}\dot{\sigma}}\Psi_{ei}^{\dot{\sigma}}\delta_{ab},

(187)

\displaystyle\left(U_{\Psi_{l}H^{*}}\right)_{\dot{\beta}akb}

\displaystyle=\left(U_{H^{*}\Psi_{l}}\right)_{b\dot{\beta}ak}=-\left(y_{e}^{*}\right)^{ki}\Psi_{ei}^{\dot{\alpha}}\cc^{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\rho}}P_{L}^{\dot{\rho}\dot{\beta}}\delta_{ab},

(188)

\displaystyle\left(U_{\Psi_{f}B}\right)_{\dot{\beta}ai\mu}

\displaystyle=\left(U_{B\Psi_{f}}\right)_{\mu\dot{\beta}ai}=-\frac{g_{1}}{2}\cc_{\dot{\beta}\dot{\rho}}\gamma^{\mu}_{\dot{\rho}\dot{\sigma}}Y_{\Psi_{f}}^{\dot{\sigma}\dot{\lambda}}\Psi_{fi}^{\dot{\lambda}a}+\frac{g_{1}}{2}\Psi_{fi}^{\dot{\lambda}a}\cc_{\dot{\lambda}\dot{\rho}}\gamma^{\mu}_{\dot{\rho}\dot{\sigma}}Y_{\Psi_{f}}^{\dot{\sigma}\dot{\beta}},

(189)

\displaystyle\left(U_{\Psi_{f}W}\right)_{\dot{\beta}ai\mu A}

\displaystyle=\left(U_{W\Psi_{f}}\right)_{\mu A\dot{\beta}ai}=-\frac{g_{2}}{2}\cc_{\dot{\beta}\dot{\rho}}\gamma^{\mu}_{\dot{\rho}\dot{\sigma}}\left(T^{A}_{\Psi_{f}}\right)^{ab}_{\dot{\sigma}\dot{\lambda}}\Psi_{fi}^{\dot{\lambda}b}+\frac{g_{2}}{2}\Psi_{fi}^{\dot{\lambda}b}\cc_{\dot{\lambda}\dot{\rho}}\gamma^{\mu}_{\dot{\rho}\dot{\sigma}}\left(T^{A}_{\Psi_{f}}\right)_{\dot{\sigma}\dot{\beta}}^{ba}.

(190)

Finally we have the matrix of purely fermionic derivatives

\displaystyle\mathbf{U}_{\xi\xi}

\displaystyle=\cc^{-1}_{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}}\begin{pmatrix}0&\left(U_{\Psi_{q}\Psi_{u}}\right)_{\dot{\beta}ai\dot{\rho}j}&\left(U_{\Psi_{q}\Psi_{d}}\right)_{\dot{\beta}ai\dot{\rho}j}&0&0\\
\left(U_{\Psi_{u}\Psi_{q}}\right)_{\dot{\beta}i\dot{\rho}bj}&0&0&0&0\\
\left(U_{\Psi_{d}\Psi_{q}}\right)_{\dot{\beta}i\dot{\rho}bj}&0&0&0&0\\
0&0&0&0&\left(U_{\Psi_{l}\Psi_{e}}\right)_{\dot{\beta}ai\dot{\rho}j}\\
0&0&0&\left(U_{\Psi_{e}\Psi_{l}}\right)_{\dot{\beta}i\dot{\rho}bj}&0\end{pmatrix},

(191)

where the nonzero entries are given by

\displaystyle\left(U_{\Psi_{q}\Psi_{u}}\right)_{\dot{\beta}ai\dot{\rho}j}

\displaystyle=-\left(U_{\Psi_{u}\Psi_{q}}\right)_{\dot{\rho}j\dot{\beta}ai}=-y_{u}^{ij}\cc_{\dot{\beta}\dot{\sigma}}\epsilon_{ab}H^{*}_{b}P_{R}^{\dot{\sigma}\dot{\rho}}+(y^{*}_{u})^{ij}\cc_{\dot{\rho}\dot{\sigma}}\epsilon_{ab}H_{b}P_{L}^{\dot{\sigma}\dot{\beta}},

(192)

\displaystyle\left(U_{\Psi_{q}\Psi_{d}}\right)_{\dot{\beta}ai\dot{\rho}j}

\displaystyle=-\left(U_{\Psi_{d}\Psi_{q}}\right)_{\dot{\rho}j\dot{\beta}ai}=-y_{d}^{ij}\cc_{\dot{\beta}\dot{\sigma}}H_{a}P_{R}^{\dot{\sigma}\dot{\rho}}+(y^{*}_{d})^{ij}\cc_{\dot{\rho}\dot{\sigma}}H^{*}_{a}P_{L}^{\dot{\sigma}\dot{\beta}},

(193)

\displaystyle\left(U_{\Psi_{l}\Psi_{e}}\right)_{\dot{\beta}ai\dot{\rho}j}

\displaystyle=-\left(U_{\Psi_{e}\Psi_{l}}\right)_{\dot{\rho}j\dot{\beta}ai}=-y_{e}^{ij}\cc_{\dot{\beta}\dot{\sigma}}H_{a}P_{R}^{\dot{\sigma}\dot{\rho}}+(y^{*}_{e})^{ij}\cc_{\dot{\rho}\dot{\sigma}}H^{*}_{a}P_{L}^{\dot{\sigma}\dot{\beta}}.

(194)

In the bosonic sector there are also contributions from open covariant derivatives. The nonzero matrix entries of
\mathbf{Z}_{\phi\phi}^{\mu}
are

\displaystyle\left(Z^{\mu}_{H^{*}B}\right)_{a\nu}

\displaystyle=-\frac{g_{1}}{2}H_{a}g^{\mu}_{\nu},

(195)

\displaystyle\left(Z^{\mu}_{H^{*}W}\right)_{a\nu A}

\displaystyle=-g_{2}T^{A}_{ab}H_{b}g^{\mu}_{\nu},

(196)

\displaystyle\left(Z^{\mu}_{HB}\right)_{a\nu}

\displaystyle=\frac{g_{1}}{2}H^{*}_{a}g^{\mu}_{\nu},

(197)

\displaystyle\left(Z^{\mu}_{HW}\right)_{a\nu A}

\displaystyle=g_{2}T^{A}_{ba}H^{*}_{b}g^{\mu}_{\nu}.

(198)

As discussed in Section
5

\tofu
computes these matrices automatically and in the matching step inserts the classical fields where appropriate. For this particular model the matrices were also computed by hand as a check. There are 500 BSUOLEA operators contributing to the matching, highlighting that hand calculation is not feasible. The reduction of the redundant effective Lagrangian to a set of non-redundant dimension six operators was performed in two different ways.

In the first approach the feature of
\tofu
was used which allows for the identification of custom operators and the implementation of replacement rules among these. This is most easily realised by writing the SM Lagrangian in terms of the Majorana spinors
\Psi_{f}
as in the SM part on the r.h.s. of Eq. (
5
) and then applying the equations of motion compatible with this Lagrangian to the matching result. These equations of motion read

\displaystyle-D^{2}H+\mu_{h}^{2}H-\lambda_{h}|H|^{2}H-\Psi_{q}y_{u}\epsilon P_{R}\Psi_{u}-\bar{\Psi}_{d}y_{d}^{\dagger}P_{L}\Psi_{q}-\bar{\Psi}_{e}y_{e}^{\dagger}P_{L}\Psi_{l}=0,

(199)

\displaystyle\left(D^{\mu}W_{\mu\nu}\right)^{A}+g_{2}\left(\bar{\Psi}_{q}T^{A}_{\Psi_{q}}\Psi_{q}+\bar{\Psi}_{l}T^{A}_{\Psi_{l}}\Psi_{l}+\frac{i}{2}H^{\dagger}\overleftrightarrow{D}^{A}H\right)=0,

(200)

\displaystyle D^{\mu}B_{\mu\nu}+g_{1}\sum\limits_{f\in\{u,d,q,e,l\}}\bar{\Psi}_{f}Y_{\Psi_{f}}\gamma_{\nu}\Psi_{f}+\frac{i}{2}H^{\dagger}\overleftrightarrow{D}_{\nu}H=0,

(201)

\displaystyle i\cc\not{D}\Psi_{q}-Hy_{d}\cc P_{R}\Psi_{d}+\bar{\Psi}_{d}y_{d}^{\dagger}P_{L}H^{\dagger}-\tilde{H}y_{u}\cc P_{R}\Psi_{u}+\bar{\Psi}_{u}y_{u}^{\dagger}P_{L}\tilde{H}^{\dagger}=0,

(202)

\displaystyle i\cc\not{D}\Psi_{u}+\bar{\Psi}_{q}y_{u}\tilde{H}P_{R}-\tilde{H}^{\dagger}y_{u}^{\dagger}\cc P_{L}\Psi_{q}=0,

(203)

\displaystyle i\cc\not{D}\Psi_{d}+\bar{\Psi}_{q}y_{d}HP_{R}-H^{\dagger}y_{d}^{\dagger}\cc P_{L}\Psi_{q}=0,

(204)

\displaystyle i\cc\not{D}\Psi_{l}-Hy_{e}\cc P_{R}\Psi_{e}+\bar{\Psi}_{e}y_{e}^{\dagger}P_{L}H^{\dagger}=0,

(205)

\displaystyle i\cc\not{D}\Psi_{e}+\bar{\Psi}_{l}y_{e}HP_{R}-H^{\dagger}y_{e}^{\dagger}\cc P_{L}\Psi_{l}=0,

(206)

and the dimension six operators that are generated in the matching are

\displaystyle Q_{H\Box}

\displaystyle=|H|^{2}\Box|H|^{2},

(207)

\displaystyle Q_{H}

\displaystyle=|H|^{6},

(208)

\displaystyle Q_{HD}

\displaystyle=\left(H^{\dagger}D_{\mu}H\right)^{\dagger}\left(H^{\dagger}D^{\mu}H\right),

(209)

\displaystyle Q_{HB}

\displaystyle=|H|^{2}B_{\mu\nu}B^{\mu\nu},

(210)

\displaystyle Q_{HW}

\displaystyle=|H|^{2}W^{A}_{\mu\nu}W^{\mu\nu A},

(211)

\displaystyle Q_{HWB}

\displaystyle=\left(H^{\dagger}\tau^{A}H\right)W^{A}_{\mu\nu}B^{\mu\nu},

(212)

\displaystyle Q_{uH}

\displaystyle=|H|^{2}\left(\bar{q}\tilde{H}u\right)=|H|^{2}\left(\bar{\Psi}_{q}\tilde{H}P_{R}\Psi_{u}\right),

(213)

\displaystyle Q_{dH}

\displaystyle=|H|^{2}\left(\bar{q}Hd\right)=|H|^{2}\left(\bar{\Psi}_{q}\tilde{H}P_{R}\Psi_{d}\right),

(214)

\displaystyle Q_{eH}

\displaystyle=|H|^{2}\left(\bar{l}He\right)=|H|^{2}\left(\bar{\Psi}_{l}\tilde{H}P_{R}\Psi_{e}\right),

(215)

\displaystyle Q_{Hu}

\displaystyle=\left(H^{\dagger}i\overleftrightarrow{D}_{\mu}H\right)\left(\bar{u}\gamma^{\mu}u\right)=\left(H^{\dagger}i\overleftrightarrow{D}_{\mu}H\right)\left(\bar{\Psi}_{u}\gamma^{\mu}P_{R}\Psi_{u}\right),

(216)

\displaystyle Q_{Hd}

\displaystyle=\left(H^{\dagger}i\overleftrightarrow{D}_{\mu}H\right)\left(\bar{d}\gamma^{\mu}d\right)=\left(H^{\dagger}i\overleftrightarrow{D}_{\mu}H\right)\left(\bar{\Psi}_{d}\gamma^{\mu}P_{R}\Psi_{d}\right),

(217)

\displaystyle Q_{Hud}

\displaystyle=\left(\tilde{H}^{\dagger}iD_{\mu}H\right)\left(\bar{u}\gamma^{\mu}d\right)=\left(\tilde{H}^{\dagger}iD_{\mu}H\right)\left(\bar{\Psi}_{u}\gamma^{\mu}P_{R}\Psi_{d}\right),

(218)

\displaystyle Q_{He}

\displaystyle=\left(H^{\dagger}i\overleftrightarrow{D}_{\mu}H\right)\left(\bar{e}\gamma^{\mu}e\right)=\left(H^{\dagger}i\overleftrightarrow{D}_{\mu}H\right)\left(\bar{\Psi}_{e}\gamma^{\mu}P_{R}\Psi_{e}\right),

(219)

\displaystyle Q_{Hq}^{\left(1\right)}

\displaystyle=\left(H^{\dagger}i\overleftrightarrow{D}_{\mu}H\right)\left(\bar{q}\gamma^{\mu}q\right)=\left(H^{\dagger}i\overleftrightarrow{D}_{\mu}H\right)\left(\bar{\Psi}_{q}\gamma^{\mu}P_{L}\Psi_{q}\right),

(220)

\displaystyle Q_{Hq}^{\left(3\right)}

\displaystyle=\left(H^{\dagger}i\overleftrightarrow{D}^{A}_{\mu}H\right)\left(\bar{q}\sigma^{A}\gamma^{\mu}q\right)=\left(H^{\dagger}i\overleftrightarrow{D}^{A}_{\mu}H\right)\left(\bar{\Psi}_{q}\sigma^{A}\gamma^{\mu}\Psi_{q}\right),

(221)

\displaystyle Q_{Hl}^{\left(1\right)}

\displaystyle=\left(H^{\dagger}i\overleftrightarrow{D}_{\mu}H\right)\left(\bar{l}\gamma^{\mu}l\right)=\left(H^{\dagger}i\overleftrightarrow{D}_{\mu}H\right)\left(\bar{\Psi}_{l}\gamma^{\mu}P_{L}\Psi_{l}\right),

(222)

\displaystyle Q_{Hl}^{\left(3\right)}

\displaystyle=\left(H^{\dagger}i\overleftrightarrow{D}^{A}_{\mu}H\right)\left(\bar{l}\sigma^{A}\gamma^{\mu}l\right)=\left(H^{\dagger}i\overleftrightarrow{D}^{A}_{\mu}H\right)\left(\bar{\Psi}_{l}\sigma^{A}\gamma^{\mu}\Psi_{l}\right),

(223)

\displaystyle Q_{2y}

\displaystyle=|\bar{q}^{b}y_{u}u\epsilon_{ba}+\bar{d}y_{d}^{\dagger}q^{a}+\bar{e}y_{e}^{\dagger}l^{a}|^{2}=|\bar{\Psi}^{b}_{q}P_{R}y_{u}\Psi_{u}\epsilon_{ba}+\bar{\Psi}_{d}y_{d}^{\dagger}P_{L}\Psi_{q}^{a}+\bar{\Psi}_{e}y_{e}^{\dagger}\Psi_{l}^{a}|^{2},

(224)

where the last operator
Q_{2y}
is a combination of several four-fermion operators in the Warsaw basis, see Ref.
[
110
]
. We here introduced the Pauli matrices
\tau^{A}
and the Hermitian derivatives

\displaystyle H^{\dagger}i\overleftrightarrow{D}^{\mu}H

\displaystyle=H^{\dagger}iD^{\mu}H-\left(D^{\mu}H\right)^{\dagger}iH,

(225)

\displaystyle H^{\dagger}i\overleftrightarrow{D}^{A}_{\mu}H

\displaystyle=H^{\dagger}i\tau^{A}D_{\mu}H-\left(D_{\mu}H\right)^{\dagger}i\tau^{A}H.

(226)

The Wilson coefficients corresponding to these dimension six operators are found to be in agreement with the ones cited in Ref.
[
48
]
and are therefore not repeated here, with one exception, namely the presence of the additional operator
Q_{Hud}
and its Hermitian conjugate. This operator was originally missed both in Ref.
[
48
]
and in Ref.
[
49
]
since it is obtained from correlation functions contributing to
HH\rightarrow\bar{f}^{{}^{\prime}}f
, which were not calculated in the matching of those references. The Wilson coefficient at
Q_{\text{match}}=M
is given by

\displaystyle C_{Hud}=-\frac{5A^{2}}{4M^{4}}y_{u}^{\dagger}y_{d},

(227)

which has been confirmed by the authors of Ref.
[
48
]
. The fact that this operator was missed twice using diagrammatic techniques highlights a strength of the UOLEA approach: It is impossible to miss a contribution to the matching once a sufficiently general UOLEA has been computed. This is due to the fact that the effective Lagrangian is fully generated from the UV Lagrangian through a matching condition which, by construction, includes all correlation functions relevant to the matching. It is not necessary to know the effective Lagrangian prior to the matching and to construct a matching condition that captures all possible contributions to the different operators contained in that effective Lagrangian.

In the second approach
\tofu
was again used to perform the matching and the raw, redundant result was passed to
\route
, which first translates operators containing the Majorana fermions
\Psi_{f}
into the usual SM notation and then reduces the set of structures to the Warsaw basis. The result of this computation matches the result of the previous calculation once
Q_{2y}
is translated into the Warsaw basis. Note that this illustrates the main difference between the two approaches. The first approach is flexible in the sense that one can use custom operators or operator bases. This also implies more work since an algorithm has to be devised which translates redundant structures into these bases without being circular. The benefit of using
\route
is that it is guaranteed to work and terminate. The cost is that one always ends up in the Warsaw basis and that this is limited to the SMEFT.

Chapter 4
Matching of a triplet benchmark model

In this chapter we apply the functional matching formalism to a specific simplified model, which is not covered by the BSUOLEA, described by the Lagrangian

\displaystyle\LagNoT={}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4}V^{\mu\nu A}V_{\mu\nu}^{A}+\frac{m_{V}^{2}}{2}V^{\mu A}V_{\mu}^{A}+V^{\mu A}\left(\gamma_{H}g_{*}J_{\mu}^{HA}+\gamma_{V}\frac{g_{2}}{g_{*}}J_{\mu}^{A}+\sum_{f\in\{l,q\}}\gamma_{f}g_{*}J_{\mu}^{fA}\right)

\displaystyle+\Lag{SM},

(1)

where
\Lag{SM}
is the SM Lagrangian,
V^{\mu A}
is a
SU(2)_{L}
triplet vector field and the currents are given by

\displaystyle J_{\mu}^{HA}

\displaystyle=\frac{i}{2}H^{\dagger}\overleftrightarrow{D}^{A}_{\mu}H,

(2)

\displaystyle J_{\mu}^{A}

\displaystyle=\left(D^{\nu}W_{\nu\mu}\right)^{A},

(3)

\displaystyle J_{\mu}^{fA}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\bar{f}\gamma_{\mu}\tau^{A}f,

(4)

with
\tau^{A}

A=1,2,3
being the Pauli matrices. The field strength tensor
V^{\mu\nu A}
is defined as

\displaystyle V^{\mu\nu A}=D^{[\mu}V^{\nu]A}=D^{\mu}V^{\nu A}-D^{\nu}V^{\mu A},

(5)

and the SM conventions are the same as in Section
5
. This model was introduced in Ref.
[
111
]
and is a special case of the model discussed in Ref.
[
112
]
. The explicit mapping is given in Section
3
and the matching presented in that section also applies to the more general model. Phenomenologically, these models are interesting for two reasons. Firstly, the model of Ref.
[
112
]
acts as a simplified model that accurately describes several UV completions of the SM. In Ref.
[
112
]
it was shown explicitly that both the weakly coupled UV completion of the SM discussed in Ref.
[
113
]
and the Minimal Composite Higgs model discussed in Refs.
[
114
,
115
]
can be mapped onto this simplified model under the assumption that the remainder of the NP sector is heavy compared to the vector field
V^{\mu A}
. In Ref.
[
111
]
the model defined by Eq. (
1
) was studied in the context of SMEFT fits. It was argued that the extended energy reach of the LHC can lead to an increased sensitivity to some higher dimensional operators, the effects of which are enhanced by powers of
E/\Lambda
, where
E
is some energy scale in the process under consideration and
\Lambda
is the scale of NP. This enhancement is most pronounced in kinematic regions where one might suspect the EFT expansion to have broken down, rendering any derived constraints inconsistent. However, whether or not this is the case cannot be inferred by only considering the general SMEFT Lagrangian since the Wilson coefficients cannot be disentangled from the unknown NP scale
\Lambda
. Nevertheless, in specific UV completions the scale
\Lambda
is known and has a clear physical meaning. It is the pole mass of the lightest particle in the NP sector. The question then becomes whether there are specific UV completions of the SM for which the apparently inconsistent constraints are meaningful. One way of realising an energy enhancement without breaking the EFT assumptions is to have large Wilson coefficients, which can be seen from the following argument presented in Ref.
[
111
]
. Assume that the dimension six operator
\op
with Wilson coefficient
C_{\op}
is enhanced by
E^{2}/\Lambda^{2}
and let
\Lambda
be the pole mass of the lightest particle in the NP sector. With the naive expectation of
C_{\op}\sim 1
the EFT expansion indeed breaks down as
E/\Lambda\to 1
and no enhancement arises. However, if
C_{\op}=g_{e}^{2}\hat{C}_{\op}
, where
\hat{C}_{\op}\sim 1
and the enhancement factor
g_{e}^{2}>1
, we get an effective suppression scale
f=\Lambda/g_{e}
such that
C_{\op}\op/\Lambda^{2}=\hat{C}_{\op}\op/f^{2}
and the energy enhancement becomes
E^{2}/f^{2}
rather than
E^{2}/\Lambda^{2}
. Nothing special happens as
E/f\to 1
and the EFT expansion is perfectly valid in the region where the operator is enhanced (as long as
E<\Lambda
is satisfied). Clearly, this argument crucially depends on the fact that the scale
\Lambda
has a well defined physical meaning, which it only carries once a certain UV completion has been specified. It was precisely this effect that was studied at tree level in Ref.
[
111
]
with
g_{*}
playing the role of the enhancement. Since it is necessary that
g_{*}>1
Eq. (
1
) was interpreted as the effective description of a composite sector and it was shown that in this case an enhancement does occur for certain operator combinations that contribute to Higgs physics.

Given the fact that the model defined by Eq. (
1
) (or rather its generalisation as given in Ref.
[
112
]
) can be interpreted as the effective description of several interesting UV completions of the SM at an intermediate scale it is an interesting benchmark model to study. Taking into account that parametrically large Wilson coefficients can occur when the model is interpreted as the effective description of a composite sector, this latter scenario becomes particularly interesting.
