X_{\theta\sigma}&X^{\nu}_{\theta w}&X_{\theta\sigma^{*}}&X^{\nu}_{\theta w^{*}}&X_{\theta\theta}&X^{\nu}_{\theta v}\\
-X^{\mu}_{v\sigma}&-X^{\mu\nu}_{vw}&-X^{\mu}_{v\sigma^{*}}&-X^{\mu\nu}_{vw^{*}}&-X^{\mu}_{v\theta}&-X^{\mu\nu}_{vv}\end{pmatrix},

where

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\mathbf{X}_{AB}=\mathbf{U}_{AB}+P_{\mu}\mathbf{Z}_{AB}^{\mu}+\mathbf{Z}^{\dagger\mu}_{AB}P_{\mu},\text{ if }A\wedge B\in\{\phi,\Phi\}\\
\mathbf{X}_{AB}=\mathbf{U}_{AB}\text{ otherwise}\end{cases}.

(1)

In the operators published at
[
87
]
we further defined

\displaystyle\mathbf{U}_{AB}

\displaystyle=U[\text{A},\text{B}],

(2)

\displaystyle\mathbf{Z}_{AB}^{\mu}

\displaystyle=Z[\text{A},\text{B}][\text{lor}],

(3)

\displaystyle\mathbf{Z}_{AB}^{\dagger\mu}

\displaystyle=ZT[\text{A},\text{B}][\text{lor}].

(4)

Finally, covariant derivatives and field strength tensors are denoted by
P[\text{lor}]
and

G[\text{A},\text{A}][\text{lor1},\text{lor2}]
, respectively. The field type A in the field strength tensor defines which covariant derivative is to be used in its construction. For example,
G[\text{F},\text{F}]
is obtained from the commutator of two covariant derivatives acting on the heavy fermion multiplet
\Xi
. A commutator is denoted by
c
, for instance
c[P[\text{lor}],U[\text{A},\text{B}]]=[P^{\mu},\mathbf{U}_{AB}]
, where the apropriate covariant derivative is the one acting on multiplet
A
.

Chapter 8
Proof of consistency of shifts

In this appendix we discuss the consistency of the shifts given in Eqs. (
98
) and (
99
). The treatment of the shifts given in
Eqs. (
104
) and (
105
) is analogous. Since

\xi
is a multiplet of Majorana-like component spinors,
for the shifts

\displaystyle\delta\xi^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\delta\xi+\mathbf{\Delta}_{\xi}^{-1}\left[\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\xi\Xi}\delta\Xi-\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\xi\Phi}\delta\Phi-\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\xi\phi}\delta\phi\right],

(1)

\displaystyle\delta\xi^{\prime T}

\displaystyle=\delta\xi^{T}+\left[\delta\Xi^{T}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\Xi\xi}+\delta\Phi^{T}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\Phi\xi}+\delta\phi^{T}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\phi\xi}\right]\overleftarrow{\mathbf{\Delta}}_{\xi}^{-1},

(2)

to be consistent it is necessary and sufficient that

\displaystyle\left(\mathbf{\Delta}_{\xi}^{-1}\left[\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\xi\Xi}\delta\Xi-\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\xi\Phi}\delta\Phi-\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\xi\phi}\delta\phi\right]\right)^{T}=\left[\delta\Xi^{T}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\Xi\xi}+\delta\Phi^{T}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\Phi\xi}+\delta\phi^{T}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\phi\xi}\right]\overleftarrow{\mathbf{\Delta}}_{\xi}^{-1}.

(3)

In the following we show that (
3
) holds.
We first construct
\mathbf{\Delta}_{\xi}^{-1}
in position space through
its Neumann series

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Delta}_{\xi}^{-1}(x,y)

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\left(\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=1\\
n>0\end{subarray}}^{n}\int\rd^{d}x_{i}\;\mathbf{S}(x_{i-1},x_{i})\left(-\mathbf{X}_{\xi\xi}(x_{i})\right)\right)\mathbf{S}(x_{n},y)\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{F}\cc^{-1}

\displaystyle\equiv\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\left(\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=1\\
n>0\end{subarray}}^{n}\mathbf{S}_{x_{i-1}x_{i}}\left(-\mathbf{X}_{\xi\xi x_{i}}\right)\right)\mathbf{S}_{x_{n}y}\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{F}\cc^{-1},

(4)

where
x_{0}\equiv x
and
\mathbf{S}(x,y)
is the matrix-valued Green’s
function for
(\not{P}-M_{\xi})
, which itself can be expressed
through a Neumann series. We may write

(\not{P}-M_{\xi})=(i\not{\partial}-M_{\xi}-\mathbf{A})
with

\displaystyle\mathbf{A}=i\sum_{j}g_{j}\not{A}_{j}^{a}T_{j}^{a},

(5)

where we sum over all factors of the gauge group for a direct product
group and
T_{j}^{a}
is a block-diagonal matrix which generates the
reducible representation of
\xi
. Due to the fact that
\xi
contains

\omega
,
\ccfield{\omega}
and
\lambda
(see Table
2
),
the generator is of the form

\displaystyle T^{a}=\begin{pmatrix}T^{a}_{R(\omega)}&&0&&0\\
0&&T^{a}_{\bar{R}(\omega)}&&0\\
0&&0&&T^{a}_{R(\lambda)}\end{pmatrix},

(6)

where
R(\omega)
is the representation under which
\omega

transforms,
\bar{R}(\omega)
its conjugate representation and

R(\lambda)
is the representation of
\lambda
, which is necessarily
real. We then have

\displaystyle\mathbf{S}_{xy}=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\left(\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=1\\
k>0\end{subarray}}^{k}\mathbf{S}_{f,x_{i-1}x_{i}}\mathbf{A}_{x_{i}}\right)\mathbf{S}_{f,x_{k}y},

(7)

where again
x_{0}\equiv x
and
\mathbf{S}_{f,xy}
is the matrix
containing the Green’s function of the free Dirac equation on its
diagonal. It can be verified by explicit calculation that

\displaystyle\mathbf{S}_{xy}\left(-i\overleftarrow{\not{\partial}_{y}}-M_{\xi}-\mathbf{A}_{y}\right)

\displaystyle=\delta_{xy},

(8)

which means that

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Delta}_{\xi,xy}^{-1}\overleftarrow{\mathbf{\Delta}}_{\xi,y}=\delta_{xy}

(9)

and therefore

\overleftarrow{\mathbf{\Delta}}_{\xi,yx}^{-1}=\mathbf{\Delta}_{\xi,yx}^{-1}
. Hence (
3
)
reads

\displaystyle\left(\mathbf{\Delta}_{\xi,xy}^{-1}\left[\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\xi\Xi}\delta\Xi-\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\xi\Phi}\delta\Phi-\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\xi\phi}\delta\phi\right]_{y}\right)^{T}=\left[\delta\Xi^{T}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\Xi\xi}+\delta\Phi^{T}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\Phi\xi}+\delta\phi^{T}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\phi\xi}\right]_{y}\mathbf{\Delta}_{\xi,yx}^{-1}.

(10)

It is then useful to calculate

\displaystyle\cc\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{F}\mathbf{S}^{T}_{xy}

\displaystyle=\cc\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{F}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\mathbf{S}^{T}_{f,x_{k}y}\left(\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=k\\
k>0\end{subarray}}^{1}\mathbf{A}^{T}_{x_{i}}\mathbf{S}^{T}_{f,x_{i-1}x_{i}}\right)

\displaystyle=\cc\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{F}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\cc\mathbf{S}_{f,yx_{k}}\cc^{-1}\left(\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=k\\
k>0\end{subarray}}^{1}\mathbf{A}^{T}_{x_{i}}\cc\mathbf{S}_{f,x_{i}x_{i-1}}\cc^{-1}\right)

\displaystyle=-\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{F}\mathbf{S}_{f,yx_{k}}\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{F}\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{F}\left(\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=k\\
k>0\end{subarray}}^{1}(-\mathbf{A}^{t}_{x_{i}})\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{F}\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{F}\mathbf{S}_{f,x_{i}x_{i-1}}\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{F}\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{F}\right)\cc^{-1}

\displaystyle=-\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\mathbf{S}_{f,yx_{k}}\left(\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=k\\
k>0\end{subarray}}^{1}(-\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{F}\mathbf{A}^{t}_{x_{i}}\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{F})\mathbf{S}_{f,x_{i}x_{i-1}}\right)\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{F}\cc^{-1}

\displaystyle=-\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\mathbf{S}_{f,yx_{k}}\left(\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=k\\
k>0\end{subarray}}^{1}\mathbf{A}_{x_{i}}\mathbf{S}_{f,x_{i}x_{i-1}}\right)\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{F}\cc^{-1}

\displaystyle=-\mathbf{S}_{yx}\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{F}\cc^{-1},

(11)

where
\mathbf{A}^{t}
means taking the transpose of the gauge group
generators only and we used that

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{F}\begin{pmatrix}A&&0&&0\\
0&&B&&0\\
0&&0&&C\end{pmatrix}\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{F}=\begin{pmatrix}B&&0&&0\\
0&&A&&0\\
0&&0&&C\end{pmatrix}.

(12)

We note in passing that Eq. (
8
) also holds if the generators contain projectors in spinor space, since the charge conjugation operator commutes with these projectors in the chiral representation, which we have adopted. Thus, this also covers the construction of Section
5
. We now find

\displaystyle\left(\mathbf{\Delta}_{\xi,xy}^{-1}\right)^{T}

\displaystyle=\cc\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{F}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\mathbf{S}_{x_{n},y}^{T}\left(\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=1\\
n>0\end{subarray}}^{n}\left(-\mathbf{X}_{\xi\xi,x_{i}}\right)^{T}\mathbf{S}_{x_{i-1}x_{i}}^{T}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\mathbf{S}_{yx_{n}}\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{F}\cc\left(\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=1\\
n>0\end{subarray}}^{n}\left(-\mathbf{X}_{\xi\xi,x_{i}}\right)^{T}\mathbf{S}_{x_{i-1}x_{i}}^{T}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\mathbf{S}_{yx_{n}}\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{F}\cc\left(\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=1\\
n>0\end{subarray}}^{n}\left(-\mathbf{X}_{\xi\xi,x_{i}}\right)^{T}\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{F}\cc^{-1}\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{F}\cc\mathbf{S}_{x_{i-1}x_{i}}^{T}\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{F}\cc^{-1}\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{F}\cc\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\mathbf{S}_{yx_{n}}\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{F}\cc\left(\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=1\\
n>0\end{subarray}}^{n}\left(-\mathbf{X}_{\xi\xi,x_{i}}\right)^{T}\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{F}\cc^{-1}\mathbf{S}_{x_{i}x_{i-1}}\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{F}\cc\right)

\displaystyle=-\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\mathbf{S}_{yx_{n}}\left(\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=1\\
n>0\end{subarray}}^{n}\left(-\mathbf{X}_{\xi\xi,x_{i}}\right)\mathbf{S}_{x_{i}x_{i-1}}\right)\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{F}\cc^{-1}

\displaystyle=-\mathbf{\Delta}_{\xi yx}^{-1},

(13)

where we used that

\displaystyle\cc\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{F}\mathbf{X}^{T}_{\xi\xi}\tilde{\mathds{1}}_{F}\cc^{-1}=\mathbf{X}_{\xi\xi}.

(14)

Noting that

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbf{X}}^{T}_{\xi\Xi}

\displaystyle=-\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\Xi\xi},

(15)

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbf{X}}^{T}_{\xi\Phi}

\displaystyle=\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\Phi\xi},

(16)

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbf{X}}^{T}_{\xi\phi}

\displaystyle=\tilde{\mathbf{X}}_{\phi\xi},

(17)

the validity of (
3
) follows immediately.

Chapter 9
Loop functions

The integrals
\ZI[q^{2n_{c}}]^{n_{i}n_{j}\dots n_{L}}_{ij\dots 0}
are defined as in Ref.
[
61
]
, that is

\displaystyle\int\frac{\rd^{d}q}{(2\pi)^{d}}\frac{q^{\mu_{1}}q^{\mu_{2}}\dots q^{\mu_{2n_{c}}}}{(q^{2}-M_{i}^{2})^{n_{i}}(q^{2}-M_{j}^{2})^{n_{j}}\dots(q^{2})^{n_{L}}}\equiv\frac{i}{16\pi^{2}}g^{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}\dots\mu_{2n_{c}}}\ZI[q^{2n_{c}}]^{n_{i}n_{j}\dots n_{L}}_{ij\dots 0},

(1)

where
g^{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}\dots\mu_{2n_{c}}}
is the completely symmetric
combination of metric tensors with
2n_{c}
indices, for instance

g^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}=g^{\mu\nu}g^{\rho\sigma}+g^{\mu\rho}g^{\nu\sigma}+g^{\mu\sigma}g^{\nu\rho}
. For
n_{c}=0
we define the
shorthand notation

\ZI[q^{0}]^{n_{i}n_{j}\dots n_{L}}_{ij\dots 0}\equiv\ZI^{n_{i}n_{j}\dots n_{L}}_{ij\dots 0}
. The integrals can be reduced to basis
integrals using the following reduction relations, which were already stated in Ref.
[
61
]

\displaystyle\ZI[q^{2n_{c}}]^{n_{i}n_{j}\dots n_{L}}_{ij\dots 0}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\Delta^{2}_{ij}}\left(\ZI[q^{2n_{c}}]^{n_{i}n_{j}-1\dots n_{L}}-\ZI[q^{2n_{c}}]^{n_{i}-1\,n_{j}\dots n_{L}}\right),

(2)

\displaystyle\ZI[q^{2n_{c}}]^{n_{i}n_{j}\dots n_{L}}_{ij\dots 0}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{M_{i}^{2}}\left(\ZI[q^{2n_{c}}]^{n_{i}n_{j}\dots n_{L}-1}-\ZI[q^{2n_{c}}]^{n_{i}-1\,n_{j}\dots n_{L}}\right),

(3)

where
\Delta^{2}_{ij}=M_{i}^{2}-M_{j}^{2}
. The basis integrals can be computed using the formula

\displaystyle\ZI[q^{2n_{c}}]^{n_{i}n_{L}}_{i0}={}

\displaystyle\left(-M_{i}^{2}\right)^{2+n_{c}-n_{i}-n_{L}}\frac{1}{2^{n_{c}}(n_{i}-1)!}\frac{\Gamma(\frac{\epsilon}{2}-2-n_{c}+n_{i}+n_{L})}{\Gamma(\frac{\epsilon}{2})}

\displaystyle\times\frac{\Gamma(-\frac{\epsilon}{2}+2+n_{c}-n_{L})}{\Gamma(-\frac{\epsilon}{2}+2+n_{c})}\left(\frac{2}{\bar{\epsilon}}-\log\frac{M_{i}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right),

(4)

where
\epsilon=4-d
and
\frac{2}{\bar{\epsilon}}=\frac{2}{\epsilon}-\gamma+\log 4\pi
.
In the lists of BSUOLEA operators found at
[
87
]
the integrals are denoted by

\displaystyle J[n_{c},\{\{M_{i},n_{i}\},\{M_{j},n_{j}\},\ldots\},n_{L}]\equiv\ZI[q^{2n_{c}}]^{n_{i}n_{j}\dots n_{L}}_{ij\dots 0},

(5)

and in the code
\tofu
the insertion of the correct integrals is performed automatically.

Chapter 10
The Berezin algebra

Appendix 10.A
Generalities

In this appendix we introduce some properties of the Berezin algebra which are used throughout this thesis. For more details we refer the reader to Appendix D of Ref.
[
116
]
and to Ref.
[
124
]
. We will define the Berezin algebra
B
as the algebra over the field
\mathds{C}
generated by the elements
z^{a}=(\theta^{i},\xi^{\alpha})
, where the generating elements
\xi^{\alpha}
satisfy

\displaystyle\xi^{\alpha}\xi^{\beta}+\xi^{\beta}\xi^{\alpha}=0,

and any
b\in B
may be written as

\displaystyle b=f_{0}(\theta)+f_{\alpha}(\theta)\xi^{\alpha}+\ldots+f_{\alpha_{1}\ldots\alpha_{n}}(\theta)\xi^{\alpha_{1}}\cdots\xi^{\alpha_{n}},

(1)

where the functions
f_{{\alpha_{1}\ldots\alpha_{n}}}
are anti-symmetric in their indices. Any
b\in B
may be written as an expansion in
z^{a}
by Taylor expanding these functions. We say that an element of
B
is Grassmann odd if its representation of the form given in Eq. (
1
) only contains odd powers of
\xi
. Similarly, any element of
B
whose representation only contains even powers of
\xi
will be referred to as Grassmann even. For an element
b\in B
we introduce the parity
P_{b}
as

\displaystyle\begin{cases}P_{b}=1,\text{ if $b$ is Grassmann odd}\\
P_{b}=0,\text{ if $b$ is Grassmann even}\end{cases},

(2)

whenever this is well-defined. The only function of the parity
P
relevant for this thesis is
(-1)^{P}
and hence the parity of
b_{3}=b_{1}b_{2}
can be taken to be
P_{b_{3}}=P_{b_{1}}+P_{b_{2}}
, whenever
b_{1}
and
b_{2}
have definite parities. We also introduce the parity of the index
a
,
P_{(a)}
, corresponding to the element
z^{a}
, as

\displaystyle P_{(a)}=P_{z^{a}}.

(3)

This definition allows for the introduction of supermatrices as follows. A supermatrix
M
is a set of matrix elements
M_{ab}\in B
, with definite parity, together with a set of parities of its indices
\left(P_{(a)},P_{(b)}\right)
such that

\displaystyle P_{M_{ab}}=P_{(a)}+P_{(b)}.

(4)

For any supermatrix
M
we introduce the normal form
M^{(N)}
, which is obtained from
M
by simultaneous permutation of rows and columns so that
M^{(N)}
has a definite order of indices: first come all Grassmann even and then all Grassmann odd indices. This matrix can be represented as

\displaystyle M^{(N)}=\begin{pmatrix}\left(M_{1}\right)_{ij}&\left(M_{2}\right)_{i\beta}\\
\left(M_{3}\right)_{\alpha j}&\left(M_{4}\right)_{\alpha\beta}\end{pmatrix},

(5)

with
P_{(i)}=P_{(j)}=0
and
P_{(\alpha)}=P_{(\beta)}=1
. Given any supermatrix
M
we may define its supertrace
\Str\,M
and its superdeterminant
\Sdet\,M
as

\displaystyle\Str\,M

\displaystyle=\sum_{a}(-1)^{P_{(a)}}M_{aa},

(6)

\displaystyle\Sdet\,M

\displaystyle=\exp\Str\log\,M.

(7)

Several properties of these quantities can be found in Appendix D of Ref.
[
116
]
. The relevant ones for this thesis are

\displaystyle\Sdet\,M=\Sdet\,M^{(N)}

\displaystyle=\det M_{1}\det(M_{4}-M_{3}M_{1}^{-1}M_{2})^{-1}

(8)

\displaystyle=\det(M_{1}-M_{2}M_{4}^{-1}M_{3})\det M_{4}^{-1}.

(9)

We finally define the left and right derivatives w.r.t. generating elements. It is sufficient to define these on a product of generating elements. The left derivative is thus defined through

\displaystyle\frac{\partial_{l}}{\partial z^{a}}z^{a_{1}}z^{a_{2}}\ldots z^{a_{n}}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}(-1)^{\sum_{j=1}^{i-1}P_{(a)}P_{(a_{j})}}\delta^{a_{i}}_{a}z^{a_{1}}\ldots z^{a_{i-1}}z^{a_{i+1}}\ldots z^{a_{n}},

(10)

whereas the right derivative is defined through

\displaystyle\frac{\partial_{r}}{\partial z^{a}}z^{a_{1}}z^{a_{2}}\ldots z^{a_{n}}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}(-1)^{\sum_{j=n}^{i+1}P_{(a)}P_{(a_{j})}}\delta^{a_{i}}_{a}z^{a_{1}}\ldots z^{a_{i-1}}z^{a_{i+1}}\ldots z^{a_{n}}.

(11)

Appendix 10.B
The Poisson bracket in the Berezin algebra

In order to formulate a classical theory in which some of the degrees of freedom, collectively denoted by
q^{i}
, are Grassmann even and some Grassmann odd one has to generalise the Poisson bracket. Taking
q^{i}
and the associated conjugate momenta
p_{i}
as the generators of the Berezin algebra, any function
\mathcal{F}
of these variables is itself an element of the generated Berezin algebra. Then, considering the functions
\mathcal{F}
and
\mathcal{G}
of definite parity, we define their Poisson bracket to be

\displaystyle\{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}\}=\frac{\partial_{r}\mathcal{F}}{\partial q^{i}}\frac{\partial_{l}\mathcal{G}}{\partial p_{i}}-(-1)^{P_{\mathcal{F}}P_{\mathcal{G}}}\frac{\partial_{r}\mathcal{G}}{\partial q^{i}}\frac{\partial_{l}\mathcal{F}}{\partial p_{i}}.

(12)

In Eq. (
12
) a summation over the index
i
is implied. In the case of a field theory, the partial derivatives are to be interpreted as functional derivatives taken at a fixed time and the summation over
i
includes an integration over space. Two useful properties of this generalised Poisson bracket are

\displaystyle\{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}\}

\displaystyle=-(-1)^{P_{\mathcal{F}}P_{\mathcal{G}}}\{\mathcal{G},\mathcal{F}\},

(13)

\displaystyle\{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}\mathcal{K}\}

\displaystyle=\{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}\}\mathcal{K}+(-1)^{P_{\mathcal{F}}P_{\mathcal{G}}}\mathcal{G}\{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{K}\}.

(14)

Chapter 11
Some details of the constraint analysis

In this section we show the validity of Eq. (
37
), that is, we prove that

\displaystyle\{\Phi_{1(2)}^{A},H_{T}\}\approx-gf^{ABC}\left(W^{0B}\Phi^{C}_{1(2)}+V^{0B}\Phi^{C}_{2(2)}\right).

(1)

For convenience we repeat the two relevant constraints,

\displaystyle\Phi_{1(2)}^{A}={}

\displaystyle D^{i}\pi(W)_{i}^{A}+gf^{DAC}\pi(V)^{D}_{\mu}V^{\mu C}+\frac{i}{2}g\Hdagg\tau^{A}\pi(\Hdagg)-\frac{i}{2}g\pi(H)\tau^{A}H

\displaystyle-\frac{i}{2}g\bar{\pi}(f)\tau^{A}f-\frac{i}{2}g\bar{f}\tau^{A}\pi(\bar{f}),

(2)

\displaystyle\Phi_{2(2)}^{A}={}

\displaystyle D^{i}\pi(V)_{i}^{A}+\frac{i}{2}g_{H}\Hdagg\tau^{A}\pi(\Hdagg)-\frac{i}{2}g_{H}\pi(H)\tau^{A}H

\displaystyle+V^{0A}|H|^{2}\left(g_{VH}-\frac{g_{H}^{2}}{2}\right)+g_{f}J_{0}^{fA}+m_{V}^{2}V^{0A}.

(3)

Using the linearity of the PB we may write

\displaystyle\{\Phi_{1(2)}^{A},H_{T}\}={}

\displaystyle\{D^{i}\pi(W)_{i}^{A},H_{T}\}+gf^{DAC}\{\pi(V)^{D}_{\mu}V^{\mu C},H_{T}\}

\displaystyle+\frac{i}{2}g\{\Hdagg\tau^{A}\pi(\Hdagg),H_{T}\}-\frac{i}{2}g\{\pi(H)\tau^{A}H,H_{T}\}

\displaystyle-\frac{i}{2}g\{\bar{\pi}(f)\tau^{A}f,H_{T}\}-\frac{i}{2}g\{\bar{f}\tau^{A}\pi(\bar{f}),H_{T}\}.

(4)

One may then compute the different contributions independently using Eq. (
14
) and the definition as given in Eq. (
12
). It should be noted that the PBs have to be interpreted as functionals and the equalities below hold when acting upon appropriate test functions. Consider the first PB of Eq. (
4
), which can be written as

\displaystyle\{D^{i}\pi(W)^{A}_{i}(x),H_{T}\}={}

\displaystyle\{\partial^{i}\pi(W)^{A}_{i}(x),H_{T}\}+gf^{ABC}W^{iB}(x)\{\pi(W)_{i}^{C}(x),H_{T}\}

\displaystyle+gf^{ABC}\pi(W)_{i}^{C}(x)\{W^{iB}(x),H_{T}\},

(5)

where we have written out the dependence of the field on the spacetime point explicitly. The first term in the first line is given by

\displaystyle\{\partial^{i}\pi(W)^{A}_{i}(x),H_{T}\}=-\int\rd^{3}y\int\rd^{3}z\left(\partial^{i}_{x}\delta_{xy}\right)\frac{\delta_{t}\Ham(z)}{\delta W^{iA}(y)},

(6)

which acting on a test-function
\varphi^{A}(x)
yields

\displaystyle-\int\rd^{3}x\int\rd^{3}y\int\rd^{3}z\,\varphi^{A}(x)\left(\partial^{i}_{x}\delta_{xy}\right)\frac{\delta_{t}\Ham(z)}{\delta W^{iA}(y)}=-\int\rd^{3}y\int\rd^{3}z\,\varphi^{A}(y)\partial^{i}_{y}\frac{\delta_{t}\Ham(z)}{\delta W^{iA}(y)}.

(7)

To arrive at Eq. (
7
) we first used the delta function,
\delta_{xy}\equiv\delta(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y})
, to perform the
x
-integral and then integrated by parts in the
y
-integral. It follows from this that

\displaystyle\{\partial^{i}\pi(W)^{A}_{i}(x),H_{T}\}=-\int\rd^{3}z\,\partial^{i}_{x}\frac{\delta_{t}\Ham(z)}{\delta W^{iA}(x)},

(8)

and in a similar fashion one can show that

\displaystyle gf^{ABC}W^{iB}(x)\{\pi(W)_{i}^{C}(x),H_{T}\}=-gf^{ABC}\int\rd^{3}z\,W^{iB}(x)\frac{\delta_{t}\Ham(z)}{\delta W^{iA}(x)},

(9)

so that the first line of Eq. (
5
) is given by

\displaystyle\{\partial^{i}\pi(W)^{A}_{i}(x),H_{T}\}+gf^{ABC}W^{iB}(x)\{\pi(W)_{i}^{C}(x),H_{T}\}=-\int\rd^{3}z\,D^{i}_{x}\frac{\delta_{t}\Ham(z)}{\delta W^{iA}(x)}.

(10)

The relevant functional derivative of the Hamiltonian density can be computed from Eq. (
30
) and reads

\displaystyle-\frac{\delta_{t}\Ham(z)}{\delta W^{iA}(x)}={}

\displaystyle\Big(-gf^{DAF}\pi(W)_{i}^{D}(z)W^{0F}(z)-gf^{DAF}\pi(V)_{i}^{D}(z)V^{0F}(z)

\displaystyle+\frac{g_{H}g}{4}V_{i}^{B}(z)\Hdagg(z)[\tau^{B},\tau^{A}]_{+}H(z)+\frac{g}{2}\bar{f}(z)\tau^{A}\gamma_{i}f(z)

\displaystyle+\frac{ig}{2}\Hdagg(z)\tau^{A}\overleftrightarrow{D}_{i}H(z)+gf^{ABC}V^{B}_{ij}(z)V^{jC}(z)+gg_{M}f^{ABC}W_{ij}^{B}(z)V^{jC}(z)

\displaystyle+W^{B}_{ik}(z)D^{kBA}_{z}+g_{M}V^{B}_{ik}(z)D^{kBA}_{z}\Big)\delta_{zx},

(11)

where

\displaystyle D^{kBA}_{z}=\partial_{z}^{k}\delta^{AB}+gf^{BCA}W^{kC}(z).

(12)

For the first three lines of Eq. (
11
) the delta function ensures that we obtain a total covariant derivative of each individual term upon insertion into Eq. (
10
). For the last line of Eq. (
11
) the delta function allows us to move the covariant derivative to the field strength tensors at the cost of a minus sign. We may therefore write Eq. (
10
) as

\displaystyle\{\partial^{i}\pi(W)^{A}_{i}(x),H_{T}\}+gf^{ABC}W^{iB}(x)\{\pi(W)_{i}^{C}(x),H_{T}\}=D^{i}\mathcal{R}_{i}^{A}-\frac{gg_{M}}{2}f^{ABC}W^{ikB}V_{ik}^{C},

(13)

where the r.h.s. is evaluated at the spacetime point
x
. We here defined

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{i}^{A}={}

\displaystyle-gf^{DAF}\pi(W)_{i}^{D}W^{0F}-gf^{DAF}\pi(V)_{i}^{D}V^{0F}

\displaystyle+\frac{g_{H}g}{4}V_{i}^{B}\Hdagg[\tau^{B},\tau^{A}]_{+}H+\frac{g}{2}\bar{f}\tau^{A}\gamma_{i}f

\displaystyle+\frac{ig}{2}\Hdagg\tau^{A}\overleftrightarrow{D}_{i}H+gg_{M}f^{ABC}W_{ij}^{B}V^{jC}+gf^{ABC}V^{B}_{ij}V^{jC},

(14)

and used that

\displaystyle-D^{i}D^{k}V_{ik}^{B}=-\frac{g}{2}f^{BCD}W^{ikC}V_{ikB}.

(15)

A similar term arises from
-D^{i}D^{k}W_{ik}^{B}
and vanishes due the antisymmetry of the structure constants. To Eq. (
13
) we have to add

\displaystyle gf^{ABC}\pi(W)_{i}^{C}\{W^{iB},H_{T}\}={}

\displaystyle-\frac{g_{M}}{g_{M}^{2}-1}gf^{ABC}\pi(W)_{i}^{C}\pi(V)^{iB}

\displaystyle+gf^{ABC}\pi(W)_{i}^{C}(x)D^{i}W^{0B},

(16)

to obtain a full expression for the first term contributing to Eq. (
4
). It should be noted that combining the first term of
D^{i}\mathcal{R}^{A}_{i}
with the last line of Eq. (
16
) one obtains

\displaystyle-gf^{ABC}W^{0B}D^{i}\pi(W)^{C}_{i}\subset-gf^{ABC}W^{0B}\Phi_{1(2)}^{C}.

(17)

Having treated the first term contributing to Eq. (
4
) in detail we simply state the results for the remaining ones. We find

\displaystyle gf^{DAC}\{\pi(V)^{D}_{\mu}V^{\mu C},H_{T}\}\approx{}

\displaystyle gf^{DAC}\Big(\frac{-g_{M}}{g_{M}^{2}-1}\pi(V)_{i}^{D}\pi(W)^{iC}+\pi(V)_{i}^{D}D^{i}V^{0C}

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~-gf^{CFE}\pi(V)_{i}^{D}W^{0F}V^{iE}+gf^{EBD}\pi(V)_{i}^{E}V^{iC}W^{0B}

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~+g_{H}V^{iC}J^{HD}_{i}+g_{f}V^{iC}J^{fD}_{i}

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~+V^{iC}D^{jDB}V_{ji}^{B}+g_{M}V^{iC}D^{jDB}W_{ji}^{B}\Big),

(18)

where the first term in Eq. (
18
) cancels the first term in Eq. (
16
) and the second term combines with the second term of
D^{i}\mathcal{R}^{A}_{i}
to yield

\displaystyle-gf^{ABC}V^{0B}D^{i}\pi(V)^{C}_{i}\subset-gf^{ABC}V^{0B}\Phi_{2(2)}^{C}.

(19)

Furthermore, the last term in the last line in the definition of
\mathcal{R}^{A}_{i}
, when acted on with a covariant derivative, combines with the first term in the last line of Eq. (
18
) to yield a contribution that is proportional to
f^{ABC}V_{ij}^{B}V^{ijC}=0
. Similarly, the second term in the last line of Eq. (
18
) combines with the second to last term of
D^{i}\mathcal{R}_{i}^{A}
to yield
\frac{gg_{M}}{2}f^{ABC}V^{jiC}W_{ji}^{B}
, which then cancels the second term on the r.h.s. of Eq. (
13
). Using the Jacobi identity, the second line of Eq. (
18
) can be brought into the form

\displaystyle-gf^{AFC}W^{0F}\left(gf^{DCE}\pi(V)^{D}_{i}V^{iE}\right)\subset-gf^{AFC}W^{0F}\Phi^{C}_{1(2)}.

(20)

This eliminates all contributions to Eqs. (
13
) and (
18
) that do not depend on at least one fermionic or one scalar field. The second line of Eq. (
4
) is given by

\displaystyle\frac{i}{2}g\{\Hdagg\tau^{A}\pi(\Hdagg),H_{T}\}-\frac{i}{2}g\{\pi(H)\tau^{A}H,H_{T}\}\approx{}

\displaystyle-\frac{ig}{2}f^{ABC}\Hdagg\tau^{C}\pi(\Hdagg)(gW_{0}^{B}+g_{H}V_{0}^{B})

\displaystyle+\frac{ig}{2}f^{ABC}\pi(H)\tau^{C}H(gW_{0}^{B}+g_{H}V_{0}^{B})

\displaystyle-\frac{gg_{H}}{4}\Hdagg\tau^{A}\tau^{B}\left(2V^{iB}D_{i}H+HD_{i}V^{iB}\right)

\displaystyle-\frac{gg_{H}}{4}\left(2V^{iB}D_{i}\Hdagg+\Hdagg D_{i}V^{iB}\right)\tau^{B}\tau^{A}H

\displaystyle-\frac{ig}{2}\Hdagg\tau^{A}D_{i}D^{i}H+\frac{ig}{2}\left(D_{i}D^{i}\Hdagg\right)\tau^{A}H

\displaystyle+\frac{ig}{2}\Hdagg\tau^{A}\frac{\partial\Lag{rest}}{\partial\Hdagg}-\frac{ig}{2}\frac{\partial\Lag{rest}}{\partial H}\tau^{A}H,

(21)

the first two lines of which are recognised as

\displaystyle-gf^{ABC}W_{0}^{B}\left(\frac{ig}{2}\Hdagg\tau^{C}\pi(\Hdagg)-\frac{ig}{2}\pi(H)\tau^{C}H\right)\subset-gf^{ABC}W_{0}^{B}\Phi^{C}_{1(2)},

(22)

and

\displaystyle-gf^{ABC}V_{0}^{B}\left(\frac{ig_{H}}{2}\Hdagg\tau^{C}\pi(\Hdagg)-\frac{ig_{H}}{2}\pi(H)\tau^{C}H\right)\subset-gf^{ABC}V_{0}^{B}\Phi^{C}_{2(2)},

(23)

completing the first line of the constraints of Eqs. (
11
) and (
11
). Noting that

\displaystyle D^{i}\left(\frac{ig}{2}\Hdagg\tau^{A}\overleftrightarrow{D}_{i}H\right)=\frac{ig}{2}\left(-\left(D_{i}D^{i}\Hdagg\right)\tau^{A}H+\Hdagg\tau^{A}D^{i}D_{i}H\right),

(24)

we find that this contribution to
D^{i}\mathcal{R}_{i}^{A}
cancels the second to last line of Eq. (
21
). In order to treat the third line of Eq. (
21
) we act on a test function
\varphi^{A}
and integrate one of the two terms where the covariant derivative acts on the scalar doublet by parts

\displaystyle-\frac{gg_{H}}{4}\int\rd^{3}x\,\Hdagg\tau^{A}\tau^{B}\left(2V^{iB}D_{i}H+HD_{i}V^{iB}\right)\varphi^{A}

\displaystyle=\frac{gg_{H}}{4}\int\rd^{3}x\,V^{iB}\left[\left(D_{i}\Hdagg\right)\tau^{A}\tau^{B}H\varphi^{A}+\Hdagg\tau^{A}\tau^{B}HD_{i}\varphi^{A}-\Hdagg\tau^{A}\tau^{B}\left(D_{i}H\right)\varphi^{A}\right].

(25)

Performing the corresponding operation on the fourth line of Eq. (
21
) and combining the two results we find

\displaystyle-\frac{gg_{H}}{4}\!\int\rd^{3}x\!\left[\Hdagg\tau^{A}\tau^{B}\left(2V^{iB}D_{i}H+HD_{i}V^{iB}\right)+\left(2V^{iB}D_{i}\Hdagg+\Hdagg D_{i}V^{iB}\right)\tau^{B}\tau^{A}H\right]\varphi^{A}

\displaystyle=-\frac{gg_{H}}{4}\int\rd^{3}x\left[V^{iB}\Hdagg[\tau^{A},\tau^{B}]\left(D_{i}H\right)-V^{iB}\left(D_{i}\Hdagg\right)[\tau^{A},\tau^{B}]H\right]\varphi^{A}

\displaystyle~~~~-\frac{gg_{H}}{4}\int\rd^{3}x\,D_{i}\left(V^{iB}\Hdagg[\tau^{A},\tau^{B}]_{+}H\right)\varphi^{A},

(26)

where
[\tau^{A},\tau^{B}]
is the commutator of
\tau^{A}
and
\tau^{B}
. Removing the test function we note that the last line of Eq. (
26
) cancels with the first term of the second line of
D^{i}\mathcal{R}_{i}^{A}
. The first line of Eq. (
26
) yields

\displaystyle-\frac{gg_{H}}{4}\left[V^{iB}\Hdagg[\tau^{A},\tau^{B}]\left(D_{i}H\right)-V^{iB}\left(D_{i}\Hdagg\right)[\tau^{A},\tau^{B}]H\right]=-gg_{H}f^{CAB}V^{iB}J^{HC}_{i},

(27)

which cancels the first term of the third line of Eq. (
18
). This only leaves the last line of Eq. (
21
), which we will comment on shortly. First, we address the last line of Eq. (
4
), which is given by

\displaystyle-\frac{i}{2}g\{\bar{\pi}(f)\tau^{A}f,H_{T}\}-\frac{i}{2}g\{\bar{f}\tau^{A}\pi(\bar{f}),H_{T}\}\approx{}

\displaystyle-gg_{F}f^{ABC}V^{\mu B}J_{\mu}^{fC}-g^{2}f^{ABC}W^{\mu B}J_{\mu}^{fC}

\displaystyle-ig\frac{\partial_{r}\Lag{rest}}{\partial f}\frac{\tau^{A}}{2}f-ig\frac{\partial_{r}\Lag{rest}}{\partial\bar{f}}\frac{\tau^{A}}{2}\bar{f}

\displaystyle-\frac{g}{2}\partial_{i}\left(\bar{f}\tau^{A}\gamma^{i}f\right).

(28)

The spatial part of the first term of Eq. (
28
) cancels the last remaining term of Eq. (
18
) and the time component yields

\displaystyle-gg_{F}f^{ABC}V^{0B}J_{0}^{fC}\subset-gf^{ABC}V^{0B}\Phi^{C}_{2(2)}.

(29)

The time component of the second term of Eq. (
28
) is given by

\displaystyle-g^{2}f^{ABC}W^{0B}\bar{f}\gamma^{0}\frac{\tau^{A}}{2}f\approx\frac{i}{2}g^{2}f^{ABC}W^{0B}\left(\bar{\pi}(f)\tau^{A}f+\bar{f}\tau^{A}\pi(\bar{f})\right)\subset-gf^{ABC}W^{0B}\Phi^{C}_{1(2)},

(30)

where we used the fermionic constraints. The spatial part of this term combines with the last line of Eq. (
28
) to yield

\displaystyle-\frac{g}{2}D_{i}\left(\bar{f}\tau^{A}\gamma^{i}f\right),

(31)

which cancels the last remaining term of
D^{i}\mathcal{R}_{i}^{A}
. This only leaves the terms

\displaystyle\frac{ig}{2}\Hdagg\tau^{A}\frac{\partial\Lag{rest}}{\partial\Hdagg}-\frac{ig}{2}\frac{\partial\Lag{rest}}{\partial H}\tau^{A}H-ig\frac{\partial_{r}\Lag{rest}}{\partial f}\frac{\tau^{A}}{2}f-ig\frac{\partial_{r}\Lag{rest}}{\partial\bar{f}}\frac{\tau^{A}}{2}\bar{f},

(32)

which vanish as long as
\Lag{rest}
is gauge invariant. For a proof see Ref.
[
116
]
. Collecting Eqs. (
17
), (
20
), (
22
) and (
30
) we find precisely
-gf^{ABC}W^{0B}\Phi_{1(2)}^{C}
. Similarly, collecting Eqs. (
19
), (
23
) and (
29
) one finds
-gf^{ABC}V^{0B}\Phi_{2(2)}^{C}
since
f^{ABC}V^{0B}V^{0C}=0
.

I also thank Prof. Robert Harlander for agreeing to be my second supervisor.
For encouraging me to work on this project and for countless hours of useful discussions, and useful comments on earlier versions of this thesis, I would like to thank Dr. Alexander Voigt. I also thank Jonas Klappert and Fabian Lange for useful discussions, and Jonas Klappert in particular for comments on earlier versions of this thesis. Furthermore, I thank Jonas Klappert and Fabian Lange for making my time in the office a very pleasant experience.
A special thank you goes to everybody who supported me with encouragement throughout the completion of this work. This in particular includes my parents Roland and Sabine, without whom I would not be the person I am today, as well as my sister Alice. Furthermore I thank Hesam and Moncef, not only for encouragement, support and friendship throughout the completion of this thesis, but also the five years of studies that led up to it. I thank Jonas for his friendship and support and for the many rounds of Swedish basketball that we played together.
Last but not least I thank Physics itself for being such a rich subject that never seizes to surprise me. Thank you for the smile created by the feeling that a new layer of reality has revealed itself.
